# PegaSUS Mathematical / Theoretical Critique Ledger

Research phase (2026-07-07): a deep critique of the MSD theory AND the implementation, favoring **direct code+math inspection** over tests, aimed at (1) architectural/intent completeness + *enhancement over* the MSDs (which are treated as fallible theory), (2) modularization, (3) optimization/GPU. **Full granularity is preserved — no finding is compacted away.** Findings from the 12-agent `math-theory-critique` workflow (`wf_99a2944f-2d7`) are appended verbatim below the lead's own direct-inspection findings.

Severity: CRITICAL (invalidates a scientific claim) · HIGH (materially biases results) · MEDIUM · LOW.

---

## Part A — Lead's own direct-inspection findings (LDO core)

These came from reading the actual math in `margins.py`, `covariance.py`, `lowrank.py`, `lags.py`, `assemble.py` — not from tests.

### A1 · `DIRECT-W-01` — CRITICAL — the reliability-weight tensor `W` never enters the precision estimator
- **kind:** implementation_gap / statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** `assemble_ldo_tensor` builds a per-cell reliability tensor `W` from the §2.3 provenance ladder (`observed`=1.0, `geo/time_invariant_broadcast`=0.5, `reconstructed`=0.5, `bounded`=0.4, …), the §3.12 Kish/Moran `n_eff`, and (as of O12) the Q-tensor `denom_fragility`/`provenance_risk`. The MSD premise is that fragile/reconstructed cells are DOWN-WEIGHTED in the dependency estimate. But the core estimator ignores `W` entirely: `covariance.pairwise_correlation` and `covariance.whitened_lagged_correlation` compute the (whitened) correlation from `Z` with **no weighting**; `lags.fit_lagged_links` and `precision.fit_contemporaneous_precision` never read `field.W`. Grep for `field.W`/`weights` in `covariance.py`/`lags.py`/`precision.py` = **zero hits**. `W` is only carried (margins/edges/resolution) and used in ONE peripheral place — the ≤24-edge `spatial_field` BYM readout. So every cell — a directly-observed SIM death count and a gravity-reconstructed intercensal population denominator — contributes EQUALLY to the precision.
- **evidence:** `covariance.py` (no W); `lags.py:108-137` passes `field.Z` only; `precision.py:118-119`; W consumers = `margins.py:142`, `edges.py:46`, `resolution.py:46/72`, `spatial_field.py:84`.
- **recommendation:** make the covariance a genuinely WEIGHTED estimator: `pairwise_correlation` and `whitened_lagged_correlation` must weight each cell's contribution by `W` (weighted means/cross-moments: `Σ w·x / Σ w`, weighted `n_eff` for the Fisher-z SE). This also finally makes the O12 state→W wiring functional. Without it, the entire §3.12 uncertainty architecture is decorative and the precision is biased toward reconstructed structure.
- **risk_if_ignored:** the LDO discovers dependence driven by the population-reconstruction prior (a shared latent driver across all rate variables via the common denominator), reported as epidemiological structure. This is a first-order validity failure.

### A2 · `DIRECT-MARG-01` — CRITICAL — count-exposure margin uses a single POOLED rate λ for all cells & all years
- **kind:** msd_theoretical_fragility / statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** `count_exposure_gaussianize` sets `λ = Σx / Σexposure` — ONE scalar over the whole variable (all municipalities, all 2000–2024 years) — then `μ_i = λ·E_i` and PITs each count under `Poisson(μ_i)`. So the latent `Z_i` encodes deviation from the **national-average rate**, not from a local/temporal expectation. A high-mortality municipality has systematically high `Z` in every year (a pure spatial artefact); if national rates trend over 25 years, the whole panel's `Z` trends. The margin therefore INJECTS spatial-mean and temporal-trend structure that the LDO subsequently "discovers" as dependence. The docstring claims dependence is modelled "net of exposure" — true for exposure, but NOT net of the rate's spatial/temporal variation, which is the dominant structure.
- **evidence:** `margins.py:111` `lam = x.sum() / total_e`; `margins.py:112` `mu = lam * e`.
- **recommendation:** use a spatially/temporally-varying `μ` — fit a baseline offset GLM (log μ = log E + s(space) + f(time)) or at least an empirical-Bayes shrunk per-(space) and per-(time) rate — so `Z` is deviation from the LOCAL expected count. Equivalent to modelling the count with a proper mean structure before extracting residual dependence.
- **risk_if_ignored:** spurious spatial and temporal "edges" that are just the marginal rate surface; the spatial GMRF whitening cannot fully remove this because the copula transform is nonlinear.

### A3 · `DIRECT-MARG-02` — HIGH — Poisson margin ignores overdispersion → invalid PIT → non-Gaussian latent
- **kind:** statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** Municipal disease/mortality counts are almost always overdispersed (Var ≫ Mean) from unobserved heterogeneity and clustering. Under overdispersion the Poisson PIT `u = F(x-1;μ)+U·p(x;μ)` is NOT Uniform(0,1) (it is over-dispersed → u concentrates near 0 and 1), so `Z = Φ⁻¹(u)` is NOT standard Gaussian. The Gaussian-copula precision then estimates a correlation on a non-Gaussian latent — biased, and the Fisher-z SE is wrong.
- **evidence:** `margins.py:113` Poisson CDF/PMF; no dispersion parameter anywhere.
- **recommendation:** negative-binomial (or quasi-Poisson) margin with a per-variable dispersion estimate; or a moment-based dispersion correction. Test PIT uniformity (a KS/χ² diagnostic on `u`) and warn when it fails.
- **risk_if_ignored:** systematically miscalibrated edge uncertainties and biased partial correlations for the extensive-count variables (i.e. the disease outcomes themselves).

### A4 · `DIRECT-MARG-03` — HIGH — rank-PIT over the joint (S×T) pool bakes the marginal rate surface into `Z`; nonlinear-vs-linear tension with whitening
- **kind:** statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** `randomized_pit_gaussianize` ranks each variable across ALL cells jointly (space×time flattened). A variable with a strong spatial gradient becomes monotone-in-gradient in `Z` → strong spatial autocorrelation in `Z`. The downstream GMRF whitening is a LINEAR operator; it cannot undo the NONLINEAR rank transform's induced autocorrelation. So marginal transform and spatial de-correlation fight each other. Same pooled-reference problem as A2 but for the rank margin.
- **evidence:** `margins.py:42-63`, `gaussianize_field` flattens `field.X[j].reshape(-1)`.
- **recommendation:** rank within a stratification that removes the nuisance surface (e.g. rank within-time, or residualize a spatial spline before ranking), consistent with whichever de-correlation the whitening performs.
- **risk_if_ignored:** residual spatial autocorrelation in `Z` inflates apparent dependence between any two spatially-structured variables (Clifford-Richardson effective-df problem).

### A5 · `DIRECT-MARG-04` — HIGH — extreme zero-inflation makes the randomized-PIT latent mostly noise for rare diseases
- **kind:** epidemiological_risk / statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** For a rare cause (e.g. C25 pancreatic deaths) most of Brazil's 5,570 municipalities have 0 deaths/year. The randomized PIT spreads that huge tie mass uniformly over `[0, F(0)]`, so all zero-cells get i.i.d. uniform-noise `Z`. The LDO then estimates dependence largely from noise for those cells, diluting real signal and potentially manufacturing weak spurious edges. This is intrinsic to the discrete copula under mass-at-zero.
- **evidence:** `margins.py:57-62` ties handled by `less/leq/eq` uniform spread.
- **recommendation:** a hurdle/zero-inflated margin (model P(zero) separately), OR aggregate rare outcomes to a spatial grain where counts are non-degenerate (the multiresolution machinery should choose the grain by count adequacy, not just memory).
- **risk_if_ignored:** the flagship rare-cancer study estimates municipal dependence from tie-breaking noise.

### A6 · `DIRECT-WHIT-01` — HIGH — GMRF whitening imputes missing cells to the mean (0), attenuating spatial correlation for sparse panels
- **kind:** statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** `whitened_lagged_correlation` sets `Zc = where(finite, Z, 0.0)` before the sparse `Q`-matvecs. Imputing missing cells to 0 (the standardized mean) and treating them as observed injects a mass of "average" cells the whitening smooths across → attenuation of the estimated spatial correlation toward 0, and a biased whitened cross-moment `Gᵀ Q G`. The docstring concedes "a dense op cannot honour per-cell missingness," but for sparse national panels this is a material bias, not a footnote.
- **evidence:** `covariance.py:167` `Zc = np.where(np.isfinite(Z), Z, 0.0)`.
- **recommendation:** either restrict the whitened Gram to observed cells per pair (pairwise-complete whitening), or carry a per-cell mask into the `Q`-quadratic and normalize by observed mass — this is where `W` (A1) should also enter.
- **risk_if_ignored:** spatial edges attenuated where data is sparse (exactly the small/rural municipalities), a coverage-correlated bias.

### A7 · `DIRECT-WHIT-02` — MEDIUM — `_nearest_correlation` is a single eigen-clip, not a true nearest-correlation projection
- **kind:** computational_trap
- **detail:** `_nearest_correlation` clips eigenvalues at `floor=1e-3` then divides by `√diag`. That is one step, not Higham's alternating projection onto the intersection of the PSD cone and the unit-diagonal set; after the renormalization the matrix can again have eigenvalues < floor or |off-diag| slightly > 1, and it is not the Frobenius-nearest correlation matrix. It biases the input to CPW.
- **evidence:** `covariance.py:29-36`.
- **recommendation:** Higham's `nearcorr` (a few alternating projections) or `scipy`/`statsmodels` `corr_nearest`; cheap at `p·(K+1)` scale.
- **risk_if_ignored:** a subtly non-PSD or mis-scaled correlation fed to the ADMM, degrading the S/L split.

### A8 · `DIRECT-CPW-01` — HIGH — λ1, λ2 are fixed magic numbers; the entire edge set depends on an unselected penalty
- **kind:** msd_theoretical_fragility / statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** CPW/LVGLASSO edge recovery depends critically on `(λ1, λ2)`; the CPW theory ties the admissible range to sample size and the incoherence parameter. The code fixes `λ1=0.1`, `λ2=1.0` (production) with NO data-driven selection — no CV, no theoretical `√(log p / n)` scaling, no stability-path selection of λ itself (the O4 path-agreement conjunct only checks robustness at a fixed operating point, it does not choose λ). The number of discovered edges is therefore an artefact of an arbitrary threshold.
- **evidence:** `orchestrator.py` defaults; `lowrank.py:fit_sparse_plus_lowrank(lambda1=0.1, lambda2=0.1)`; investigate `lambda2=1.0`.
- **recommendation:** select `λ1` by a stability-selection criterion (Meinshausen-Bühlmann, control per-family error at a target) or by an eBIC/CV over a path; scale with `√(log p / n_eff)`. Report the selected λ + the selection's error guarantee.
- **risk_if_ignored:** "we found K edges" is not reproducible or defensible; a reviewer changes λ and the finding set changes.

### A9 · `DIRECT-CPW-02` — MEDIUM/HIGH — fixed ADMM ρ, no adaptive penalty / residual balancing → convergence risk misread as "descriptive"
- **kind:** computational_trap
- **detail:** The CPW ADMM uses a fixed `ρ=1.0` with no Boyd-style primal/dual residual balancing. For an ill-conditioned `C` the split can converge very slowly or stall; the `max_iter=500, tol=1e-5` gate then yields `converged=False`, which the certifier downgrades to "descriptive." But an unconverged S/L split is not merely low-confidence — it can be QUALITATIVELY wrong (mass that belongs in `L` sits in `S` as spurious dense edges, or vice versa). Downgrading to descriptive ships a wrong graph, not a cautious one.
- **evidence:** `lowrank.py:fit_sparse_plus_lowrank(rho=1.0, max_iter=500, tol=1e-5)`; no ρ update in the loop.
- **recommendation:** adaptive ρ (residual-balancing), over-relaxation, and a convergence certificate (primal+dual residual, duality gap) rather than an iteration cap; refuse (not "descriptive") if the gap is large.
- **risk_if_ignored:** on the hardest (most interesting) national fits, a wrong graph is emitted with a "descriptive" label that reads as merely uncertain.

### A10 · `DIRECT-CPW-03` — MEDIUM — the incoherence gate (`min_factor_support=3`) is a crude proxy for CPW identifiability
- **kind:** msd_theoretical_fragility
- **detail:** CPW identifiability rests on an incoherence parameter μ(L) (the low-rank part must be "spread"/incoherent) and a transversality/degrees-of-freedom condition. The code approximates this with `min_factor_support=3` (a factor supported on <3 strong loadings is reclassified as a direct edge). A genuine 2-variable confounder (a shared driver of exactly two variables) is then misassigned to `S` as a direct edge — the exact ambiguity CPW is supposed to resolve. The number 3 has no theoretical grounding.
- **evidence:** `lowrank.py:110/135/226` `min_factor_support`.
- **recommendation:** compute the actual incoherence (max leverage of `L`'s eigenvectors) and gate on it; or report both hypotheses (direct vs confounded) with the identifiability caveat when support is small.
- **risk_if_ignored:** two-variable confounding is systematically reported as a direct causal-candidate edge.

### A11 · `DIRECT-LAG-01` — HIGH — distributed-lag "directed" edges are Granger-predictive, not causal, and assume 25-year stationarity
- **kind:** epidemiological_risk / msd_theoretical_fragility
- **detail:** The lag-extended precision reads a nonzero `X_i(t−k) → X_j(t)` block as a "directed lag-k link." This is Granger-style temporal precedence, which is confounded by any common trend or a slow third driver; the MSD's framing risks over-reading it as mechanism. Worse, the single lag structure is fit over all of 2000–2024 — a period of major epidemiological transition (mortality decline, coverage expansion) — implicitly assuming a stationary lag response. At annual aggregation, lag-0 vs lag-1 is also barely separable.
- **evidence:** `lags.py:154-179` directed peak-lag readout; no stationarity test/segmentation.
- **recommendation:** state the Granger (not causal) semantics explicitly on lagged edges (the causal ladder already helps — enforce it); test stationarity / allow a regime split; consider sub-annual grain where lag identification is meaningful.
- **risk_if_ignored:** temporal-precedence associations over-interpreted as lagged mechanisms across a non-stationary period.

### A12 · `DIRECT-TEMP-01` — MEDIUM — temporal-smoothness prior can manufacture a smooth response curve that isn't there
- **kind:** statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** The §III.4(5) temporal quadratic `(γ/2)tr(SᵀL_lag S)` shrinks adjacent-lag precision blocks toward each other. With `γ=0.1` default this biases the discovered distributed-lag response_curve toward smoothness. If the true response is a sharp single-lag spike, the prior smears it across neighbours (a spurious-smoothing trap); the reported "response curve" then reflects the prior, not the data.
- **evidence:** `lowrank.py:build_smoothness_operator`, `orchestrator.py gamma_temporal=0.1`.
- **recommendation:** make `γ` data-driven (marginal-likelihood/CV) rather than a fixed default; report the curve's prior-sensitivity.
- **risk_if_ignored:** response-curve shapes are partly artefacts of an unjustified smoothing weight.

### A13 · `DIRECT-SE-01` — HIGH — Fisher-z edge SE uses the RAW cell count, not the Moran-corrected effective-n → over-certification
- **kind:** statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** Edge uncertainty is `stat_se = 1/√(n_eff−3)` with `n_eff = _n_eff(field) =` the raw count of observed cells (`isfinite.any(axis=0).sum()`). Under strong spatial (and temporal) autocorrelation the EFFECTIVE sample size is far smaller than the raw cell count (Clifford-Richardson / Dutilleul); using the raw n makes the SE too small → edge confidence intervals too narrow → the §III.8 conjunction certifies edges that a correct SE would leave descriptive. The Moran-corrected effective-n IS computed in the Q-tensor (`_moran_corrected_n_eff`), but — exactly like `W` (A1) — it never reaches the SE. Same architectural gap: the effective-df correction is computed and discarded.
- **evidence:** `edges.py:151` `_n_eff`, `edges.py:173` `stat_se`; `compile_attach.py:253` computes the corrected n_eff but the LDO reads the raw one.
- **recommendation:** feed the Moran/Kish-corrected effective-n (per the panel's actual autocorrelation) into the Fisher-z SE; this is the same fix vector as A1 (make the estimator consume the computed uncertainty state).
- **risk_if_ignored:** systematic over-certification of spatially-autocorrelated edges — anticonservative exactly where epi data is most dependent.

### A14 · `DIRECT-STAB-01` — HIGH — spatial stability-selection randomly subsamples municipalities, fragmenting the GMRF adjacency
- **kind:** statistical_validity_risk / msd_theoretical_fragility
- **detail:** `stability_select` draws a random 70% subset of municipalities; each refit calls `build_spatial_precision_sparse(subsampled space_ids)`, which keeps only adjacency edges BETWEEN surviving municipalities. Randomly deleting 30% of munis shatters the contiguity graph (every deleted muni removes its edges), so each subsample whitens with a much sparser, inconsistent GMRF than the true one → the whitening under-corrects differently every subsample → stability frequencies are measured under a degraded and varying spatial model. You cannot i.i.d.-subsample spatial units and preserve the spatial dependence structure.
- **evidence:** `edges.py:34-41` `space_ids=tuple(field.space_ids[i] for i in idx)`; `lags.py:113` rebuilds `Q_space` on the subset.
- **recommendation:** use spatial BLOCK subsampling (drop whole contiguous regions / UF blocks, keeping the within-block graph intact) or a spatial bootstrap that respects contiguity; or subsample TIME (already added in MIN-3) and keep space whole for the whitening.
- **risk_if_ignored:** the stability guarantee — the LDO's headline "edges survive subsampling" claim — is measured under a broken spatial model, so it neither controls false selection nor reflects the fitted whitening.

### A15 · `DIRECT-STAB-02` — MEDIUM — per-subsample GMRF rebuild + whitening is redundant national compute
- **kind:** computational_trap
- **detail:** Beyond A14's validity issue, rebuilding `build_spatial_precision_sparse` + the matrix-free whitened Gram on every one of `n_subsamples` refits re-walks the national adjacency and re-accumulates the `O(F·S)` Gram each time — a large repeated cost at national `S≈5570`. The full-graph whitening is (or could be) computed once; only the subsample selection changes.
- **evidence:** `edges.py:_subsample_edges → lags.fit_lagged_links → whitened_lagged_correlation` per subsample.
- **recommendation:** precompute the full-graph whitened features once; subsample in the whitened space (respecting A14's block structure). Candidate for GPU batching (many small refits) — see the GPU-rematch ledger.
- **risk_if_ignored:** stability selection dominates LDO wall-clock at national scale for no benefit.

### A16 · `DIRECT-POP-01` — CRITICAL — intercensal (age×sex×race) denominators are prior-dominated ("fabricated demography"), and that uncertainty is never propagated
- **kind:** identifiability_concern / epidemiological_risk
- **detail:** The population tensor `P` over (S,T,A,X,R) is pinned by data only at census years (anchors: 2000/2010/2022) and by the per-(locality,time) closure total (a marginal). The full age×sex×race JOINT structure in every INTERCENSAL year is determined by the PRIORS — the cohort-aging recursion from the last census, migration/age second-difference smoothness, and the race-ILR prior — not by observation. So intercensal municipal age-sex-race denominators are substantially MODEL OUTPUT. The flagship C25 study age-standardizes on exactly these denominators, so its rates in ~22 of 25 years rest on a prior-dominated interpolation whose uncertainty is neither quantified nor propagated (compounding A1: even if it were quantified, `W` is ignored by the LDO).
- **evidence:** `loss.py` — anchors only where observed; closure by projection; aging/race/smoothness are priors; `orchestrator.py` two-layer denominator (closed-form interp + refine).
- **recommendation:** quantify the data-vs-prior contribution per intercensal cell (e.g. the posterior variance / effective-prior-weight), emit it as a per-cell denominator uncertainty, and propagate it into the rate margins and the LDO `W`. Consider a proper cohort-component demographic model with credible intervals rather than a penalized LS point estimate.
- **risk_if_ignored:** age-standardized municipal trends reported with false precision; the denominator's model structure leaks into "epidemiological" findings.

### A17 · `DIRECT-POP-02` — HIGH — reconstruction objective weights are hand-set magic numbers governing the data-vs-prior bias-variance tradeoff
- **kind:** msd_theoretical_fragility / statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** `PopulationObjectiveWeights` fixes anchor=10, aging=1, migration=0.1, migration_total=0, race=0, age_smooth=0.05. These are the relative influence of DATA (anchor=10) vs PRIORS (age_smooth=0.05) — a 200:1 hand-chosen ratio — with no calibration to the actual variances of each term. The entire reconstruction's bias-variance tradeoff, and hence the intercensal demography, is set by unjustified constants.
- **evidence:** `loss.py:33-46` `PopulationObjectiveWeights` defaults.
- **recommendation:** set weights as inverse noise variances (a proper GLS/hierarchical-Bayes weighting), or profile them by cross-validating held-out census years; report sensitivity.
- **risk_if_ignored:** reconstructed demography is an artefact of arbitrary regularization strength.

### A18 · `DIRECT-POP-03` — MEDIUM — cohort survival uses `1 − nan_to_num(death_rate, 0)` → missing-mortality cells are treated as immortal
- **kind:** computational_trap
- **detail:** The aging recursion uses `survival = 1 − nan_to_num(death_rates, nan=0.0)`. A cell with a MISSING death rate gets `survival = 1.0` (nobody dies), so its aged population is overestimated. Missingness is silently mapped to "no mortality," a directional bias in the cohort projection precisely where mortality data is absent (small/rural municipalities).
- **evidence:** `loss.py:143-144`.
- **recommendation:** impute a regional/age-typical survival for missing cells (or widen its uncertainty), not survival=1.
- **risk_if_ignored:** population over-projected in data-poor cells, deflating their apparent rates.

### A19 · `DIRECT-POP-04` — MEDIUM — the migration tensor is an unidentified smoothness-prior residual reported as a specific flow field
- **kind:** identifiability_concern
- **detail:** Municipal age-sex-race net migration is unobserved; the solve produces a specific migration tensor `η` driven almost entirely by the 2nd-difference smoothness prior (w=0.1) plus the per-locality net-total anchor (w=0 by default). The result is a smooth but essentially fabricated flow field that then feeds the population aging recursion. The MSD notes migration unidentifiability, but the pipeline still emits and consumes a point migration field with no uncertainty flag.
- **evidence:** `loss.py:288-312`; `migration.py` gravity prior; `migration_total` weight default 0.
- **recommendation:** treat `η` as a nuisance with wide uncertainty (or marginalize it), and do not let its point estimate silently shape the denominator without an uncertainty band.
- **risk_if_ignored:** the denominator inherits a fabricated migration structure as if observed.

### A20 · `DIRECT-QT-01` — HIGH — the Moran's-I effective-n correction uses 1-D cell-ORDER contiguity, not geography
- **kind:** statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** `_moran_contiguity` computes Moran's I treating cells adjacent in the FLATTENED tensor ordering as spatial neighbours (`w_ij=1` iff `|i−j|=1`). But cell order is lexical cod6 (or panel order), which has NO relation to geographic contiguity — municipality 270430 and 270431 can be hundreds of km apart. So this Moran's I measures autocorrelation along an arbitrary 1-D chain, not space. It is honestly warning-tagged a "proxy," but it then DEFLATES the effective-n (`_moran_corrected_n_eff`), so the effective sample size — the very quantity A13 says should (but doesn't) drive the LDO edge SE — is corrected by a meaningless statistic. The REAL adjacency exists (`structural_cod6_adjacency`, used by the LDO GMRF) and is simply not passed here.
- **evidence:** `q_tensor.py:116-144` chain contiguity; `q_tensor.py:208-209` used as the deflation input.
- **recommendation:** compute Moran's I with the real municipality adjacency (share the `SpatialWeightGraph` the LDO already builds); this is also a modularization win (one spatial graph, many consumers).
- **risk_if_ignored:** the effective-df / design-effect correction is garbage; combined with A13 (it isn't even used), the whole spatial-autocorrelation accounting is non-functional.

### A21 · `DIRECT-QT-02` — MEDIUM/HIGH — Kish `(Σw)²/Σw²` misapplied to outcome counts, not survey weights
- **kind:** msd_theoretical_fragility / statistical_validity_risk
- **detail:** The Kish effective-n `(Σw)²/Σw²` is the effective size under unequal SAMPLING weights (a design-effect for weighted surveys). Here `weights = denom_values or values` — the DENOMINATOR or the outcome COUNTS themselves. Feeding event counts to the Kish formula conflates "concentration of the outcome" with "effective sample size for a rate," which are different. A rate estimated over 5,570 municipalities where one has 10⁶ population is not "effectively fewer observations" in the Kish survey sense. The correct effective-n for a spatially-clustered rate is `n / DEFF` with `DEFF = 1 + (m̄−1)·ρ` (intra-cluster correlation), not the Kish weight-concentration formula.
- **evidence:** `q_tensor.py:147-162`, `q_tensor.py:217`.
- **recommendation:** define effective-n via a design-effect from the estimated spatial ICC (Moran-based, on the real graph per A20); reserve Kish for genuinely weighted estimators. Reconcile with the MSD-I §3.12.3 definition.
- **risk_if_ignored:** the effective-n — hence every downstream power gate and (should-be) SE — has no clear statistical meaning.

### A22 · `DIRECT-QT-03` — HIGH — `compute_q_state`/`classify_q_state` are orphaned; declared field state never re-derived from n_eff
- **kind:** implementation_gap
- **detail:** The live compile path (`compile_attach._q_row`) computes n_eff via `_kish_effective_n`+`_moran_corrected_n_eff` directly and passes `field.state` through UNCHANGED — it never calls `compute_q_state`/`classify_q_state`. So the threshold logic (n_eff≥100→verified, ≥30→fragile, else quarantined_descriptive) NEVER runs: a count with n_eff≪100 can remain `verified`. The Q-tensor's own state machine is dead code, and the certification-relevant field state is unbacked by the computed diagnostics. (This is the T2.6 modularization item, confirmed at the math level.)
- **evidence:** `q_tensor.py:34-49`, `178-227` orphaned; `compile_attach.py:337` passes `field.state` unchanged.
- **recommendation:** route `_q_row` through `compute_q_state`, re-derive `field.state`, delete the duplicated `_vector_diagnostics`/`_moran_*` in compile_attach (one implementation).
- **risk_if_ignored:** field reliability labels (which gate dashboard-safety and downstream trust) are declared, not measured.

### A23 · `DIRECT-QT-04` — MEDIUM — q-state thresholds and provenance-risk are uncalibrated ordinal magic numbers with hard cliffs
- **kind:** msd_theoretical_fragility
- **detail:** `classify_q_state` cliffs at n_eff 100/30, denom_fragility 0.05/0.20, missingness 0.10, risk 0.5; `provenance_risk` assigns hand-picked 0.0–1.0 by tag. n_eff=99→fragile vs 100→verified is a discontinuity with no calibration. These drive trust/gating decisions as if principled.
- **evidence:** `q_tensor.py:45-49`, `17-31`.
- **recommendation:** replace hard cliffs with a continuous reliability score (or at least document the thresholds' empirical basis); treat provenance_risk as a documented prior, not a probability.
- **risk_if_ignored:** trust classification is brittle at the boundaries and not defensible.

## Cross-cutting theme (lead) — THE UNCERTAINTY PIPELINE IS DISCONNECTED FROM THE INFERENCE PIPELINE
A1 + A13 + A20 + A21 + A22 together are ONE systemic architectural failure, and the clearest evidence for the user's objective-2 concern (no clear module/data-contract boundaries): the EFG/§3.12 layer computes a rich per-field/per-cell uncertainty state (provenance weight `W`, Kish/Moran-corrected `n_eff`, `denom_fragility`, `provenance_risk`, the state classification) — and the LDO inference layer **consumes almost none of it**: the covariance is unweighted (A1), the edge SE uses the raw cell count (A13), the effective-n it would use is itself mis-computed (A20/A21), and the state classification never runs (A22). The two subsystems have no enforced data contract tying "uncertainty produced" to "uncertainty consumed." **This should become a first-class, tested interface** (an `ObservationReliability` contract that the estimator is REQUIRED to consume) — it is simultaneously a statistical-validity fix and a modularization redesign.

*(Lead's direct reading continues; the modularization + GPU thrusts follow in their own ledgers. Workflow findings append to Part B.)*

### LDO-margins / count-with-exposure

**`LDO-MARG-01` · CRITICAL · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Poisson-offset margin hardcodes equidispersion, contradicting MSD-I's own NB/ZINB mandate
- **detail:** count_exposure_gaussianize (margins.py:88-115) builds the margin CDF as F_i = Poisson(mu_i), mu_i = lambda*E_i with a single MLE rate lambda = sum(count)/sum(exposure). The Poisson assumes Var = E[count] (equidispersion). Brazilian municipal count data is massively OVERDISPERSED: unmodeled spatial heterogeneity, clustering of cases, and reporting variation make Var >> mean. Crucially MSD-I §6.2 (lines 6270-6272) itself prescribes 'negative binomial or quasi-Poisson' for count outcomes and 'hurdle or zero-inflated' for zero-inflated support, and MSD-III §III.5 lists 'Poisson/NB/binomial/beta-binomial' as the admissible family set. The implementation collapses all of these to Poisson. Consequence for the PIT: under an overdispersed truth, the Poisson CDF is mis-specified, so u_i = F(x-1;mu)+U*p(x;mu) is NOT Uniform(0,1) — it is over-dispersed relative to uniform (too much mass in the tails). ndtri(u) then produces a latent Z that is heavy-tailed and whose second moment is inflated in high-count municipalities, injecting spurious cross-cell correlation (all large municipalities look jointly 'surprising') that the precision solver reads as a latent_shared factor or dense edges. This is a scientific-validity failure: the margin's own distributional assumption is falsified by the data it is applied to, and the spec it cites disallows it.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §III.5 (line 222); MSD-I §6.2 (lines 6270-6272), §3.12.9 (line 5019); src/pegasus/ldo/margins.py:88-115 (lam, mu, poisson.cdf/pmf)
- **recommendation:** Replace the fixed Poisson with a Negative-Binomial (or quasi-Poisson) offset margin: estimate an overdispersion parameter (e.g. method-of-moments theta from Pearson residuals, or an MLE NB2 fit) and use nbinom.cdf/pmf for the randomized PIT. For zero-inflated / structural-zero variables route to a hurdle/ZINB margin (a Bernoulli 'at-risk' gate + a truncated count margin), matching MSD-I §6.2. Select the family from the carrier registry / support diagnostics rather than hardcoding, and record the chosen family + dispersion in the margin provenance so the readout is auditable.
- **risk:** Every extensive count variable's latent scale is mis-standardized; overdispersion is re-manufactured as a shared latent driver or dense spurious edges, biasing the entire precision graph and inflating false-positive determinant links.

**`LDO-MARG-02` · CRITICAL · epidemiological_risk** — Single global rate lambda pushes real spatial/temporal rate variation into the latent 'surprise' scale
- **detail:** The count-with-exposure margin sets mu_i = lambda*E_i with ONE national lambda = sum(x)/sum(e) (margins.py:111). The docstring's justification ('two cells at the same rate but different exposure map to the same latent value, not a spurious exposure signal') is only half the story: the margin residualizes each cell against the NATIONAL-AVERAGE rate. But the entire epidemiological object of interest — the between-municipality variation in disease rate (the epidemiological transition, the North/Northeast gradient, urban vs rural) — is EXACTLY the deviation of local rate from the national mean. By Gaussianizing against a flat lambda, a high-rate municipality gets a large positive Z on EVERY high-rate outcome simultaneously, and a low-rate one a large negative Z, so the latent scale is dominated by a single 'municipal development / access' axis. The precision solver then discovers this as a giant latent_shared factor (correct MSD-III behavior 'prevents everything-riding-a-wave' — but here the wave is an ARTEFACT of using a flat offset instead of a structured expectation). The margin is Layer 0 and cannot see the structured model, so this is architecturally baked in: the offset should be the structured E[count] (spatial GMRF + covariates), not a scalar. This is the classic small-area-estimation error of standardizing counts against a global rate instead of a shrinkage/BYM expected count.
- **evidence:** margins.py:111 (lam = x.sum()/total_e); MSD-III §III.5 (line 222), §III.3 BYM/INLA (lines 205-208)
- **recommendation:** Compute expected counts from a small-area model, not a scalar: at minimum a per-stratum (state or health-region) rate; better, an INLA/SPDE BYM expected-count field E_i = exp(offset + spatial_random_effect) so the PIT residualizes against the SHRUNK local expectation (Freeman-Tukey or Anscombe-style variance-stabilized standardized-morbidity residual). This makes Z carry genuine local anomaly rather than the national rate gradient. If Layer-0/Layer-1 separation must be preserved, iterate: fit precision, re-derive expected counts from the fitted structure, re-Gaussianize (an EM-style copula-margin refinement).
- **risk:** The dominant discovered structure is a mechanical consequence of flat standardization, masquerading as an epidemiological latent driver; genuine determinant edges are shrunk beneath this artefactual factor.

**`LDO-MARG-08` · HIGH · identifiability_concern** — Exposure denominator treated as a known constant despite being a reconstructed uncertain tensor
- **detail:** count_exposure_gaussianize (margins.py:88) takes exposure E_i as a hard, error-free offset (mu_i = lambda*E_i, the log-exposure offset is exact). But the exposure is the POPULATION/denominator tensor, which MSD-III §II.4 explicitly says is a RECONSTRUCTED quantity carrying per-cell uncertainty that widens with anchor distance (line 151: 'a rate computed on a projected denominator carries visibly wider bars than one on a census denominator'). The margin ignores this entirely: an intercensal / extrapolated denominator with (say) 5% uncertainty is used as if exact, so the standardized count residual z_i has an UNDERSTATED variance (the true variance is Var(count|E) PLUS lambda^2*Var(E), a denominator-uncertainty term). Worse, denominator errors are SPATIALLY CORRELATED (a mis-vintaged IBGE projection biases whole regions the same way — exactly the §II.4 anti-discontinuity concern), so ignoring Var(E) injects a spatially-structured bias into Z that the spatial GMRF whitening will read as genuine spatial dependence. The denominator principle is honored for the point offset but its uncertainty — the whole reason §II.4 builds an uncertainty-typed denominator asset — is dropped at the margin.
- **evidence:** margins.py:88-115 (exposure as exact offset); MSD-III §II.4 (line 147-151, denominator uncertainty as function of anchor distance)
- **recommendation:** Propagate denominator uncertainty into the margin: either (a) widen the count variance to Var = mu + lambda^2*sigma_E^2 (a compound Poisson-lognormal / observed-Fisher deflation of z), or (b) draw E from its FAL-POP-PROJ posterior in the multiple-randomization loop (LDO-MARG-03) so the between-draw variance captures denominator uncertainty. Feed the §II.4 per-cell cell_uncertainty into the reliability weight W so extrapolated-denominator cells are down-weighted (once W is actually used).
- **risk:** Rates on projected/extrapolated denominators are treated with false precision and their spatially-correlated denominator errors are misread as epidemiological spatial structure — the exact spurious-trend failure §II.4 was written to prevent, re-entering through the margin.

**`LDO-MARG-14` · LOW · statistical_validity_risk** — Rate lambda is a plug-in MLE from the same data, ignoring estimation error and providing no shrinkage for sparse strata
- **detail:** lambda = x.sum()/total_e (margins.py:111) is a single plug-in point estimate used as if it were the true rate when forming every cell's margin CDF. Two issues: (i) it ignores the uncertainty in lambda itself (a fully Bayesian / empirical-Bayes margin would integrate over the rate posterior, giving a negative-binomial predictive even before overdispersion is added — the Poisson-Gamma predictive is NB, which incidentally is the LDO-MARG-01 fix); (ii) pooling ALL variables/cells into one lambda is a maximal-pooling estimator with no small-area shrinkage, whereas MSD-I §3.12.8 sets mu_min=50 support floors and §III.3 mandates BYM shrinkage precisely for sparse strata. Using the global MLE means a single anomalous high-count region can shift lambda for everyone, and there is no borrowing of strength for the rate across the disease hierarchy (L_D) or space (L_W) even though those priors exist elsewhere in the LDO.
- **evidence:** margins.py:111; MSD-I §3.12.8 (line 5012 mu_min=50); MSD-III §III.3 (lines 205-208)
- **recommendation:** Use an empirical-Bayes / Gamma-prior rate (equivalently the NB predictive of LDO-MARG-01) and, better, a stratified or spatially-shrunk lambda_s (borrowing across L_W / L_D) so the offset expectation is a small-area estimate. Propagate the rate's posterior variance into the margin (ties into the multiple-draw scheme LDO-MARG-03/08).
- **risk:** The margin's expected counts are a maximally-pooled point estimate with no uncertainty and no small-area shrinkage, mildly biasing every standardized residual and contradicting the shrinkage philosophy the rest of the LDO adopts.


### LDO-margins / randomized PIT

**`LDO-MARG-03` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Randomized-PIT jitter drawn once and frozen; stability selection treats one stochastic realization as data
- **detail:** gaussianize_field is called ONCE in _prepare_ldo_inputs (orchestrator.py:71) with a single seed, producing one realization of the randomized quantile transform. stability_select (edges.py) then subsamples this FIXED gf.Z. The randomized PIT (Dunn-Smyth) is only valid *in distribution*: z = Phi^{-1}(F(x-1)+U*p(x)) is standard normal only when averaged over U~Uniform. For a single draw, each tied zero-cell gets one arbitrary jittered rank; two zero cells that are epidemiologically identical receive different Z values purely from the RNG. Because the same frozen Z feeds every stability subsample, the auxiliary randomization is NOT integrated out — it is a fixed nuisance that biases every refit identically, so stability selection cannot detect or average it away (it looks like signal to all subsamples). With rare-event counts the zero-mass fraction is often >90% of cells, so the majority of every variable's latent values are pure jitter frozen into the correlation structure. The statistically correct procedure is to draw MULTIPLE randomizations and pool (multiple-imputation-style: Rubin combination of the precision estimates, or at least averaging the ranks), or to use the mid-quantile/non-randomized deterministic PIT for the point estimate with the randomization only for a diagnostic band.
- **evidence:** orchestrator.py:71 (single gaussianize_field call); margins.py:60 (rng.uniform once); edges.py:41-48 (subsample slices fixed Z)
- **recommendation:** Draw R>=20 independent randomized-PIT realizations (or use mid-P / mid-quantile deterministic Gaussianization for the point estimate), run the precision fit on each, and combine via multiple-imputation rules (average partial correlations, inflate variance by the between-realization component). At minimum, expose the margin seed and run the whole LDO under several seeds to quantify how much of each edge is jitter-driven. Report the Monte-Carlo SE of every edge weight attributable to the auxiliary randomization.
- **risk:** Edge weights and their stabilities are partly a function of one arbitrary RNG draw over a majority-tied variable; results are not reproducible in the scientific sense (a different seed gives materially different low-count edges) and stability selection gives false confidence because it never varies the jitter.


### LDO-margins / rank PIT

**`LDO-MARG-04` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Empirical CDF computed on the full national sample then subsampled — information leak across stability folds
- **detail:** randomized_pit_gaussianize (margins.py:56-60) ranks each value against the FULL set of observed values (order = np.sort(obs) over all cells), u = (#{<x} + U*#{=x})/n with n the full-sample size. This Gaussianization happens BEFORE stability_select subsamples the lattice (edges.py). Therefore each subsample's Z values encode the empirical CDF of the ENTIRE national sample, not of that subsample — the rank of a cell depends on cells NOT in the fold. Stability selection's validity premise (MSD-III §III.7: 'refit on lattice subsamples, keep edges recurring above a frequency threshold') requires the subsamples to be quasi-independent resamples of the estimation pipeline; here the marginal transform is a global statistic shared across all folds, so the folds are positively correlated through the common CDF and the recurrence frequency overstates stability. It also means the transform is not a legitimate cross-validation / holdout: a temporal-holdout subsample (O5) still sees future years' ranks baked into its Z. This is a subtle but real leakage that inflates every edge's selection frequency.
- **evidence:** margins.py:56-62 (global order/searchsorted over full obs); MSD-III §III.7 (line 230), §IX.3 lattice subsampling; orchestrator gaussianize-once ordering
- **recommendation:** Gaussianize INSIDE each subsample (re-estimate the empirical/parametric CDF on the fold's cells only) so each refit is a self-contained pipeline, OR use a fixed parametric margin (NB-offset with parameters estimated only on the fold) rather than a global empirical rank. For temporal holdout specifically, the CDF must be estimated on the training window only. Document that the rank margin is a pipeline step subject to resampling, not a pre-computed feature.
- **risk:** Reported stability frequencies are anticonservative; edges that would not survive an honest fold-internal transform are certified, undermining the anti-spurious gate (§III.8).

**`LDO-MARG-11` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — Rank margin ignores the reliability/observation weights when forming the empirical CDF (weighted ECDF needed)
- **detail:** Separate from LDO-MARG-05 (estimator), the empirical CDF itself in randomized_pit_gaussianize is UNWEIGHTED: searchsorted over np.sort(obs) counts every observed cell equally when forming ranks (margins.py:56-60). But the observed cells span radically different reliabilities and effective sample sizes (a large-population municipality-year with n_eff=10^5 vs a tiny one with n_eff=20, plus broadcast/reconstructed cells at W<1). The correct marginal CDF for a heterogeneous panel is a WEIGHTED ECDF (Hajek estimator): F_hat(x) = sum_{obs<=x} w_i / sum w_i. Using the unweighted ECDF means thousands of tiny, noisy, or reconstructed municipalities dominate the rank scale (they are numerous), so the Gaussian scale is calibrated to the small-municipality distribution rather than the population-weighted one, shifting where the bulk of Z mass sits and distorting correlations toward small-area noise.
- **evidence:** margins.py:56-60 (unweighted searchsorted/sort); assemble.py W available but unused here
- **recommendation:** Form a weighted empirical CDF using the per-cell reliability/exposure weight (Hajek), so the rank transform reflects the population-weighted marginal; equivalently for count variables use the parametric NB-offset CDF (which is inherently exposure-aware) instead of the empirical rank. Pass W into randomized_pit_gaussianize.
- **risk:** The Gaussian latent scale is calibrated to the numerous small/noisy/reconstructed municipalities rather than to where the population and information actually are, systematically distorting every marginal and hence every correlation.


### LDO-margins / assembly

**`LDO-MARG-05` · HIGH · implementation_gap** — Per-cell reliability weights W are computed and propagated but NEVER used in the margin or the precision estimator
- **detail:** assemble.py builds a rich per-cell reliability tensor W from provenance (_STATE_WEIGHT: observed=1.0, broadcast=0.5, reconstructed=0.5, bounded=0.4, etc., lines 51-60) and the docstring promises W 'down-weights broadcast/reconstructed cells relative to observed ones'. margins.py carries W unchanged into GaussianField (line 8 comment 'Reliability weights W propagate unchanged'). But: (a) randomized_pit_gaussianize and count_exposure_gaussianize IGNORE W entirely — a reconstructed cell (W=0.25) is ranked and Gaussianized identically to an observed one; (b) the precision estimator pairwise_correlation / whitened_lagged_correlation (covariance.py) compute an UNWEIGHTED pairwise correlation — grep confirms field.W is only ever sliced/carried, never entered into any moment. So the entire reliability-weighting apparatus is dead weight: a municipality whose value is a national domain-scalar broadcast (W=0.25, essentially no information) contributes to the correlation with the same unit weight as a directly-observed high-n_eff cell. This silently violates the honest-edge principle (§0.4) and the MSD-III mandate that reconstructed/broadcast cells carry less evidential weight.
- **evidence:** assemble.py:51-60 (_STATE_WEIGHT), margins.py:8; covariance.py:79-101 (no W term); grep: field.W only sliced in edges.py:46, resolution.py, spatial_field.py — never in a moment
- **recommendation:** Enter W into the estimator: use weighted pairwise moments (mean_a = sum(w*x)/sum(w), cov = sum(w_ab*x_a*x_b)/sum(w_ab) with w_ab = sqrt(w_a*w_b) or min(w_a,w_b)), i.e. a reliability-weighted correlation, and derive an effective sample size (Kish n_eff = (sum w)^2/sum(w^2)) for the min_coverage / min_overlap gates and the Fisher-z SE. Equivalently down-weight cells in the whitened Gram accumulation. Verify with a test that a low-W reconstructed cell moves an edge less than an observed one.
- **risk:** Broadcast/reconstructed cells (which can be a large fraction of a context-heavy national panel) drive edges with full weight, so associations are dominated by fabricated-fill structure rather than observed data — a direct 'missingness with silence' style violation.


### LDO-margins / Gaussianization

**`LDO-MARG-06` · HIGH · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Gaussianization discards count-scale variance the denominator principle exists to preserve
- **detail:** MSD-III §II.3 / I.2 (lines 132, 63) make it normative that normalization stays 'in the model as an offset ... where the rate is the implied view with correct uncertainty. Materializing rates and having the LDO reverse-engineer them ... loses the count-variance.' The stated purpose of the count+exposure margin is to PRESERVE count variance (a rate of 1/10 and 100/1000 are not equally certain). But the copula margin then maps every count to a rank/PIT-uniform-to-Gaussian scale where, by construction, each variable has unit marginal variance and the count magnitude / Poisson variance information is ANNIHILATED — a municipality with 2 deaths and one with 2000 deaths at the same standardized-residual sign map to nearly the same Z. The heteroscedasticity (low-count cells are noisier) is exactly what §II.3 says must survive, yet the Gaussianization throws it away at Layer 0 and only the FROZEN, equal-variance Z reaches the precision solver. The count-with-exposure margin thus honors the denominator principle for the MEAN (offset) but violates it for the VARIANCE (the marginal is renormalized to unit variance, so a noisy 2-count cell influences the correlation exactly as much as a precise 2000-count cell). The reliability weight W was the intended vehicle to carry this and it is unused (LDO-MARG-05), so the count-variance is lost after all — the precise failure §II.3 warns against.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §II.3 (line 132), §I.2 (line 63), §3.12.9 CV=1/sqrt(Y) (line 5019); margins.py forces unit-variance Z via PIT
- **recommendation:** Carry a per-cell precision weight derived from the count into the estimator (LDO-MARG-05) so cell influence scales with count-based information (e.g. w ∝ mu_i or 1/CV^2 ≈ Y). Alternatively use a Gaussian-copula estimator on the PIT scale but with a per-cell Fisher-information weight, or keep a variance-stabilized (Anscombe 2*sqrt(x+3/8)) representation alongside the rank scale so the solver sees both location and count-precision.
- **risk:** The system claims (per §II.3) to preserve count-variance but in fact discards it; rare-disease municipal signals are treated with false precision, defeating the entire stated rationale for the count+exposure architecture.


### LDO-margins / copula

**`LDO-MARG-07` · HIGH · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Gaussian copula imposes tail-independence that epidemic threshold/clustering dependence violates
- **detail:** The whole Layer-0/Layer-1 architecture (§III.5: transform to Gaussian, model dependence THERE, map back) is a GAUSSIAN-COPULA assumption: it models dependence via a single correlation/precision matrix on the latent normal scale, which has ZERO tail dependence (lambda_U = 0) and symmetric, monotone dependence. Epidemiological co-dynamics are the opposite: arbovirus outbreaks produce JOINT extremes (dengue and chikungunya spike together in the same municipalities in the same season — upper-tail dependence), threshold effects (transmission only above a temperature/rainfall cutoff), and asymmetry (co-absence of two rare diseases is uninformative, co-presence is highly informative). A Gaussian copula fitted to such data underestimates joint-extreme co-occurrence and represents a threshold relationship as a weak linear partial correlation, so the LDO's central edge (e.g. Zika->microcephaly during the epidemic peak) is attenuated toward the bulk. The HSIC residual audit (§III.6) is the intended backstop but it only FLAGS nonlinearity for promotion — the primary precision graph is still Gaussian-copula and drives certification/stability. This is a theoretical limitation of the MSD design, not just the code.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §III.5 (line 222, 'dependence is modeled there'), §III.6 HSIC as detector-not-engine (line 226); precision.py graphical_lasso on Gaussian corr
- **recommendation:** Augment the Gaussian copula with tail-aware structure where flagged: (i) fit a t-copula (one extra d.o.f. parameter) to admit symmetric tail dependence, or vine/factor copulas for asymmetric tails; (ii) promote HSIC-flagged pairs to explicit threshold/interaction terms BEFORE the final graph, not merely as annotations; (iii) report a tail-dependence diagnostic per certified edge so a Gaussian-attenuated epidemic edge is not under-reported. At minimum, document that contemporaneous edge weights are lower bounds for tail-dependent pairs.
- **risk:** The signature epidemiological signals (joint outbreak co-occurrence, threshold-triggered transmission) are systematically under-estimated by the very layer meant to discover them; the win-condition edges (Zika, arbovirus co-circulation) are attenuated.


### LDO-margins / structural zeros

**`LDO-MARG-09` · MEDIUM · epidemiological_risk** — Structural (impossible) zeros and sampling zeros conflated; rank PIT spreads them all across the same CDF band
- **detail:** The docstring claims the randomized PIT correctly handles 'structural zeros' by spreading ties uniformly across their CDF band (margins.py:10-12, 42-49). But it does NOT distinguish a STRUCTURAL zero (a disease that cannot occur in that municipality — e.g. a coastal-only vector absent inland, or a procedure not offered by any local facility: a true zero probability at risk) from a SAMPLING zero (at-risk but no case observed) from an UNDERCOUNT zero (case occurred but was not reported — pervasive in SIM/SINAN for small municipalities). Randomized PIT treats all identical zeros as exchangeable ties and jitters them uniformly over [0, F(0)] — so a structural-zero municipality gets assigned a random 'low but nonzero latent propensity' indistinguishable from a municipality that merely had a quiet year. This fabricates a latent gradient among cells that are epidemiologically not-at-risk, and for undercount zeros it treats a reporting failure as a true low rate (biasing rate-determinant edges toward 'poor municipalities have low disease' when they in fact have low REPORTING). The count-exposure margin's exposure>0 gate (margins.py:103) only removes zero-EXPOSURE cells, not structural zeros with positive population.
- **evidence:** margins.py:10-12, 42-63, 103; MSD-I §6.2 zero-inflated routing to hurdle/ZINB (line 6270)
- **recommendation:** Model the zero process explicitly with a hurdle/ZINB margin (a Bernoulli at-risk gate whose structural-zero probability is estimated / supplied by the carrier registry and DiseaseGraph presence), so structural zeros are gated OUT of the count margin rather than jittered into it. For undercount, carry a reporting-completeness weight (SIM/SINAN completeness by municipality-year) into W so undercount zeros are down-weighted, not read as true low rates. Distinguish the three zero types in provenance.
- **risk:** Not-at-risk and under-reporting municipalities are assigned spurious latent disease propensities; determinant edges linking poverty/remoteness to LOW disease are reporting artefacts presented as epidemiology.


### LDO-margins / inverse map

**`LDO-MARG-10` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — inverse_gaussianize uses a biased, off-by-one nearest-rank empirical quantile with no interpolation
- **detail:** inverse_gaussianize (margins.py:66-85) maps a latent z back to native scale by idx = clip(int(u*obs.size), 0, n-1); obs[idx]. This is the crude nearest-rank quantile with floor rounding: (i) it is NOT the inverse of the forward randomized PIT (the forward map uses (#<x + U*#=x)/n; the inverse uses floor(u*n) — these are inconsistent estimators of F^{-1}, so a forward-then-inverse round trip does not return x); (ii) int() truncates toward zero, systematically biasing the recovered value DOWN by up to one order statistic (a proper empirical quantile uses ceil or a (n+1) plotting-position with interpolation, e.g. numpy.quantile's linear method); (iii) it clamps to the observed min/max so any counterfactual / extrapolative readout (a predicted level above anything historically seen — precisely what a projection asks for) is silently truncated to the sample maximum, hiding out-of-range predictions. For a heavily zero-inflated variable, u below F(0) maps to obs[0]=0 fine, but just above it jumps discontinuously to the smallest positive count with no smoothing.
- **evidence:** margins.py:66-85 (idx = clip((u*obs.size).astype(int)...)); forward map margins.py:57-60
- **recommendation:** Use a consistent, interpolating inverse: idx via numpy.quantile(obs, u, method='linear') or the Hazen/(k-0.5)/n plotting position matching the forward PIT's expected rank, and for extensive counts invert the SAME parametric CDF used forward (nbinom.ppf(u, mu_i)) rather than an empirical table, so the round trip is exact. Flag (not clamp) out-of-support readouts so extrapolative counterfactuals are visible with widened uncertainty.
- **risk:** Any native-scale readout (predicted count, counterfactual level) is biased low and cannot represent out-of-sample values; round-trip inconsistency means the 'inverse' is not actually the inverse, quietly corrupting counterfactual outputs.


### LDO-margins / routing

**`LDO-MARG-12` · MEDIUM · implementation_gap** — Count-vs-rank routing is a numeric heuristic (any positive exposure entry), not a typed extensive/intensive decision
- **detail:** gaussianize_field (margins.py:118-136) chooses the count-exposure margin for variable j iff its exposure row has any finite entry >0, else the rank margin. This is a fragile numeric switch, not the §I.2 typed extensive/intensive decision. Failure modes: (a) an INTENSIVE variable (already a rate/ratio/proportion — e.g. a SIDRA index, a Gini coefficient) that happens to be handed a spurious/partial exposure column would be wrongly Poisson-standardized as if it were a count; (b) an EXTENSIVE count whose exposure sidecar failed to load or is partially missing (assemble.py returns None silently on any read/schema error, lines 27-33) SILENTLY falls back to the rank margin — so the denominator principle is quietly abandoned for exactly the variables it is supposed to protect, with no warning; (c) mixed coverage (exposure present in some cells, absent in others for the same variable) leads the two branches to Gaussianize different cell subsets on incompatible scales within one variable is avoided only because the branch is all-or-nothing, but the cells lacking exposure in a count variable are then dropped to NaN (margins.py:103) rather than rank-transformed — a partial-exposure count loses cells entirely.
- **evidence:** margins.py:118-136 (np.nanmax(...)>0 switch); assemble.py:27-33, 195-198 (silent None on missing/malformed sidecar); MSD-III §I.2 extensive/intensive (line 63)
- **recommendation:** Drive the routing from the variable's declared kind/extensive flag in the measures/carrier registry (MSD-III line 109: extensive: bool, exposure_ref), not from the numeric presence of exposure. Raise (or emit a typed warning + provenance flag) when a declared-extensive variable lacks a usable exposure, rather than silently using the rank margin. For partial-exposure counts, impute the exposure from the denominator asset (never drop the cell) so the variable is not silently truncated.
- **risk:** The denominator principle is applied by accident of data presence rather than by contract; extensive variables silently lose the count+exposure treatment when a sidecar hiccups, and intensive variables risk being mis-standardized — untyped, unlogged, undetectable in the output.


### LDO-margins / numerics

**`LDO-MARG-13` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — Magic epsilon clip 1e-6 caps |Z| at ~4.75 and is applied uniformly regardless of sample size, censoring true extremes
- **detail:** _EPS = 1e-6 (margins.py:25) clips u to [1e-6, 1-1e-6] before ndtri, capping |Z| at ndtri(1e-6) ≈ 4.75. This is a fixed magic number independent of n. Consequences: (i) for a national panel with n ~ 10^5-10^6 cells, the most extreme observed value legitimately has empirical rank ~1/n which can be far into the tail; clipping at 1e-6 CENSORS the genuine extremes (the biggest outbreak municipality-year) to a common ceiling, flattening exactly the high-signal tail the LDO wants; (ii) for the count margin, poisson.cdf(x-1,mu)+U*pmf can legitimately land in the extreme tail for a large surprise, again capped; (iii) the SAME constant also floors mu at 1e-6 (margins.py:112) — a cell with tiny lambda*E gives mu≈1e-6, and a single case there yields an astronomically small poisson.pmf, so u≈F(x-1)≈1 and z pins to +4.75, i.e. every rare-disease case in a small municipality maps to the SAME saturated latent value, manufacturing ties at the ceiling (the opposite of what the randomized PIT is supposed to fix). The clip should scale like 1/(2n) or be replaced by a continuity-corrected tail.
- **evidence:** margins.py:25 (_EPS=1e-6), 61, 112, 114
- **recommendation:** Make the clip sample-size aware: u_clip = 1/(2n) .. 1-1/(2n) (or use the log-CDF via scipy for tail accuracy and ndtri on log-scale) so extremes are not all pinned to ~4.75. Do not reuse the same epsilon as a mu floor; instead gate cells with mu below a support threshold (mu_min, cf MSD-I §3.12.8 mu_min=50) to the at-risk/hurdle branch rather than saturating them. Verify the largest-outbreak cell retains a distinct, larger |Z| than the second-largest.
- **risk:** The extreme tail — the epidemic peaks that are the primary scientific target — is censored to a common ceiling and rare-event small-area cases collapse into ceiling ties, degrading precisely the signals the system exists to detect.


### pairwise-complete covariance → nearest-correlation

**`LDO-PSD-01` · HIGH · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Pairwise-complete correlation is provably indefinite; the MSD treats it as a valid covariance
- **detail:** The MSD (§II.6.2) writes the estimation objective over an empirical covariance C as if C were a bona fide Gram matrix. But the implementation (covariance.py:83-100) assembles each entry corr[a,b] from the DIFFERENT subset of cells where both a and b are observed. A matrix whose entries are estimated on incompatible sub-samples is a classic source of NON-PSD 'covariance': it is not the Gram matrix of any single data set, so it can and generically will have negative eigenvalues (this is a well-known pathology of pairwise-deletion covariance, cf. Wothke 1993; Lounici 2014 on missing-data covariance). The MSD never acknowledges this — it silently assumes the copula-margined field yields a PSD C. For sparse Brazilian municipal panels where overlap patterns vary wildly across variable pairs (SIM mortality observed in different cells than SIH morbidity than SIA procedures), the indefiniteness is not a rounding artifact but structural. Feeding an indefinite C into a Gaussian log-likelihood (-logdet(S-L)+tr((S-L)C)) makes the objective non-convex / unbounded below in the direction of C's negative eigenspace.
- **evidence:** MSD-II §II.6.2 objective (line 247); covariance.py:83-100 (per-pair overlap n_ab, mean_a/mean_b computed on overlap-with-b vs overlap-with-a)
- **recommendation:** Do not paper over indefiniteness with a one-shot eigen-clip. Either (a) estimate C by a JOINTLY-PSD method — EM/IPF for the Gaussian-copula covariance under MAR, or a single low-rank+sparse matrix completion (Candès-Recht) that is PSD by construction, or (b) use a shrinkage estimator (Ledoit-Wolf toward a PSD target) applied to a consistent pairwise estimate. Report the pre-projection minimum eigenvalue and the Frobenius distance moved by projection as a data-quality diagnostic — a large move should abort the fit.
- **risk:** Edges are read off a precision matrix derived from a fabricated PSD surrogate that can differ materially from any real covariance; spurious or missing edges with no way to tell which.


### nearest-correlation projection

**`LDO-NCORR-02` · HIGH · implementation_gap** — '_nearest_correlation' is eigen-clip+rescale, NOT the metric-nearest correlation matrix (Higham)
- **detail:** The function name and docstring claim 'the nearest PSD correlation matrix' (covariance.py:38-45), but the code does a single eigenvalue clip to floor=1e-3, reconstructs, then divides by sqrt(diag) to restore unit diagonal. This is NOT Higham's (2002) nearest-correlation-matrix projection. Two defects: (1) The clip-then-rescale is a single step of an alternating projection between the PSD cone and the unit-diagonal set; one step does not converge to the intersection, so the result is generally neither the nearest correlation matrix nor even guaranteed to still be PSD after the final diagonal rescale (rescaling by outer(d,d) can reintroduce indefiniteness because it is a congruence that changes the spectrum). (2) The floor=1e-3 is applied to the RAW eigenvalues, not to a relative scale, so for a well-conditioned block it barely perturbs, while for a near-degenerate block it inflates small eigenvalues arbitrarily — biasing the corresponding partial correlations (precision = inverse, so the smallest eigenvalues dominate the precision and hence the EDGES). This directly distorts the scientific output.
- **evidence:** covariance.py:38-45; called at covariance.py:105 and 216
- **recommendation:** Replace with Higham's alternating projection (Newton or Dykstra variant; scipy has no built-in but statsmodels.stats.correlation_tools.corr_nearest implements it) and iterate to a tolerance. Make the eigenvalue floor RELATIVE (floor = eps * lambda_max) and record the achieved min eigenvalue. If the nearest-correlation move is large, treat the block as ill-posed rather than silently proceeding.
- **risk:** Partial correlations (the emitted edge weights) are biased by an ad-hoc spectral surgery that concentrates its distortion exactly on the small eigenvalues that determine the precision.


### separable Kronecker precision / temporal whitening

**`LDO-TIME-03` · CRITICAL · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Temporal autocorrelation is never whitened before the fit; Σ_time⁻¹ is post-hoc telemetry only
- **detail:** The MSD (§II.6.1, §III.4) posits Prec(Z) ≈ Ω_var ⊗ Σ_space⁻¹ ⊗ Σ_time⁻¹ and the whole tractability rests on all three factors acting. The implementation only ever WHITENS the spatial axis (whitened_lagged_correlation, precision.py _whiten_spatial) and then treats the n_cells = S·T_eff observations as iid Gaussian when computing the correlation that feeds CPW. Σ_time⁻¹ appears ONLY in kron.py as a best-effort log-det telemetry number (orchestrator.py:400, wrapped in try/except that swallows all errors), NOT in the estimator. This is a fundamental identity gap: lag-extended features [Z(t),Z(t-1),...,Z(t-K)] are massively temporally autocorrelated (that's the entire point of lag extension), and stacking overlapping windows means the 'samples' are serially dependent. Estimating a precision from serially-correlated data as if iid understates the effective sample size by a factor of ~1/(1-φ) to 1/(1-φ²) and BIASES partial correlations toward spurious lagged edges (Yule's nonsense-correlation between trending/autocorrelated series). For Brazilian series with strong secular trends (epidemiological transition, SUS coverage expansion) φ is near 1, so the effective-n inflation is severe.
- **evidence:** lags.py:109-141 (only spatial whiten, no temporal); kron.py used only in orchestrator.py:380-407 telemetry; MSD-II §II.6.1 line 238-239
- **recommendation:** Actually apply the Kronecker temporal whitener: whiten Z along time by Σ_time^{-1/2} (the AR/smoothness factor) BEFORE forming the correlation, or equivalently GLS-correct the covariance for serial dependence, so the iid assumption the CPW likelihood makes is approximately true. Alternatively compute an autocorrelation-corrected effective sample size and propagate it into edge uncertainty and stability thresholds. At minimum, stop reporting the Kronecker log-det as if the fit used that operator — it does not.
- **risk:** Every lagged edge — including the flagship 'discover Zika's 6-9 month lag' capability — is estimated from serially-correlated windows treated as iid, inflating significance and manufacturing spurious distributed-lag curves from common trends.


### GMRF spatial whitening

**`LDO-WHITEN-04` · HIGH · msd_theoretical_fragility** — (κI+L_W)^{1/2} whitening imputes missing cells to 0 and uses the unnormalized degree Laplacian — over/under-corrects heterogeneously
- **detail:** Two coupled problems. (1) The whitener Σ_space^{-1/2}=(κI+L_W)^{1/2} is a DENSE spatial operator that cannot honour per-cell missingness, so missing cells are set to the margin mean 0 before whitening (precision.py:93, covariance.py:176, explicitly documented lags.py:104-106). Mean-imputation into a differencing operator (L_W is a graph difference operator) creates artificial spatial GRADIENTS at the boundary between observed and missing municipalities — a municipality that is truly missing gets treated as 'exactly average', so its observed neighbours acquire a spurious large whitened residual. Missingness in DATASUS is spatially clustered (poor/remote municipalities under-report), so this bias is systematic, not noise. (2) L_W = D − A is the UNNORMALIZED combinatorial Laplacian with degree on the diagonal (precision.py:73). Its eigenvalues scale with node degree, so κI+L_W whitens high-degree (interior, ~6 neighbours) municipalities far more aggressively than low-degree (coastal/border, 1-2 neighbours) ones. The whitening strength is therefore confounded with geography — exactly the spatial structure it claims to remove — distorting partial correlations non-uniformly across space.
- **evidence:** precision.py:70-78 (κ+degree diagonal, -1 off-diag = unnormalized L); precision.py:93 & covariance.py:176 (NaN→0); lags.py:104-106
- **recommendation:** Use the SPDE/BYM2-style parameterization (Simpson et al. 2017; Riebler 2016): a scaled/normalized structured precision whose marginal variances are homogenized, with a mixing parameter separating structured from unstructured spatial variance. For missingness, whiten only over the observed sub-graph (restrict L_W to observed nodes and recompute degrees) rather than imputing 0 — this is exact and honours the sparsity. Fit κ (and the spatial range) rather than fixing it.
- **risk:** Spatial 'net-of-space' edges are net of a mis-specified, degree- and missingness-confounded whitening — the correction can be worse than the disease it treats, especially for the sparse remote municipalities that are the epidemiological blind spots.


### GMRF prior

**`LDO-KAPPA-05` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — κ=1 default is an unmotivated magic number; it sets the spatial smoothing scale with no estimation or sensitivity
- **detail:** κ in Σ_space⁻¹=κI+L_W is the ratio of unstructured (nugget) to structured spatial precision — it controls how far the GMRF borrows strength and how strongly whitening damps spatial autocorrelation. It is hardcoded κ=1.0 everywhere (precision.py:44,54; lags.py:74; orchestrator.py:101) with no data-driven estimation and no sensitivity analysis. With degree ~6, κ=1 means the identity term is ~1/6 of the diagonal, so the whitener is dominated by the Laplacian — a strong, essentially arbitrary smoothing choice. In a proper GMRF/INLA treatment κ is a hyperparameter with a prior, estimated by marginal likelihood; here it is a fiat constant. Different κ give materially different partial-correlation matrices (the whitener is κ-dependent), so the entire edge set is a function of an unjustified constant. The docstring at lags.py:106 even claims whitening 'reduces to a κ-scaling (a no-op at κ=1)' when municipalities are absent from the adjacency — but a κ-scaling of a correlation matrix is genuinely a no-op only because correlation is scale-free, which quietly means the whitening did NOTHING for those nodes, inconsistent with the nodes that ARE in the adjacency.
- **evidence:** precision.py:44,54,73; lags.py:74,106; orchestrator.py:101
- **recommendation:** Estimate κ (and ideally a spatial range parameter under an SPDE Matérn parameterization) by empirical Bayes / marginal likelihood, or at minimum run the fit across a κ grid and report edge stability across κ as part of the certification gate (regularization-path agreement already exists for λ — extend it to κ). Document the sensitivity.
- **risk:** The published dependency graph silently depends on an unestimated smoothing constant; reviewers cannot assess whether an edge is real or a κ artifact.


### CPW sparse+low-rank ADMM

**`LDO-ADMM-06` · HIGH · computational_trap** — ADMM stopping test uses only the primal residual — can certify convergence at a non-stationary iterate
- **detail:** The ADMM loop (lowrank.py:224-231) declares converged when pn/rn < tol where pn = ||R−(S−L)|| is the PRIMAL residual only. Standard ADMM convergence (Boyd et al. 2011 §3.3) requires BOTH the primal residual (constraint violation R=S−L) AND the DUAL residual (ρ·||(S−L) − (S−L)_prev||, the change in the consistency variable) to be small. Checking only the primal residual is a well-known trap: the primal residual can be tiny simply because R tracks S−L closely while both are still moving substantially each iteration (dual infeasibility), so the algorithm reports 'converged' at a point that is far from the KKT stationary point / saddle of the augmented Lagrangian. For the CPW problem this matters because the S/L SPLIT — which pair is direct vs latent_shared — is exactly what is unstable near a non-stationary point; a premature stop can lock in a wrong split. There is also no adaptive ρ (Boyd §3.4.1) to balance primal/dual, and no residual-normalization by problem scale, so tol=1e-5 means different things for different p and C-scale.
- **evidence:** lowrank.py:224-231 (primal-only stop); no dual residual anywhere in the loop; converged flag consumed by the §V.6 convergence gate downstream
- **recommendation:** Add the dual residual s^{k+1}=ρ(M^{k+1}−M^k) and require BOTH ||r||≤ε_pri and ||s||≤ε_dual with the standard absolute+relative tolerances (ε = √n·ε_abs + ε_rel·max(...)). Add adaptive ρ (τ_incr/τ_decr on the primal/dual ratio). Warm-start ρ from the covariance scale. This is cheap and turns 'converged' into a meaningful certificate.
- **risk:** The convergence gate downstream trusts a flag that can be true at a non-stationary point; the direct-vs-latent edge classification (the core scientific claim) may reflect an unconverged saddle.


### CPW incoherence / identifiability

**`LDO-INCOH-07` · HIGH · identifiability_concern** — min_factor_support=3 and loading_threshold=0.3 are magic proxies for the CPW incoherence condition, not a guarantee it holds
- **detail:** The Chandrasekaran-Parrilo-Willsky identifiability of the S−L split requires an INCOHERENCE condition: the sparse S must not be low-rank-like (bounded degree relative to p) and the low-rank L must not be sparse-like (its column space incoherent w.r.t. standard basis, quantified by μ(L)=max ||P_{col} e_i||). The code substitutes a heuristic READOUT rule (lowrank.py:270-282): a recovered factor is called 'shared' only if ≥ min_factor_support=3 variables load above loading_threshold=0.3. This is a reasonable-sounding but UNVALIDATED surrogate: (1) it operates on the OUTPUT L after ADMM, not on the identifiability of the problem — if the true structure is not CPW-identifiable, ADMM converges to the wrong (S,L) and no readout gate can recover the truth. (2) The thresholds 3 and 0.3 are arbitrary: a genuine weak-but-dense driver loading 0.25 on 20 variables (a real epidemiological wave touching many diseases mildly) fails the 0.3 gate on every variable and gets MISASSIGNED to S as a web of spurious direct edges — precisely the failure the low-rank layer exists to prevent. (3) A truly dense-but-weak confounder (climate, socioeconomic gradient) is exactly the 'dense but weak' case the guiding question warns about, and it violates the sparse-S incoherence assumption, so CPW cannot separate it regardless of the readout gate.
- **evidence:** lowrank.py:120-122 defaults (loading_threshold=0.3, min_factor_support=3); lowrank.py:270-282 readout gate; MSD-III §III.4(2)
- **recommendation:** Compute the actual CPW incoherence diagnostics (Chandrasekaran et al. 2012): the coherence μ(L) of the recovered low-rank column space and the max degree of S, and REFUSE/flag the fit when they violate the identifiability regime (the theory gives an explicit μ·deg < c bound). Make min_factor_support scale with p and loading_threshold relative to the factor's own loading distribution (e.g. energy concentration / participation ratio) rather than an absolute 0.3. Add a synthetic 'dense-weak confounder' to the planted-structure test and verify it lands in L, not S.
- **risk:** A real weak-dense driver (climate, poverty) is shattered into dozens of spurious direct disease-disease edges — the central scientific artifact CPW was chosen to prevent.


### separable Kronecker assumption

**`LDO-SEP-08` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Separability Ω_var⊗Σ_space⁻¹⊗Σ_time⁻¹ forbids space-varying and lag-varying dependence — the epidemiologically interesting case
- **detail:** The MSD itself flags separability as 'an approximation (audited by 6)' (§III.4(3)), but the audit (§V.6 exact-certifies-approximate + residual HSIC) checks whether the SEPARABLE fit reproduces held-out data — it does NOT test whether a variable dependency itself varies across space or lag, which separability structurally forbids. Separability asserts that the variable-dependency structure Ω_var is IDENTICAL in every municipality and the spatial correlation is IDENTICAL for every variable. This is epidemiologically false in Brazil: dengue-rainfall coupling is strong in the Northeast and weak in the temperate South (Ω_var varies by region); the spatial range of an airborne respiratory disease differs from a vector-borne one (Σ_space varies by variable). A separable model averages these into a single Ω_var and a single Σ_space, producing an edge that is a spatial average — potentially strong-somewhere/absent-elsewhere reported as a uniform moderate edge, or two regionally-opposite-sign couplings cancelling to 'no edge'. The BYM varying-coefficient field (fit_spatial_varying_coefficient, orchestrator.py:364) is a post-hoc per-edge readout, not a relaxation of the estimator's separability.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §III.4(3) line 216; kron.py:1-31; the fit uses one global Ω_var (lags.py) and one global whitener
- **recommendation:** Treat separability as a testable hypothesis, not a backbone: (a) fit region-stratified Ω_var and test homogeneity (a Kronecker-vs-non-Kronecker likelihood-ratio / the residual-HSIC on region-interaction terms specifically), (b) allow a low-rank NON-separable correction term (Kronecker + low-rank, which kron_cg_solve already anticipates) so dominant space×variable interactions are captured, (c) report per-edge spatial heterogeneity as a first-class output with an explicit 'sign-flips-across-space' warning, not just a smoothed field.
- **risk:** Regionally heterogeneous couplings — the whole point of a national vs single-state study — are averaged away or cancelled; the model reports a fictitious homogeneous Brazil.


### regularization selection

**`LDO-LAMBDA-09` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — λ1, λ2, α fixed at 0.1/0.1/0.05 with no data-driven selection; stability selection controls the edge set but not the penalty
- **detail:** The graphical-lasso α=0.05 (precision.py:108) and the CPW λ1=λ2=0.1 (lowrank.py:118-119, orchestrator.py:102-103) are fixed constants. The MSD leans on stability selection (§III.7) for multiplicity control, and stability selection (Meinshausen-Bühlmann 2010) is indeed designed to be relatively insensitive to λ over a RANGE — but that guarantee requires either subsampling ACROSS a λ path (randomized lasso) or a principled λ_min; a single fixed λ does not inherit the stability-selection error control. Moreover λ1 and λ2 jointly determine the S/L tradeoff (how much variance is pushed to the low-rank component); at fixed λ2 the RANK of L is not selected, it is whatever the trace penalty yields, and there is no guarantee it matches the true number of drivers.
