Two fixed scalars silently pin both the sparsity level and the latent rank — the two most consequential modeling choices — to round numbers.
- **evidence:** precision.py:108 (alpha=0.05); lowrank.py:118-119; orchestrator.py:102-103; MSD-III §III.7 stability selection
- **recommendation:** Select λ1 by an information criterion (EBIC for graphical models, Foygel-Drton 2010) or by the stability-selection-across-a-λ-path protocol (StARS, Liu-Roeder-Wasserman 2010) rather than a fixed value; select λ2 / the latent rank by matching the eigengap of L or by cross-validated held-out log-likelihood. Report the chosen values and the sensitivity of the edge set to them.
- **risk:** The sparsity and latent rank of the national dependency graph are set by round-number defaults, not the data; edge counts are an artifact of λ.


### copula margin (Layer 0 feeding the covariance)

**`LDO-MARGIN-10` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Count-with-exposure margin is Poisson-offset (equidispersed); Brazilian municipal counts are strongly overdispersed → mis-calibrated latent Z → biased correlations
- **detail:** The covariance/precision consumes the Gaussianized field Z. For extensive counts the margin is a Poisson-with-offset CDF, μ=λ·E with a single pooled MLE rate λ=Σcount/Σexposure (margins.py:88-115). Poisson forces Var=Mean. Municipal disease counts (SIM deaths, SIH admissions) are overdispersed by orders of magnitude — heterogeneity across municipalities, zero-inflation, reporting variation — the textbook case for Negative Binomial. Using a Poisson PIT on overdispersed data mis-calibrates the probability integral transform: the randomized-PIT u=F(x-1)+U·p(x) is only uniform if F is the TRUE CDF; under overdispersion the Poisson CDF is wrong in the tails, so Φ⁻¹(u) produces a latent Z that is NOT standard Gaussian and whose tail dependence is distorted. Since the whole LDO models dependence on the Z scale, a mis-calibrated margin propagates directly into biased correlations and hence spurious/attenuated edges. A single pooled λ compounds this: it ignores between-municipality rate heterogeneity, so high-rate municipalities get systematically negative Z and low-rate positive — a manufactured spatial gradient that the whitener then tries (and fails, per LDO-WHITEN-04) to remove.
- **evidence:** margins.py:88-115 (Poisson cdf/pmf, single pooled lam=x.sum()/total_e); MSD-III §III.5 lists NB as an admissible family but the count-with-exposure path hardcodes Poisson
- **recommendation:** Use a Negative-Binomial-with-offset margin (estimate the dispersion by method-of-moments or MLE) for the PIT, which the MSD-I family set already licenses. Allow a spatially/covariate-varying rate (a quick GLM offset fit) rather than one pooled λ so the PIT is conditionally calibrated. Add a PIT-calibration diagnostic (are the u's uniform? a KS test) as a gate before the covariance step.
- **risk:** The latent Gaussian field is not Gaussian and carries a manufactured rate gradient; every downstream correlation, whitening, and edge inherits the mis-calibration.


### K=0 precision fallback

**`LDO-GLASSO-11` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — graphical_lasso failure silently falls back to pinv — abandons sparsity/regularization without any signal
- **detail:** In fit_contemporaneous_precision (precision.py:122-125) a graphical_lasso exception is caught and replaced by np.linalg.pinv(corr). This is a silent, semantics-changing fallback: pinv gives a DENSE, UNREGULARIZED precision, so every pair becomes a nonzero 'edge' and the sparsity discipline that defines the method is abandoned — with no warning propagated to the link records or certification gate. graphical_lasso most often fails precisely when C is ill-conditioned or barely-PSD (i.e. exactly the LDO-PSD-01 / LDO-NCORR-02 situation), so the fallback fires in the worst cases and produces the least trustworthy output while looking like a normal result. The pinv of a near-singular projected-correlation matrix also amplifies the small-eigenvalue distortions from the nearest-correlation step.
- **evidence:** precision.py:122-125 (bare except → pinv)
- **recommendation:** On graphical_lasso failure, do not silently densify: increase the diagonal loading / raise α and retry, or fail the fit with a typed warning that is recorded on every affected edge's certification_status. Never emit a dense pinv precision as if it were a sparse graphical model result.
- **risk:** An ill-conditioned block yields a fully-connected 'dependency graph' indistinguishable from a real one in the output — a maximally misleading failure mode.


### quadratic prior-regularizers (temporal + disease Laplacian)

**`LDO-SMOOTH-12` · MEDIUM · implementation_gap** — Smoothness prior enters via a single linearized prox-gradient step per ADMM iteration — not the exact proximal operator; convergence claim is overstated
- **detail:** The §III.4(5) quadratic priors +½tr(SᵀGS) are added to the S-subproblem by ONE proximal-GRADIENT step (lowrank.py:219-220): grad = G·S + ρ(S−M), then soft-threshold with step 1/lipschitz. The docstring claims 'at the fixed point ∇q=0 so the ADMM converges to the true smoothed-objective solution'. This is imprecise: a single gradient step is NOT the exact prox of the composite (½tr(SᵀGS)+ℓ1+ (ρ/2)||S−M||²); it is one iteration of a proximal-gradient inner solve. Embedding one inner step inside the outer ADMM makes this a linearized/inexact ADMM whose convergence requires the inner step to be sufficiently contractive AND the outer tolerances to account for inner inexactness (Ouyang et al. 2015; inexact ADMM theory). The Lipschitz step 1/(ρ+λ_max(G)) guarantees the single step is non-expansive but NOT that the composite fixed point is reached in one step per outer iteration without additional conditions. Combined with the primal-only stop (LDO-ADMM-06), the 'true smoothed solution' claim is not established.
- **evidence:** lowrank.py:213-220 and docstring lines 214-218
- **recommendation:** Either solve the S-subproblem to a genuine inner tolerance (a few prox-gradient/FISTA steps) each outer iteration, or adopt a linearized-ADMM formulation with a proven convergence guarantee and inner-inexactness-aware outer tolerances. Add a planted-smoothness test verifying the recovered S matches the exact smoothed-objective minimizer, not just that the loop terminates.
- **risk:** The temporal/disease smoothing may be under- or over-applied relative to the stated objective; the 'converges to the true solution' guarantee the code advertises is not actually met.


### randomized-SVD low-rank readout + noise floor

**`LDO-RANDSVD-13` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — 2%-of-top-eigenvalue noise floor is a magic threshold that can drop real weak drivers or admit PSD-projection noise; error propagation is a point estimate
- **detail:** The latent factors are read off L with a hard keep-rule vals > max(1e-6, 0.02·vals.max()) (lowrank.py:258). The 2% relative floor is a magic number doing real scientific work: any genuine shared driver whose eigenvalue is below 2% of the largest driver is discarded (a secondary seasonal/reporting factor coexisting with a dominant epidemic wave is exactly this case), while at the same time the floor exists to suppress the fat tail of tiny PSD-projection eigenvalues — so it is simultaneously too aggressive for weak real factors and a band-aid for a numerical artifact of the L-step. The randomized-SVD truncation error is then reported as numerical_error = dropped.max()/vals.max() (lowrank.py:264-267), a single ratio, propagated as each latent_shared edge's uncertainty — but randomized SVD error is stochastic with a distribution (Halko-Martinsson-Tropp give tail bounds), not a deterministic ratio, and n_iter=4 power iterations / n_oversamples=10 are fixed regardless of the eigengap, so the actual error can exceed this point estimate when the spectrum decays slowly.
- **evidence:** lowrank.py:105-107 (fixed n_iter=4, n_oversamples=10), 258 (0.02 floor), 264-267 (point-estimate error)
- **recommendation:** Choose the retained rank by an eigengap / parallel-analysis / Marchenko-Pastur criterion rather than a fixed 2%, and separate 'real weak factor' from 'PSD-projection noise' by their STABILITY across stability-selection subsamples (a noise eigenvalue won't recur). Adapt n_iter to the observed spectral decay and report a proper randomized-SVD error interval, not a single ratio.
- **risk:** Secondary latent drivers (e.g. a reporting shock under an epidemic wave) are silently dropped, and the reported factor uncertainty understates the true randomized-approximation error.


### Kronecker temporal factor telemetry

**`LDO-AR1-14` · MEDIUM · msd_theoretical_fragility** — A single globally-pooled AR(1) φ stands in for all temporal dependence across every variable and municipality
- **detail:** When the (telemetry-only) Kronecker log-det is computed, the temporal factor's φ is a SINGLE scalar estimated as the mean lag-1 autocorrelation pooled across ALL variables and ALL municipalities (orchestrator.py:388-399), clipped to ±0.95. Beyond the fact that this factor never enters the actual fit (LDO-TIME-03), the pooling is itself a strong misspecification even for the telemetry: chronic-disease mortality (pancreatic C25) is near-random-walk (φ→1) while acute vector-borne incidence is oscillatory/seasonal (negative or complex autocorrelation) — a single φ describes neither, and AR(1) cannot represent seasonality at all (the MSD explicitly wants Σ_time to 'encode seasonality', §II.6.1 line 239). Clipping to 0.95 also silently caps the trending/non-stationary series that most need a unit-root-aware treatment. So the separable log-det that feeds the §V.6 validity report is computed under a temporal model that is wrong for most variables.
- **evidence:** orchestrator.py:388-399 (pooled φ, clip 0.95); kron.py:41-62 (AR(1) only); MSD-II line 239 wants seasonality
- **recommendation:** Use at least a per-variable φ, and replace AR(1) with a seasonal-plus-trend temporal precision (an ARMA/seasonal-difference or a low-order harmonic GMRF over the time axis) to represent the seasonality the MSD explicitly requires. If the factor stays telemetry-only, label the validity number as a crude bound, not a validated separable log-det.
- **risk:** The separability validity report is computed under a temporal model that fits neither chronic nor seasonal disease dynamics, undermining the §V.6 audit it feeds.


### edge uncertainty / sample size

**`LDO-NEFF-15` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — n_samples reported as min pairwise coverage; ignores overlap heterogeneity and serial/spatial dependence → overstated precision of edges
- **detail:** PrecisionFit.n_samples is set to the MINIMUM per-variable coverage (precision.py:142) and is the sample size any downstream uncertainty/CI computation would use. This is doubly wrong for the pairwise-complete design: (1) the effective sample size for a given EDGE (a,b) is the pairwise OVERLAP n_ab of that specific pair (covariance.py:83), which varies enormously across pairs — using a single scalar min-coverage mis-sizes the uncertainty of every edge, over-stating well-overlapped pairs' n and under-stating others'. (2) It counts cells as independent, ignoring the spatial autocorrelation (partially whitened) and temporal autocorrelation (not whitened at all, LDO-TIME-03) — so the true effective n is far smaller than the raw count. The MSD-I §3.12.3 n_eff machinery (referenced in project memory as already fixed elsewhere) is not applied here. Edge confidence intervals / stability thresholds built on this n_samples will be too tight.
- **evidence:** precision.py:142 (n_samples = pw.coverage.min()); covariance.py:83 (per-pair n_ab exists but is discarded)
- **recommendation:** Carry the per-pair overlap n_ab through to each edge and use it (not a global min) for that edge's uncertainty; deflate by an autocorrelation-based effective-sample-size factor (1−ρ)/(1+ρ) style, reusing the §3.12.3 n_eff logic. Propagate per-edge n into the link record's uncertainty field.
- **risk:** Edge uncertainties are computed against a wrong, dependence-blind sample size; certification/stability gates calibrated on it admit edges that are not real.


### LDO-lags-temporal

**`LDO-LAG-CONF-01` · CRITICAL · epidemiological_risk** — Lag-extended precision identifies conditional-Granger association, not directed effects; low-rank L cannot absorb LAGGED common trends
- **detail:** The docstring (lags.py:5-13) and MSD-II §II.6.1 (line 254) claim a nonzero cross-lag precision block X_i(t-k)→X_j(t) is a 'directed lag-k link' because 'time licenses the direction'. Mathematically this is exactly conditional Granger causality: after partialling out all other lag features, X_i(t-k) still linearly predicts X_j(t). Temporal precedence rules out reverse causation but does NOT rule out confounding by a common trend that acts with different lags on i and j. The CPW low-rank term L is estimated on the SAME lag-extended covariance, but a single shared factor f enters each variable at whatever lag it happens to load — if a latent driver (e.g. the demographic/epidemiological transition, ageing, urbanization) raises variable i and, some years later, variable j, that lagged co-movement is a rank-1 structure ACROSS DIFFERENT LAG BLOCKS, i.e. an off-diagonal cross-lag block, which the sparse S will pick up as a 'directed edge' precisely because L (fit to explain within-lag co-movement, and truncated to rank r≪p) does not naturally represent a lag-shifted common factor. So the flagship Zika-style claim 'the engine finds the lag, not told it' actually discovers lagged predictive association confounded by common trends — the single most pervasive feature of 25-year Brazilian municipal health series. Over 2000-2024 essentially every disease/mortality series shares a monotone secular trend; any pair with a phase offset will yield a spurious peaked response curve.
- **evidence:** MSD-II §II.6.1 line 239,254; MSD-III §III.4(4) line 217; src/pegasus/ldo/lags.py:5-13,158-183; low-rank absorbs only within-covariance shared structure per lowrank.py:9-14
- **recommendation:** Do NOT label cross-lag edges 'directed' from precision alone. (1) Detrend/difference each series before Gaussianization (fit on innovations, not levels) so common secular trends cannot manufacture lagged edges — or add a per-variable deterministic time-trend/spline covariate in the copula margin (§III.5) so Z is trend-residualized. (2) Explicitly test each candidate lagged edge against a common-trend null (fit a shared smooth national trend factor and require the edge to survive its removal), not just the rank-r L. (3) Rename edge_type to 'granger_predictive' and require Rung-2 (ITS/DiD around dated shocks, already in the ladder §IV) before any directional-causal language.
- **risk:** Every published 'discovered distributed-lag response curve' (the headline capability) is potentially a trend-phase artifact; the Zika acceptance test may pass on synthetic stationary data yet the national run emits confidently-wrong directed edges.

**`LDO-LAG-STAT-02` · CRITICAL · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Stationarity: a SINGLE response curve is pooled over 2000-2024, assuming the lag structure is time-invariant across a major epidemiological transition
- **detail:** whitened_lagged_correlation and pairwise_correlation accumulate one covariance/correlation matrix over ALL time slices t in [K,T) (covariance.py:191-203, 83-86). The estimator therefore assumes the joint distribution of the lag-extended vector is time-homogeneous — one Ω_var for the whole 25-year window. Brazil 2000-2024 spans a documented epidemiological transition: infectious → chronic-degenerative dominance, SUS coverage expansion, SIM/SINASC completeness rising from ~80% to ~95%+, arbovirus emergence (dengue serotype shifts, Zika 2015, chikungunya). A distributed-lag relationship (e.g. rainfall→arbovirus, or care-access→cancer-mortality) is NOT constant across this window; pooling produces a time-average curve that describes no actual period and is dominated by whichever regime has the most variance. Worse, rising reporting completeness imparts a spurious upward trend that (per LDO-LAG-CONF-01) fabricates lagged edges. The MSD never states or tests the stationarity assumption — §II.6.1 simply writes Σ_time as 'temporal smoothness/seasonality', which is a within-series smoothness prior, not a regime-invariance guarantee.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/covariance.py:83-86 (single pooled moment), 191-203 (single G over all t); MSD-II §II.6.1 line 239; no changepoint/regime handling anywhere in lags.py
- **recommendation:** Introduce regime-aware estimation: fit separate Ω_var on pre/post-2015 (or CUSUM/changepoint-detected) windows and REPORT the divergence as a first-class output (a link whose response curve differs across regimes is a finding, not noise to average away). Minimum viable: add a stationarity diagnostic — split-half response-curve agreement across the temporal midpoint — and downgrade edges whose curves disagree, exactly analogous to the existing regularization-path-agreement gate. Model reporting completeness explicitly as a time-varying offset in the count margin so it cannot masquerade as trend.
- **risk:** Response curves are epidemiologically uninterpretable time-averages; the 2015 arbovirus emergence — the exact phenomenon the 'Zika capability' is meant to catch — is smeared into a 15-year baseline and may be undetectable.

**`LDO-LAG-SMOOTH-03` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Temporal-smoothness prior biases the discovered response curve toward smoothness that may not exist (spurious-smoothing trap)
- **detail:** build_smoothness_operator adds γ_t·(L_lag⊗I_p) as a quadratic penalty (1/2)tr(Sᵀ G S) on the precision entries across adjacent lags (lowrank.py:326-347, 139-195). The chain Laplacian L_lag penalizes squared differences between a variable's precision row at lag a and lag a±1, shrinking the response curve toward flatness/smoothness. The orchestrator default is gamma_temporal=0.1 (orchestrator.py:118) — a magic number with no data-driven selection or cross-validation. This directly biases the OUTPUT OF INTEREST: the whole point is to discover the SHAPE of the distributed-lag response, yet a smoothness prior of unknown strength is imposed on that very shape. Real epidemiological responses are often NOT smooth over lags — an incubation-period effect can be a sharp single-lag spike (dengue→death at a specific delay), a threshold, or bimodal (acute + delayed waves). A smoothness prior will broaden a true spike into a smeared bump, shift the argmax peak lag toward the interior, and can even create a fake secondary shoulder by borrowing strength from neighbors. Because it penalizes PRECISION entries (partial correlations), not the marginal response, its epidemiological meaning is opaque — it is not a prior on the response curve the user reads, it is a prior on conditional dependence magnitudes that indirectly deforms that curve.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/lowrank.py:326-347 (L_lag⊗I penalty), 188-210 (prox-gradient S-step with G); orchestrator.py:118 gamma_temporal=0.1 default (unjustified magic number); lags.py:128-133
- **recommendation:** (1) Make γ_t data-driven: select by holdout predictive likelihood or an empirical-Bayes marginal, and REPORT the selected value + sensitivity of each peak_lag to it. (2) Offer a NON-smoothing default (γ_t=0) for response-curve discovery and use smoothness only as an optional reported-separately robustness variant — discovery and shrinkage should not be conflated. (3) Prefer a fused-lasso / total-variation penalty on the lag profile (allows sharp jumps, only penalizes needless wiggle) over an L2 chain Laplacian if temporal regularization is wanted, since epidemiological responses are piecewise-smooth, not globally smooth.
- **risk:** Reported peak lags and response-curve shapes are artifacts of an arbitrary 0.1 smoothing weight; sharp incubation-delay signals are systematically broadened and mis-located.

**`LDO-LAG-ANNUAL-04` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Lag-0 contemporaneous vs lag-k directed is not cleanly separable under annual aggregation; default K=8 means 8 YEARS
- **detail:** GaussianField.resolution defaults to 'year' (margins.py:35) and the national studies run annual panels. Under annual aggregation, any cause→effect delay shorter than 12 months collapses into the SAME year and is therefore indistinguishable from a contemporaneous (lag-0) link — the code excludes lag 0 from the directed peak (lags.py:163,167 'peak over lags 1..K') precisely because lag-0 within-year cannot be oriented, but this means every sub-annual epidemiological mechanism (incubation weeks, acute care within months, most infectious dynamics) is FORCED into the undirected contemporaneous bucket and NO directed curve is recoverable for it. Conversely the fit_lagged_links default K=8 (lags.py:73) on annual data asserts up to an EIGHT-YEAR distributed lag — epidemiologically meaningful for very few exposures (some cancers), absurd for arboviruses. _adaptive_lag_order caps K at T-2 and shrinks with p (orchestrator.py:89-94) but does not reason about the TIME UNIT: it will happily fit K=6 annual lags on a 8-year panel, spending the entire estimand budget on 6-8-year delays that have near-zero prior plausibility while the true sub-annual signal is aliased into lag-0. The MSD's own motivating example ('Zika's 6-9 MONTHS', §III.4(4) line 217) requires MONTHLY resolution, which the default annual run cannot represent at all.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/margins.py:35 resolution='year'; lags.py:73 K=8 default, 163-167 lag-0 excluded; orchestrator.py:89-94 adaptive-K ignores time unit; MSD-III §III.4(4) 'Zika's 6-9 months' presupposes monthly
- **recommendation:** (1) Bind K to the time unit: refuse (or warn) when annual K>3 unless a chronic-exposure prior justifies multi-year lags; the multi-resolution §II.7 fine pass MUST drop to monthly for any lagged-directed claim. (2) Emit an explicit aliasing warning on lag-0 edges under annual aggregation stating the delay is unresolved (≤1yr), and never let a lag-0 edge be silently read as 'no delay'. (3) Report the time unit in every response_curve record so a curve indexed in years is never misread as months.
- **risk:** The system either fabricates implausible multi-year delays or aliases the true fast dynamics into undirected lag-0, and the flagship Zika use-case is structurally impossible at the default resolution.

**`LDO-LAG-OVERLAP-05` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Overlapping lag windows induce mechanical serial correlation and inflate the effective sample size used for pairwise moments
- **detail:** _build_lagged_feature_matrix places the SAME cell Z(s,t) into K+1 different feature blocks (lags.py:48-67): feature (lag=0,t) and feature (lag=1,t+1) are literally identical values. Consequently the pairwise-complete moment for a cross-lag pair counts n_ab overlapping samples (covariance.py:83) that are NOT independent — consecutive constructed samples share K of their K+1 underlying observations. The correlation estimate is still consistent, but its EFFECTIVE sample size is far smaller than n_ab, so any downstream inference that treats n_ab (or coverage) as an independent count — min_coverage=30/min_overlap=20 gates (lags.py:78-79), and implicitly the stability-selection subsampling — is anticonservative. Moreover overlapping windows create a mechanical autocorrelation structure in the feature vector itself: even under i.i.d. Z, the lag-block features are correlated purely by construction near the diagonal, which biases the ADMM covariance and can seed a spurious low-order 'trend' factor in L. The MSD's separable Σ_time is supposed to model this, but the implemented path either whitens only SPACE (spatial_whiten) or does plain pairwise correlation — there is NO temporal whitening / Σ_time^{-1} applied, so the Kronecker temporal factor of §II.6.1 is effectively identity and the serial dependence is unmodeled.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/lags.py:48-67 (shared cells across blocks), 109-117 (only spatial whitening, no Σ_time); covariance.py:83 n_ab overlap counts; MSD-II §II.6.1 line 239 Σ_time^{-1} named but unimplemented
- **recommendation:** (1) Implement the Σ_time^{-1} temporal whitening the Kronecker model promises (an AR/GMRF-on-the-time-chain whitener) so serial correlation is removed before reading precision, OR compute a block-bootstrap / Newey-West-style effective-n and use THAT in the coverage gates and stability weighting. (2) Report an effective-sample-size (design effect) per pairwise moment rather than the raw overlap count so min_coverage/min_overlap thresholds are honest. (3) At minimum, document that Σ_time is identity in the current path — the docstring implies a full separable model that is not built.
- **risk:** Coverage gates pass on far fewer independent observations than claimed; stability-selection frequencies are inflated; a construction-artifact trend factor contaminates the latent_shared attribution.

**`LDO-LAG-PEAKARGMAX-06` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — peak_lag is a bare argmax over noisy lag partial-correlations with no null or uncertainty — systematically biased and overconfident
- **detail:** peak_lag = 1 + argmax_k |partial(k)| over lags 1..K (lags.py:167-168), and peak_partial_correlation is that maximized value. Selecting the maximum of K noisy statistics and reporting both its location AND its magnitude is a textbook winner's-curse: (1) the reported peak_partial_correlation is upward-biased (selection on the max), (2) the argmax location has large variance and, under a flat true response, is uniformly distributed over lags yet will always return SOME peak ≥ edge_threshold if any lag noise exceeds it, (3) there is no confidence interval on the peak lag and no test that the curve is distinguishable from flat. The LaggedLink carries no uncertainty field at all (lags.py:28-34) — the response_curve is a point estimate. MSD-III §III.8 (line 245) mandates 'edge promoted without ... propagated uncertainty' as a standing ABORT, and §II.6.4 line 263 repeats it; the response-curve peak violates this because neither the peak lag nor the curve carries propagated uncertainty at readout (stability selection runs at the edge level in the orchestrator, but the CURVE SHAPE / peak location is never given an interval).
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/lags.py:167-168 (argmax), 28-34 (no uncertainty on LaggedLink/response_curve), 179 peak magnitude reported raw; violates MSD-III §III.8 line 245 & §II.6.4 line 263 uncertainty-abort
- **recommendation:** (1) Bootstrap (or use the stability subsamples already computed) the FULL response curve and report a peak-lag posterior/frequency distribution + a curve-wise band, not a single argmax. (2) Add a 'flat-curve' null: test H0 that all lag partials are equal; only report a peak_lag when the curve is significantly non-flat, else emit 'lag-unresolved'. (3) Debias the reported peak magnitude (e.g. report the curve at a peak selected on held-out folds, or a shrunk estimate) to counter winner's-curse. (4) Populate an uncertainty field on LaggedLink so §III.8 is actually satisfied for the curve.
- **risk:** Every lagged edge ships a confidently precise peak delay that is a selection artifact; the standing §III.8 uncertainty-abort is silently unmet for the response curve.

**`LDO-LAG-IMPUTE0-08` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Missing cells imputed to the Gaussian margin mean (0) under spatial whitening bias lagged correlations toward the null and misrepresent undercount as 'average'
- **detail:** The spatial-whitening path (the default, spatial_whiten=True) cannot honor per-cell missingness and imputes every unobserved cell to 0, the Gaussian margin mean (covariance.py:176, docstring lags.py:100-106, precision.py:90-98). For a rank/PIT-transformed COUNT variable, 0 on the latent scale is the MEDIAN of observed cells, but a municipal cell is missing/zero mainly because of UNDERREPORTING or a genuine structural zero, not because it sits at the median rate. Imputing 0 therefore (1) attenuates every correlation toward the null (added mean-valued noise), disproportionately for sparse variables with many missing cells — exactly the rare-disease/small-municipality regime, and (2) treats systematic undercount (SIM/SINASC completeness varying by region and year) as random-at-the-mean, when it is strongly spatially and temporally structured and correlated with the covariates. This is a Missing-Not-At-Random problem that mean-imputation is invalid for. Note the two code paths DIFFER: pairwise_correlation (spatial_whiten=False) is honestly pairwise-complete, but the DEFAULT whitened path is impute-0 — so turning on spatial whitening silently swaps a principled missing-data estimator for the very impute-0 fabrication covariance.py:1-8 warns against.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/covariance.py:176 impute-0; lags.py:100-106,113-114 default whitened path; contrast with pairwise-complete path lags.py:116-117; MNAR structure ignored
- **recommendation:** (1) Make missingness explicit in the whitened path: whiten only observed cells and carry a per-cell reliability weight w_{j,s,t} (the §3.12 n_eff the MSD already defines at margins §II.6.1 line 235 — currently unused here), or use a sparse whitener restricted to the observed subgraph. (2) Model undercount as a time/region-varying reporting offset in the count margin rather than imputing its effect to the mean. (3) At minimum, report per-variable missing-fraction and flag whitened edges whose variables exceed a missingness threshold as attenuation-suspect; do not present impute-0 whitened correlations as equivalent to pairwise-complete.
- **risk:** Rare-disease and small-municipality lagged edges are systematically attenuated to non-detection, and structured undercount contaminates the surviving edges — an equity-relevant bias against exactly the underreported populations the study targets.

**`LDO-LAG-NEARCORR-07` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — nearest-correlation PSD projection (eigen-clip floor 1e-3) silently distorts the very off-diagonal partial correlations being reported
- **detail:** Because pairwise-complete correlation matrices are generally indefinite, _nearest_correlation clips eigenvalues at floor=1e-3 and renormalizes (covariance.py:38-45). This Frobenius-nearest PSD projection is standard for GETTING a valid input, but here the projected matrix is fed directly into the precision solve whose off-diagonals become the reported partial correlations and response curves. Eigenvalue clipping is a GLOBAL operation: it perturbs every entry, including the specific cross-lag off-diagonals that constitute the scientific claim, by an amount that depends on how indefinite the pairwise matrix was (i.e. on the missingness pattern, not the biology). With sparse municipal panels the pairwise matrix can be substantially indefinite, so the correction is not negligible. The floor 1e-3 is a magic number: too high and it inflates small eigenvalues (over-shrinking partial correlations toward 0, hiding weak-but-real lagged edges); too low and the subsequent logdet/precision is near-singular. No diagnostic reports how far the projection moved the entries, so a response curve could be materially a projection artifact with no flag.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/covariance.py:38-45 (floor=1e-3 eigen-clip), 105/218 fed to precision; partial correlations read off projected matrix in lags.py:144-156
- **recommendation:** (1) Prefer Higham's alternating-projections nearest-correlation (which preserves unit diagonal exactly and minimizes distortion) over one-shot eigen-clip, or better, estimate the precision directly under missingness (EM / graphical-lasso with a missing-data likelihood) so no ad-hoc PSD repair is needed. (2) Report the projection distance ||C_proj − C||_F and the min eigenvalue pre-projection as a data-quality diagnostic, and downgrade edges when the projection moved their entry by more than a threshold. (3) Justify or cross-validate the 1e-3 floor.
- **risk:** Weak real lagged edges are erased and phantom edges introduced by a numerical repair whose magnitude tracks missingness rather than epidemiology, with no visibility into when it dominates.

**`LDO-LAG-GAUSSCOUNT-09` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — Gaussian-copula partial-correlation machinery on sparse over-dispersed counts; no negative-binomial dispersion, ties break PIT monotonicity
- **detail:** The whole lag operator reads DIRECTED effects off linear partial correlations of the Gaussianized field Z (lags.py:144-156). The copula margin (§III.5) PIT-transforms counts to Gaussian, but municipal disease counts are (a) heavily zero-inflated and tied — many cells share count=0 or 1, so the rank/PIT map is far from a continuous quantile transform and the induced 'Gaussian' variable has atoms; a linear correlation of atom-laden variables is a weak, biased summary of dependence and its partial-correlation interpretation as a Gaussian graphical edge is approximate at best. (b) over-dispersed (variance>>mean), so a Poisson-style margin under-states tail variance; the MSD names NB/beta-binomial families but the lag path just consumes whatever Z the field produced with no check that dispersion was modeled. The linear-Gaussian precision cannot represent threshold/saturating dose-response (common in epi: effect only above a rainfall or coverage threshold) — those land only in the HSIC residual scan, which is a separate undirected detector, so a NONLINEAR distributed-lag response is invisible to the response-curve machinery entirely.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/lags.py:144-156 linear partial corr; MSD-III §III.5 line 222 names NB/beta-binomial but lag path is family-agnostic; ties/zero-inflation unaddressed in PIT
- **recommendation:** (1) Verify (and gate on) NB/beta-binomial margins with estimated dispersion for count variables feeding the lag fit; refuse a Poisson margin when overdispersion is detected. (2) For heavily-tied variables, use a rank-based dependence (Kendall/Spearman-derived latent correlation for ordinal/atom margins, e.g. the polychoric/latent-Gaussian estimator) rather than Pearson on jittered ranks. (3) Explicitly route lagged NONLINEAR responses: run the HSIC residual scan on LAGGED residual pairs (§II.6.3 mentions lagged-residual pairs) and surface a nonlinear-lag flag on the response curve, so a threshold delay is not silently reported as a null linear edge.
- **risk:** Zero-inflated, over-dispersed, or threshold-shaped lagged relationships — the norm for arboviral and small-area count data — are mis-summarized or missed by the linear-Gaussian curve.

**`LDO-LAG-KRONSEP-10` · MEDIUM · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Kronecker separability Prec=Ω_var⊗Σ_space^{-1}⊗Σ_time^{-1} is assumed but the space/var interaction is exactly where lagged-spatial epidemic spread lives
- **detail:** MSD-II §II.6.1 (line 238) and §III.4(3) posit a separable Kronecker precision; the implementation realizes it by whitening space FIRST (Σ_space^{-1/2}) then estimating Ω_var on the whitened field (lags.py:109-114). Separability asserts that the variable-dependency structure is IDENTICAL in every municipality and that spatial correlation is the SAME for every variable — both false for epidemic processes. Spatial spread of an arbovirus is itself a lagged-spatial phenomenon: cases in municipality s at t depend on neighbors at t−1 (a space×time×var interaction), which a Kronecker product of separate space, time, and var factors CANNOT represent — it is precisely the non-separable term. By whitening space out globally before reading lags, the estimator REMOVES the spatial-diffusion signal and then reports the residual variable lags as if space were a nuisance, when for communicable disease the spatial lag IS the mechanism. §III.4(3) admits separability is 'an approximation (audited by 6)' but the audit (Kronecker-separability diagnostic, §II.6.4) is not invoked in the lag path here.
- **evidence:** MSD-II §II.6.1 line 238, §III.4(3) line 216; src/pegasus/ldo/lags.py:109-114 global spatial pre-whiten then var-lag readout; no separability audit called in lags.py
- **recommendation:** (1) Report a separability-audit residual for the lag fit (compare full vs Kronecker-approximated covariance on a slice) and downgrade lagged edges when non-separability is large — wire the §II.6.4 diagnostic into fit_lagged_links. (2) For communicable-disease variables, add an explicit spatial-lag feature (neighbor-average at t−1) as a first-class predictor so spatial diffusion is a NAMED term rather than whitened-away or mis-attributed to a same-municipality variable lag. (3) Consider an SPDE/INLA space-time model (Lindgren-Rue) as a principled non-separable alternative to the Kronecker+whiten shortcut.
- **risk:** For exactly the communicable diseases the lag capability targets, the causal spatial-diffusion pathway is whitened out and its signal may be mis-attributed to a spurious within-municipality variable lag.

**`LDO-LAG-KAPPA-11` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — Spatial whitening degrades to a no-op κ-scaling for municipalities absent from the adjacency, silently mixing whitened and unwhitened variables
- **detail:** build_spatial_precision_sparse builds L_W only over municipalities present in the structural adjacency; the docstring concedes that where 'the field's municipalities are absent from the adjacency the Laplacian term is empty and whitening reduces to a κ-scaling (a no-op at κ=1)' (lags.py:104-106, precision.py:62-78). With κ defaulting to 1.0 (lags.py:74), any municipality not in the adjacency (new/split munis post-2000, boundary-change AMCs, coding errors, territories) is passed through UNWHITENED while its neighbors are whitened. The resulting Ω_var is then estimated from a field that is spatially whitened in some rows and raw in others — an inconsistent metric. Because boundary changes over 2000-2024 are exactly the AMC harmonization problem tracked elsewhere in this project, a non-trivial set of muni-years will fall through. There is no warning or count of how many cells got the no-op path, so a run can be substantially unwhitened (spatial autocorrelation NOT removed — the 'dominant municipal-scale confounder' per lags.py:101-104) while reporting spatial_whiten=True.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/lags.py:104-106 no-op admission, 74 kappa default 1.0; precision.py:68-78 neighbours filtered to idx, absent munis get κ-only diagonal; no coverage warning emitted
- **recommendation:** (1) Emit a hard diagnostic: count and fraction of muni-cells that received the κ-only no-op whitening; refuse or loudly warn (per §II.10 'never silently...') when it exceeds a small threshold. (2) Harmonize municipalities to the adjacency's vintage (reuse the AMC boundary-change harmonization) BEFORE whitening so no cell is orphaned. (3) Set κ>1 or fall back to a k-NN geographic adjacency for orphan munis rather than leaving them unwhitened, so the spatial metric is consistent across all rows.
- **risk:** Runs silently leave spatial autocorrelation in a subset of municipalities, reintroducing the exact confounder whitening was added to remove, with no signal to the analyst that whitening was partial.

**`LDO-LAG-MULTTEST-12` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — Lag extension multiplies the hypothesis space by (K+1)² with only a fixed edge_threshold=0.05 and no FDR at the curve level
- **detail:** Extending p variables to p·(K+1) features expands the number of candidate ordered pairs from ~p² to ~p²·K for lagged edges (every (i lag k → j lag 0) is a test). The readout keeps any edge whose peak |partial| ≥ edge_threshold=0.05 (lags.py:73,170,189) — a fixed magnitude cutoff, not an error-rate-controlled selection, and 0.05 is a magic number that is a CORRELATION, not a p-value. The MSD's stated multiplicity strategy is stability selection (§II.6.3, §III.7), which the orchestrator does run at the edge level, but the LAG dimension adds a within-edge multiple-comparison across K lags (the argmax of LDO-LAG-PEAKARGMAX-06) that stability selection over subsamples does not price: an edge can be 'stable' merely because the SAME noisy lag wins in most subsamples when variables are serially correlated. There is a dedicated fdr.py in the package but it is not applied to the lag-curve peak selection here.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/lags.py:73 edge_threshold=0.05 (magic), 170,189 fixed-threshold selection; multiplicity across K lags not FDR-controlled; fdr.py present but uninvoked in lag readout
- **recommendation:** (1) Replace the fixed 0.05 correlation threshold with an error-rate-calibrated cutoff (stability-selection frequency threshold tied to a per-family-error bound, or an FDR on curve-level test statistics) that accounts for the K-fold within-edge search. (2) Report the expected number of false edges given p, K, and the threshold. (3) Wire the existing fdr.py into the lagged-edge selection, treating each (i,j) response curve as a single family with a max-statistic null (calibrated by the flat-curve permutation from LDO-LAG-PEAKARGMAX-06).
- **risk:** At national p and K>1 the lagged-edge set contains an uncontrolled and unreported number of false positives; the 0.05 cutoff has no error-rate meaning.

**`LDO-LAG-NEFF-13` · MEDIUM · implementation_gap** — Per-cell reliability weights w_{j,s,t} (n_eff/fragility) that MSD-II mandates are defined but never used in the lag covariance
- **detail:** MSD-II §II.6.1 (line 235) states the §3.12 state tensor supplies per-cell reliability weights w_{j,s,t} for the LDO, and §III.8/§II.6.4 make n_eff floors a certification gate. But both covariance estimators in this subsystem — pairwise_correlation and whitened_lagged_correlation — compute UNWEIGHTED moments: every observed cell contributes equally regardless of its n_eff or fragility (covariance.py:83-100, 188-203). A municipality-year built from a tiny denominator (huge sampling variance) counts the same as a large stable one. This means (a) the correlations are driven partly by high-variance small cells, and (b) the mandated reliability weighting is simply absent from the code path, so the certification claim that edges respect n_eff floors is unmet at the estimation stage (it may be checked post-hoc, but the ESTIMATE itself is unweighted). For a distributed-lag curve, unstable small-denominator cells inject noise that the argmax then selects on.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/covariance.py:83-100,188-203 unweighted moments; MSD-II §II.6.1 line 235 mandates w_{j,s,t}; no weight argument threaded from field into either estimator
- **recommendation:** Thread the per-cell reliability weight into both moment accumulators (a weighted pairwise-complete covariance: replace the mask M with the weight matrix W so n_ab, Sx, Cxy become weighted sums), so unreliable cells are downweighted at estimation, not just gated after. This is a small change (the moment algebra already multiplies by a 0/1 mask; use w in [0,1] instead) and directly realizes the MSD's stated weighting.
- **risk:** High-variance small-denominator municipality-years dominate the response curve; the MSD's reliability-weighting requirement is unimplemented at the point it matters.


### subgroup sensitivity screen (thresholds)

**`LDO-EXH-01` · CRITICAL · statistical_validity_risk** — Screen thresholds 0.15/0.30 are arbitrary magic numbers with no false-negative guarantee
- **detail:** MSD-III §VIII.2(1) claims screening on heterogeneity/max-subgroup 'converts the coarse pass from a test (with false negatives) into a sensitive filter tuned for recall'. The implementation (exhaustiveness.py should_drill_down, het_threshold=0.15, max_threshold=0.30) realizes 'tuned for recall' as two hardcoded constants compared against std / max|.| of a vector of per-unit Pearson correlations. There is NO calibration: the thresholds are not derived from a target false-negative rate, not from a null distribution of the screen statistics, not from the per-unit sample size (T), and not from the number of subgroups (S). A 'recall-tuned filter' that bounds false negatives must, by definition, set its cut so that P(screen fires | true effect of size delta) >= 1 - beta for a stated delta and beta. Here beta is undefined and unbounded. A genuine cancellation edge whose per-unit correlations are +/-0.12 (real but modest heterogeneity, std ~0.12 < 0.15) is pruned; a pure-noise pair with S=27 units and T=15 years has std of per-unit correlations ~1/sqrt(T-3) ~0.28, which OFTEN exceeds 0.15, firing the screen. So the same threshold is simultaneously too high to guarantee recall on real weak-heterogeneity edges and too low to control false positives. The MSD's 'sensitive filter (tuned for recall)' language is an unfounded claim: nothing in the math bounds recall.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §VIII.2(1) lines 378-380; src/pegasus/ldo/exhaustiveness.py:46-62 (should_drill_down), resolution.py:112-114 defaults
- **recommendation:** Replace fixed thresholds with a calibrated cut: (a) build a within-panel null for each screen statistic by permuting time within unit (destroys the i-j co-movement but preserves marginal variance and T), (b) set the threshold at a chosen null quantile (e.g. 95th) so the per-pair false-positive rate is controlled, then (c) state the achieved recall for a menu of planted effect sizes from the §IX.2 synthetic battery. If recall is the objective, expose beta and solve for the threshold that achieves it at a stated minimum detectable heterogeneity, and record BOTH the threshold and the delta/beta it guarantees in the coverage manifest.
- **risk:** The central §VIII.2 scientific claim (bounded false negatives) is asserted but not delivered; drilled/pruned decisions are governed by two constants with no statistical meaning, so 'bounded exhaustiveness' is a label, not a guarantee.


### subgroup effect vectors

**`LDO-EXH-02` · CRITICAL · computational_trap** — Per-unit correlation over T=26 yearly points is a high-variance estimate; its std is dominated by sampling noise, not true heterogeneity
- **detail:** _subgroup_effect_vectors (resolution.py:91-109) computes, for every variable pair (i,j) and every spatial unit s, the within-unit Pearson correlation over the T time points: eff[i,j,s] = sum_t Zs[i,s,t]*Zs[j,s,t] / (T-1). The flagship national run is municipality x YEAR (investigate.py:277 gates on resolution!='year'), so T is the number of years (~15-26, often far fewer after availability windows). The heterogeneity_screen then takes std over s of these correlations. Under the null (no true link), each per-unit correlation r_s has sampling std ~ 1/sqrt(T-3); across S units the std of the r_s vector is therefore ~1/sqrt(T-3) PURELY FROM SAMPLING, independent of any real heterogeneity. With T=15 that null std is ~0.29 -- nearly double the 0.15 threshold. So on a dense socioeconomic field the heterogeneity screen fires on essentially every pair from noise alone, making the 'filter' vacuous (drills everything -> defeats the compute purpose) OR, if operators raise the threshold to compensate, silently destroys recall. Worse, the correlation is computed even for units with 1-2 finite time points (max(T-1,1) floor and std>1e-9 gate only guard divide-by-zero, not degrees of freedom), so units with T_s=2 contribute deterministic +/-1 correlations that inflate std without carrying information.
- **evidence:** resolution.py:100-109 (eff = einsum/(T-1)); investigate.py:277 (municipality x year is the flagship grain); exhaustiveness.py:25-29 (std with no df correction)
- **recommendation:** Do not use raw per-unit correlation std as the heterogeneity statistic. Use a variance-components / random-effects heterogeneity measure that separates true between-unit dispersion from sampling variance: fit r_s ~ N(mu, tau^2 + se_s^2) with se_s^2 = 1/(T_s-3) (Fisher-z) and screen on the estimated tau^2 (or Cochran's Q / I^2), which is null-centered at zero regardless of T. Require a minimum effective T_s per unit before that unit enters the screen, and record the excluded units in the manifest. This is standard meta-analytic heterogeneity estimation and gives the screen a defensible null.
- **risk:** On the actual national yearly panel the screen statistic is swamped by 1/sqrt(T) sampling noise, so the whole subgroup limb is either always-on (no pruning) or must be detuned into uselessness -- either way the §VIII.2(1) sensitivity claim fails on the exact data it is meant to protect.


### random deep audit (FNR estimator)

**`LDO-EXH-03` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — False-negative-rate estimator is a bare point estimate with no Wilson CI, no sample-size justification, and a mismatched denominator
- **detail:** _random_deep_audit (resolution.py:163-188) samples n_audit_vars (default 8) pruned VARIABLES, runs the fine LDO on them, counts selected edges, and reports false_negative_rate = n_selected / (k*(k-1)/2). Three defects: (1) No confidence interval. The estimand is a proportion from a tiny sample (k=8 vars -> 28 pairs, but only ~sqrt drawn independently); a point estimate of 0/28=0.0 is reported as if the FNR were zero, when the exact 95% upper bound (rule of three) is ~3/28 ~ 0.11. MSD §VIII.2(2) says 'measure the false-negative rate empirically' -- a measurement without an interval is not a measurement. (2) Denominator mismatch: the numerator counts selected edges AMONG the sampled pruned variables (pairs where both endpoints are pruned), but the false-negative rate the screen needs is over the pruned PAIRS from the full coarse pass, which also includes mixed pairs (one pruned endpoint, one candidate endpoint). Restricting to pruned-pruned pairs biases the FNR estimate (these are the least likely to contain a real edge, since a pair with any strong endpoint tends to be a candidate) -> systematic UNDER-estimate of the true miss rate. (3) n_audit_vars=8 is a fixed magic number unconnected to the number of pruned variables (could be thousands nationally), so the fraction audited -> 0 at scale and the estimate has no power to detect a small but nonzero FNR.
- **evidence:** resolution.py:170-188; MSD-III §VIII.2(2) line 381; report field false_negative_rate at 186-187
- **recommendation:** Report a Wilson (or Clopper-Pearson) interval on the audited FNR and surface the UPPER bound as the honest miss-rate ceiling; size the audit sample to achieve a target margin (n from Wilson for a desired half-width at the observed rate), not a constant 8. Sample pruned PAIRS (including mixed candidate/pruned pairs) rather than pruned variables so the estimand matches the screen's actual decision boundary. Record n_audited, n_selected, and the CI in the coverage manifest, and refuse to report FNR=0 as a bare number.
- **risk:** The one empirical check that could validate the sparsity-of-truth assumption reports a biased, interval-free point estimate that will almost always read 0.0, giving false confidence that the screen misses nothing while the true upper bound could be 10%+.


### coarse->fine refit

**`LDO-EXH-04` · CRITICAL · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Coarse->fine is a naive REFIT, not the multiresolution-posterior readout §III.3 mandates, reintroducing the Simpson/cancellation blind spot §VIII.2 claims to avoid
- **detail:** MSD-III §III.3 (line 208) is explicit: 'The coarse->fine scanner is therefore a READOUT of the multiresolution posterior, not a heuristic prune: unsupported fine detail is shrunk into its parent, not dropped.' The implementation does the opposite. coarsen_field_spatial (resolution.py:51-85) POOLS municipalities into UF cells by reliability-weighted mean, then run_ldo is fit on the pooled coarse field, and only candidate variables are refit at fine grain (run_multiresolution_ldo:205-236). This is two independent graphical-lasso fits joined by a variable filter -- a heuristic prune -- with NO joint hierarchical prior linking coarse to fine, NO shrinkage of fine deviations toward the coarse parent, and NO SPDE/GMRF that would let coarse and fine coexist. Consequently the very Simpson/cancellation failure §VIII.1 warns about is reintroduced AT THE POOLING STEP: taking a reliability-weighted MEAN across a UF's municipalities is exactly the aggregation that cancels opposite-signed municipal effects and reverses Simpson pairs. The subgroup screen (LDO-EXH-01/02) is a bolt-on patch that tries to detect this AFTER the damaging aggregation, rather than the posterior-readout architecture that would never have aggregated the signal away. The two passes are also never checked for consistency (the docstring at resolution.py:9-12 promises 'a candidate edge present coarse should survive fine' but nothing verifies it).
- **evidence:** MSD-III §III.3 line 208; resolution.py:51-85 (coarsen by mean), 191-236 (two independent run_ldo calls); orchestrator.py:530-532
- **recommendation:** Realize §III.3 as specified: a single latent-Gaussian GMRF/BYM model with hierarchical space components (national+region+state+municipal deviations) fit once, from which the coarse and fine views are POSTERIOR READOUTS (INLA/SPDE, e.g. R-INLA or a torch SPDE precision). Fine detail unsupported by data is then shrunk to the parent, not dropped, and there is no lossy pooling step to reverse. If a full SPDE fit is out of envelope, at minimum add a coarse<->fine consistency gate (every coarse candidate must reappear at fine at a compatible sign/lag, and every fine-only strong edge is flagged) and DOWNGRADE the naive-refit path to descriptive, not a §VIII.2-compliant scanner.
- **risk:** The system claims §VIII.2 compliance (no Simpson blind spot) while its aggregation step is precisely the Simpson-inducing operation; determinant edges that reverse or cancel across a state's municipalities are lost before the screen can see them, and the coarse/fine graphs can silently disagree.


### sparsity-of-truth assumption

**`LDO-EXH-05` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Sparsity-of-truth prior is indefensible for a dense socioeconomic-determinant field and is stated but never tested against the actual edge density
- **detail:** CoverageManifest.sparsity_of_truth_assumption (exhaustiveness.py:71-73; coverage.py:33-36) hardcodes 'most fine-grained links are null'. MSD §VIII.2 line 384 requires this be STATED (done) but the whole bounded-exhaustiveness guarantee RESTS on it being true. For the flagship study -- national pancreatic-cancer socioeconomic DETERMINANTS -- the field is dense by construction: income, education, sanitation, urbanization, health-system access, age structure, and mortality are mutually correlated through shared latent development gradients. In a development-gradient field the marginal (and even many partial) associations are pervasively nonzero; sparsity holds only AFTER the low-rank shared-driver removal (§III.4(2) Chandrasekaran-Parrilo-Willsky), which the coarse screen and audit do NOT perform on the raw subgroup correlations. So the screen operates on a field where the null 'most pairs null' is false, the low signal-to-null-density ratio destroys any recall bound, and the audit's FNR denominator assumption is violated. The assumption is asserted as a string, never checked against the observed selected-edge fraction.
- **evidence:** exhaustiveness.py:71-73; coverage.py:31-36; MSD-III §VIII.2 line 384; study config national_pancreatic_c25_determinants.json (dense socioeconomic determinants)
- **recommendation:** Replace the unconditional sparsity assumption with a CONDITIONAL one: sparsity of DIRECT edges after latent-factor deflation (the low-rank part of §III.4 must run BEFORE the subgroup screen so the screen sees residualized signal). Empirically test the assumption per run: report the observed selected-edge density and, if it exceeds a threshold (dense regime), emit a typed warning that the recall bound is void and switch to a global multiple-testing regime (BH-FDR / knockoffs) rather than a sparsity-reliant prune. State the assumption as a checked claim with the measured density, not a constant string.
- **risk:** On exactly the dense determinant fields the project targets, the foundational assumption is false, so 'unsearched regions are assumed edge-free' silently drops real determinant links and the honesty manifest certifies a guarantee that does not hold.


### pooled-signal union limb

**`LDO-EXH-06` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — Pooled-signal drill limb reuses weight_floor=0.05 and 'any stability>0' as an OR-gate, coupling the screen to uncalibrated thresholds and stability floor
- **detail:** _sensitivity_screen limb (b) (resolution.py:145-160) unions the subgroup drills with any coarse record where certification_status=='selected' OR |weight|>=0.05 OR stability>0.0. Three issues: (1) weight_floor=0.05 is another magic number on the precision-weight scale, with no relation to the coarse pass's own regularization strength lambda1 (a different lambda1 changes the weight scale, making 0.05 mean different things run to run). (2) 'stability>0.0' means ANY nonzero stability-selection recurrence -- i.e. an edge selected in even ONE subsample out of many -- promotes to a candidate. Since stability_select typically records small positive frequencies for many noise edges, this makes limb (b) fire extremely broadly, largely negating the compute savings and coupling candidate selection to the raw (uncalibrated-here) stability frequency rather than the certified stability_threshold (0.6 in run_ldo). (3) The three OR-clauses are redundant with certification_status=='selected' already encoding the calibrated gate, so the extra two clauses only ADD false-positive drills without a recall benefit that the subgroup limb doesn't already give.
- **evidence:** resolution.py:114 (weight_floor=0.05), 149-153 (pooled OR-gate); orchestrator.py:107 (stability_threshold=0.6 -- the real gate, not >0)
- **recommendation:** Drop the raw |weight|>=floor and stability>0 clauses; union only on certification_status=='selected' (the calibrated coarse decision) plus the subgroup limb. If a softer recall net is wanted, gate on stability >= (a fraction of) the certified stability_threshold, and express any weight floor as a multiple of the fitted noise scale, not an absolute constant. Document each clause's contribution to the drilled-pair count in diagnostics so its recall/cost tradeoff is visible.
- **risk:** Candidate selection is driven partly by two uncalibrated thresholds that either bloat compute (drilling near-noise edges) or vary meaninglessly with lambda1, undermining both the envelope-sizing rationale and the reproducibility of which pairs get searched.


### screen multiplicity

**`LDO-EXH-07` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Per-pair screen with no multiple-testing control over the O(p^2) pair grid
- **detail:** The subgroup screen loops over every unordered variable pair (resolution.py:135-143) and fires independently per pair at a fixed threshold. With p variables there are p(p-1)/2 pairs; for a national determinant panel p can be hundreds, so tens of thousands of pair-level screen decisions are made with NO family-wise or FDR control on the screen itself. Even if each pair's per-comparison false-positive rate were controlled (it is not -- LDO-EXH-01), the EXPECTED number of noise pairs firing scales with p^2. This is the classic multiple-comparisons trap the MSD explicitly invokes stability selection to avoid at the ESTIMATION layer (§III.7 'controls the edge-set error without per-pair testing'), but the exhaustiveness SCREEN reintroduces exactly the per-pair testing the MSD retired -- with worse (uncalibrated) thresholds. A recall-tuned filter can tolerate high per-pair FPR, but then it drills nearly everything (compute) and the AUDIT (LDO-EXH-03) is the only thing bounding misses -- and that audit is itself broken.
- **evidence:** resolution.py:133-143 (per-pair loop, per-pair threshold); MSD-III §III.7 line 230 (stability selection avoids per-pair testing)
- **recommendation:** Treat the screen as a multiple-testing problem: compute a screen statistic per pair, convert to a p-value against the within-unit permutation null (LDO-EXH-01), and apply Benjamini-Yekutieli (dependence-robust FDR) or a knockoff filter over the pair grid to set the drill set with a controlled expected proportion of noise drills. This gives the 'bounded' in bounded-exhaustiveness an actual bound and lets the compute budget be reasoned about (expected drill count = FDR target * true-edge count + controlled noise).
- **risk:** At national p the screen's false-drill count grows quadratically with no bound, so either compute explodes or the manifest's claimed coverage is illusory; the MSD's own anti-multiplicity principle is violated by its exhaustiveness layer.


### coarsen_field_spatial

**`LDO-EXH-08` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Reliability-weighted MEAN pooling ignores population denominators, so coarse cells are dominated by tiny municipalities and the epidemiological signal is distorted
- **detail:** coarsen_field_spatial (resolution.py:73-81) pools municipalities into a UF cell as a reliability-weighted MEAN of per-municipality VALUES, with weights = the LDO reliability W (from n_eff/Q-tensor), NOT population/exposure. For extensive epidemiological quantities (rates, counts-per-capita) the correct aggregation to a coarse rate is a POPULATION-WEIGHTED (denominator-weighted) mean: sum(cases)/sum(pop). A reliability-weighted mean of municipal RATES gives every municipality's rate comparable influence regardless of its population, so a UF's coarse pancreatic-mortality signal is dominated by the many tiny-population interior municipalities (high-variance, often zero-count rates) rather than the few large urban centers where most cases occur. This both (a) injects noise (small-count rate instability) into the coarse discovery pass and (b) violates the denominator principle the MSD elevates elsewhere (§III.5 count-with-exposure margin, §II.4). The coarse graph is thus fit on a demographically distorted field, biasing which edges become candidates.
- **evidence:** resolution.py:73-81 (Wc uses reliability W, num/wsum is a value-mean); contrast MSD-III §III.5 line 222 count-with-exposure / denominator principle
- **recommendation:** Coarsen on the count-with-exposure representation, not the value-mean: aggregate numerator and denominator separately (sum cases, sum population) and form the coarse rate as their ratio, or pool on the Gaussianized latent with population as the observation weight. Reserve reliability-weighting for a SECOND multiplicative factor on top of the population weight. For intensive quantities (already per-capita socioeconomic indices) a population-weighted mean is still generally correct; document the per-variable aggregation rule (extensive vs intensive) as the §O7 routing gate already distinguishes them.
- **risk:** The coarse discovery pass -- which decides what gets searched at all -- is fit on a small-municipality-dominated, denominator-blind field, systematically distorting candidate selection toward noise in low-population areas and away from the urban centers carrying the actual case load.


### coarsen_field_spatial (spatial prefix)

**`LDO-EXH-09` · MEDIUM · epidemiological_risk** — cod6-prefix pooling assumes stable municipality codes and ignores boundary changes / spatial autocorrelation
- **detail:** coarsen_field_spatial groups municipalities by the first `level` digits of the cod6 id (resolution.py:60-63): level=2 -> UF, level=1 -> macroregion. Two epidemiological problems. (1) Boundary changes: Brazilian municipal codes and boundaries change across the 2000-2025 window (splits/merges, the AMC harmonization the project handles elsewhere via FAL-POP-AMC). Prefix-grouping a raw cod6 assumes a fixed code universe; a municipality that split mid-panel appears as inconsistent membership, and any code remapping done upstream is not guaranteed to be reflected here. (2) Administrative pooling ignores spatial autocorrelation: UF/macroregion boundaries are administrative, not epidemiological. Cancellation across a UF (the §VIII.1 hazard) is MORE likely precisely because a UF spans heterogeneous health regions; pooling to administrative units maximizes within-cell heterogeneity that the mean then cancels. The MSD's own §V.2 spatial GMRF (adjacency, ~6 neighbors/row) is the correct spatial structure and is ignored by the coarsening.
- **evidence:** resolution.py:60-63 (prefix grouping); memory FAL-POP-AMC (boundary-change harmonization exists elsewhere but is not applied here); MSD-III §V.2 line 274 (spatial GMRF adjacency)
- **recommendation:** (1) Pool on the harmonized AMC code space, not raw cod6, and assert the code universe is the same one the population tensor/spatial graph use. (2) Prefer a coarsening that respects the spatial GMRF (graph-coarsening / multigrid restriction on L_W, or health-region rather than UF cells) so coarse cells are spatially coherent and minimize cancellation-inducing within-cell heterogeneity. At minimum, record the administrative-pooling choice as a typed unsearched-structure caveat.
- **risk:** Coarse cells straddle boundary changes and heterogeneous health regions, both inflating within-cell cancellation (the exact failure mode §VIII.1 targets) and risking code-universe mismatches with the harmonized foundational assets.


### screen statistics vs edge_type

**`LDO-EXH-10` · HIGH · implementation_gap** — Subgroup screen uses raw bivariate correlation, not the LDO's conditional/latent-controlled effect, so it screens a different quantity than the engine estimates
- **detail:** The subgroup effect vector (resolution.py:108) is a raw pairwise correlation of two standardized variables within a unit -- a MARGINAL association. But the LDO's actual discovered object is the PARTIAL (conditional, latent-controlled, lagged) precision entry (§III.4: sparse+low-rank, §III.7 partial_correlation). The screen therefore fires (or fails to fire) on a quantity the engine does not estimate. Consequences: (1) A pair with high marginal correlation heterogeneity that is entirely explained by a shared latent driver (the epidemic wave / development gradient -- §III.4(2)) will be drilled as a 'sensitive' candidate, wasting the fine pass on a spurious-once-deconfounded edge. (2) Conversely, a pair with a real CONDITIONAL heterogeneous effect but low marginal correlation (suppression) is not screened and is pruned -- a true false negative the audit is too weak to catch. The screen is also lag-blind: it correlates contemporaneous standardized series, so a purely LAGGED heterogeneous effect (the distributed-lag links §III.4(4) that are the LDO's headline capability, e.g. Zika 6-9mo) has near-zero contemporaneous correlation and is not screened at all.
- **evidence:** resolution.py:100-109 (contemporaneous marginal correlation); MSD-III §III.4 line 215 (sparse+low-rank partial), §III.4(4) line 217 (lagged directed), §III.7 partial_correlation field
- **recommendation:** Screen on a statistic aligned with the estimand: compute the per-unit PARTIAL correlation after projecting out the estimated low-rank shared factors (deflate by the coarse pass's latent loadings before forming eff), and add a LAGGED screen limb (per-unit cross-correlation at each candidate lag k, max over k) so lagged-only heterogeneous effects are eligible. If full per-unit partials are too costly, at least deflate the shared component and note the contemporaneous-only limitation as a typed unsearched region (lagged-heterogeneity not screened).
- **risk:** The screen systematically drills latent-confounded marginal edges (wasting the fine budget) while pruning real conditional and lagged heterogeneous effects -- including the distributed-lag links that are the LDO's flagship discovery -- so the exhaustiveness layer's recall is misaligned with the engine's actual output.


### dispersion_screen (dead / uncalibrated)

**`LDO-EXH-11` · MEDIUM · implementation_gap** — dispersion_screen (index of dispersion) is defined and spec-named but never wired into the drill decision
- **detail:** MSD-III §VIII.2(1) lists THREE sensitivity statistics: heterogeneity, DISPERSION, and max-subgroup. exhaustiveness.py implements dispersion_screen (var/mean index of dispersion, line 39-43) but should_drill_down (line 46-62) and _sensitivity_screen (resolution.py:137-138) only ever consult heterogeneity and max_subgroup -- dispersion_screen has zero callers in the drill path. So one of the three normative screens is orphaned. This matters epidemiologically: the index of dispersion is the statistic that catches THRESHOLD effects and overdispersed CLUSTERING (§VIII.1 'thresholds'), which the correlation-based heterogeneity/max limbs do NOT detect (a threshold effect can have modest correlation but strong overdispersion). Additionally, index-of-dispersion = var/mean is calibrated for COUNTS (Poisson reference =1); applying it to the Gaussianized/standardized field (mean ~0) is undefined (division by ~0). So even if wired, it would need the raw-count representation.
- **evidence:** exhaustiveness.py:39-43 (dispersion_screen), 46-62 (should_drill_down ignores it); resolution.py:137-138 (only het+max); MSD-III §VIII.2(1) line 380 lists dispersion
- **recommendation:** Either wire a dispersion limb into should_drill_down computed on the RAW count field (with a Poisson/NB reference, e.g. flag index-of-dispersion significantly >1 via a chi-square or NB-overdispersion test), giving genuine threshold/clustering sensitivity, OR remove dispersion_screen and record in the manifest that threshold-type effects are an unsearched functional form. Do not leave a spec-named screen defined-but-dead.
- **risk:** A normative third of the §VIII.2(1) sensitivity screen is silently inert, so threshold and overdispersed-clustering effects (a named §VIII.1 hazard) are neither screened nor disclosed as unsearched -- an undisclosed coverage gap.


### audit exception swallowing

**`LDO-EXH-12` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — Audit and screen swallow all exceptions to None, so a broken audit reports 'no false negatives measured' indistinguishably from 'FNR truly low'
- **detail:** _random_deep_audit wraps the fine LDO in a bare except Exception (resolution.py:176-180) returning false_negative_rate=None with only a type-name string. The manifest then stores random_audit_false_negative_rate=None (resolution.py:252). Downstream, None is easily rendered as 'not measured' / 'n/a' and read by a consumer as reassurance. But None here can mean (a) too few pruned vars to audit, (b) the audit LDO CRASHED, or (c) it was never run because there were no candidates (early return at resolution.py:226-232 never sets the audit at all). These are epidemiologically very different: (b) is a validity FAILURE that should widen uncertainty / block the compliance claim, not a benign 'n/a'. The §VIII.2(2) audit is the ONLY empirical check on the sparsity assumption; if it silently fails, the entire bounded-exhaustiveness guarantee is unverified yet the run still ships as §VIII.2-compliant.
- **evidence:** resolution.py:176-180 (bare except -> None), 226-232 (no-candidate early return sets no audit), 252 (None stored); coverage_manifest surfaced at orchestrator.py:539-546
- **recommendation:** Type the audit outcome explicitly (measured / not_applicable_no_pruned_vars / not_run_no_candidates / FAILED) rather than collapsing all to None; on FAILED, either widen the run's coverage-uncertainty and drop the §VIII.2-compliant flag, or refuse. Never let an audit crash read as a clean bill. Surface the typed outcome and (when measured) the Wilson interval in the manifest.
- **risk:** A crashed or skipped audit is indistinguishable from a passed one, so the honesty layer can certify bounded exhaustiveness on a run where the only empirical miss-rate check never actually ran.


### coverage manifest duplication

**`LDO-EXH-13` · LOW · implementation_gap** — Two divergent CoverageManifest classes with different schemas both claim to be §VIII.2(3)
- **detail:** There are TWO CoverageManifest dataclasses: exhaustiveness.py:65-80 (searched/unsearched as list-of-dict, random_audit_false_negative_rate field, sparsity string A) and coverage.py:23-52 (resolution/lag/functional-form + UnsearchedRegion typed kinds, sparsity string B, as_manifest serializer). resolution.py imports the FIRST; orchestrator.build_coverage_manifest builds the SECOND. Both are surfaced into diagnostics under different keys (coverage_manifest vs coverage_manifest_mr). They have DIFFERENT sparsity-of-truth assumption strings (exhaustiveness.py:71-73 vs coverage.py:33-36), so the run bundle carries two non-identical statements of the same normative assumption, and the searched/unsearched schemas are incompatible (pair-level dicts vs kind-typed regions). A consumer cannot uniformly parse 'what was unsearched'.
- **evidence:** exhaustiveness.py:65-80 vs coverage.py:23-52 (two classes, two sparsity strings); resolution.py:22 imports the first, orchestrator.py:412-416 builds the second
- **recommendation:** Unify to a single CoverageManifest schema that carries BOTH the pair-level searched/unsearched (from the MR screen) and the resolution/lag/functional-form + typed-region coverage (from build_coverage_manifest), with ONE canonical sparsity-of-truth string and one serializer. Emit it under one manifest key so honesty-record consumers have a single, consistent contract.
- **risk:** The honesty artifact is internally inconsistent (two schemas, two assumption strings), undermining the auditability the §VIII.2(3) manifest exists to provide and inviting consumers to parse the wrong one.


### screen null model

**`LDO-EXH-14` · MEDIUM · enhancement_over_msd** — No conformal / distribution-free guarantee on the drill decision; recall could be certified rather than asserted
- **detail:** The MSD frames bounded-exhaustiveness as an honesty story (state the assumption, audit a sample) but never gives the drill filter a DISTRIBUTION-FREE recall guarantee, and the implementation inherits that gap. Modern tools can convert the recall CLAIM into a certificate: (1) Conformal selection / conformalized screening can bound the false-negative proportion of the pruned set at a user level alpha under exchangeability, using the audit as a calibration set -- turning §VIII.2(2) from a point measurement into a guarantee. (2) Model-X knockoffs on the pair grid control the FDR of the DRILLED set exactly. (3) A Benjamini-Hochberg-on-the-screen combined with the deflated partial statistic (LDO-EXH-10) gives a stated expected miss rate. None of these are used; the current design is uncalibrated thresholds + a biased point audit.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §VIII.2 lines 378-384 (assertion-level framing); resolution.py:112-160 (uncalibrated screen); resolution.py:163-188 (point-estimate audit)
- **recommendation:** Adopt conformal selection: hold out a calibration sample of pairs, fine-analyze them (the audit already does a version of this), and use conformal p-values to set the drill threshold so the false-negative proportion of the pruned set is <= alpha with finite-sample validity under exchangeability. This directly delivers the 'bounded' the MSD only names, needs no parametric null, and reuses the existing audit machinery as the calibration set. Report the certified alpha in the manifest.
- **risk:** The project continues to ASSERT bounded false negatives without a certificate when a finite-sample, distribution-free guarantee is achievable with the audit data already being computed -- leaving the flagship honesty claim weaker than the state of the art it could reach.


### LDO-hsic residual construction

**`LDO-HSIC-01` · CRITICAL · statistical_validity_risk** — Residual scan double-dips: HSIC runs on in-sample joint-model residuals, violating the MSD's own cross-fitting mandate
- **detail:** joint_model_residuals (residual_scan.py:79-82) computes the Gaussian-graphical conditional residual e_j = (ΩZ)_j / Ω_jj using the SAME precision Ω that was fit on the SAME data Z passed to the scan. MSD-I §6.6/§6.6.1 (lines 6426, 6428-6456) explicitly mandate cross-fitted residuals e^cf = Res(Y_i, M_{-k}(X_i)) for standard and deep budgets and state 'in-sample residual HSIC is not the default.' The residual scan never partitions into folds and never refits Ω_{-k}. Consequence: the residual e_j is orthogonalized against every other variable BY CONSTRUCTION of Ω on this very sample, so the fitted precision has already absorbed exactly the in-sample linear signal being tested. Under the null of no residual nonlinear dependence, in-sample HSIC on ΩZ residuals is anticonservative because the residuals are not independent of the covariate used to build them — the precision overfits idiosyncratic sample covariance and the leftover residual is negatively correlated with the covariate's own realization. HSIC(x_i, e_j) then has an inflated statistic relative to its permutation null (which permutes e_j but leaves the covariate's over-fit imprint intact), inflating type-I error and producing spurious nonlinear_residual edges. This is the textbook post-selection / double-dipping bias the cross-fitting requirement exists to prevent, and the code silently does the forbidden thing.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §6.6.1 lines 6428-6456; src/pegasus/ldo/residual_scan.py:79-82 (joint_model_residuals), :278 (E = joint_model_residuals(Zc, precision)); hsic.py:95-101 residual_mode_for_hsic says standard→cross_fitted but the LDO path never invokes it
- **recommendation:** Implement K-fold cross-fitting that preserves spatial+temporal blocks (§6.6.1): refit Ω on folds F_{-k}, compute e for F_k from the held-out precision, concatenate the out-of-fold residuals, then HSIC. Alternatively adopt the §6.6.2 parametric-bootstrap D* = E_b[D_b]/(SD_b+ε) for deep budget. At minimum, stamp certification_status='descriptive' and a warning 'in_sample_residuals_uncrossfitted' so no edge is promoted as verified on double-dipped residuals.
- **risk:** Every promoted nonlinear_residual edge in a national C25 determinant run is at risk of being a self-induced artifact of the precision fit rather than a real leftover nonlinearity; scientific claims of nonlinear determinants are unsupported.


### structured null exchangeability

**`LDO-HSIC-02` · CRITICAL · msd_theoretical_fragility** — The within-(spatial-block × temporal-bucket) restricted permutation is NOT an exchangeable null for a spatially autocorrelated panel and stays anticonservative
- **detail:** The executed null (residual_scan.py:332-342) permutes cells only within a stratum keyed by UF (first 2 chars of the muni code) crossed with month/year bucket. A valid permutation null requires the residual cells within a stratum to be EXCHANGEABLE under H0. They are not: within a single UF there remain hundreds of municipalities with strong first-order spatial autocorrelation (Tobler's law — adjacent munis share health-system catchments, socioeconomic gradients, reporting behavior). Restricting to UF removes only BETWEEN-UF structure; the dominant WITHIN-UF neighbor correlation survives and is exactly what an iid-style intra-stratum shuffle destroys. So the permutation still breaks a real dependence that is present under H0, making the null distribution too tight → the observed HSIC exceeds it too often → anticonservative, inflated false positives.
