The MSD-III §III.6 amendment (line 539) endorsing 'within-spatial-block (UF) restricted permutation' is theoretically insufficient for this reason: UF is far too coarse a block for Brazilian municipal spatial autocorrelation, whose correlation length is ~1-3 neighbor hops, not a whole state. The MSD's registry (§6.8) actually specifies spatial-block CYCLIC TIME SHIFT (which preserves the full spatial covariance by shifting time coherently) for the annual panel — the code does NOT run that; it runs an intra-stratum shuffle and merely NAMES the regime.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §6.8 line 6564 (annual = 'spatial block + cyclic time shift'); MSD-III line 539; residual_scan.py:320-345 executes generate_null_indices(strategy='restricted_intra_uf_spatial_swap') which shuffles within UF (nulls.py:145-164), NOT a cyclic time shift
- **recommendation:** Replace the intra-UF shuffle with the registry's spatial_block_cyclic_time_shift (nulls.py already implements it at :106-127) or a moving-block bootstrap over the spatial neighbor graph, so the full within-UF spatial covariance is preserved and only the space↔time alignment is broken. Validate exchangeability empirically: run the null on a KNOWN-independent synthetic pair with realistic spatial autocorrelation and confirm the p-value is uniform (calibration test).
- **risk:** The null is systematically too tight; the FDR control it feeds is built on invalid per-pair p-values, so the entire multiple-testing guarantee collapses and spurious edges pass certification.


### null-strategy provenance

**`LDO-HSIC-03` · HIGH · implementation_gap** — The scan names a §6.8 regime (cyclic time shift / season-preserving) but executes a different, weaker permutation
- **detail:** select_null_regime(panel_type) (residual_scan.py:288) pulls the regime whose null_strategy is e.g. 'spatial_block_cyclic_time_shift' or 'season_preserving_moving_block_circular_shift', and the code inherits regime.permutations and regime.fdr_method from it. But the ACTUAL permutations generated (residual_scan.py:334-338) call generate_null_indices with the HARDCODED strategy 'restricted_intra_uf_spatial_swap' regardless of panel type. The comment at :328-331 admits this ('names the permutation that ACTUALLY RAN ... never the regime's designated circular-shift generator, which cannot even be applied to the ragged complete-case sample'). So for a monthly_seasonal_panel the code claims season-preservation via the regime warnings tuple (edge_warnings includes regime.warnings which contains 'monthly_null_preserves_season') while the executed intra-(UF×month) shuffle does NOT do a moving-block circular shift and does NOT preserve lag-1 autocorrelation (§6.9 line 6595 requires seasonality + lag-1 AC + spatial block ALL preserved). The month bucket preserves seasonality but the within-month shuffle destroys the lag-1 temporal AC the §6.9 correction explicitly demands.
- **evidence:** residual_scan.py:288-289, :334-338, :376 (edge_warnings=(...,*regime.warnings)); nulls.py:41-49 monthly regime warnings; MSD-I §6.9 lines 6590-6598
- **recommendation:** Either (a) make the complete-case sample dense enough to run the true registry generators (they need a rectangular panel_shape=[space,time]) by imputing/masking to a rectangular lattice, or (b) stop attaching regime.warnings that assert properties the executed null does not have. The warning 'monthly_null_preserves_season' is only true of seasonality, not lag-1 AC — emit a distinct honest warning like 'executed_null_breaks_lag1_autocorrelation'.
- **risk:** Provenance is falsified: a reviewer reading the link record believes the null preserved lag-1 autocorrelation and spatial-block dependence per §6.9 when it did neither, so an inflated false-positive edge looks maximally rigorous.


### multiresolution coarsening

**`LDO-HSIC-04` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Memory-triggered coarsening AVERAGES residuals per (block×bucket), which destroys the nonlinear/non-Gaussian signal HSIC exists to detect
- **detail:** _coarsen_residuals (residual_scan.py:213-234) collapses fine cells into (UF×bucket) groups and takes the ARITHMETIC MEAN of the residual within each group (E_coarse /= counts, :233). Two compounding harms. (1) Jensen/aggregation: nonlinear dependence is not preserved under averaging — a threshold or interaction effect present at the municipality level (e.g. HSIC-detectable nonlinearity between residual mortality and a socioeconomic covariate) is smeared out when you average hundreds of munis into one UF-month number; the coarse HSIC has near-zero power to detect exactly the structure §III.6 targets. (2) CLT Gaussianization: averaging n_g municipal residuals makes the coarse residual approximately Gaussian (Berry-Esseen), and HSIC's advantage over linear correlation is precisely on NON-Gaussian dependence — so post-averaging, HSIC≈linear correlation, defeating the whole 'residual NONLINEAR audit' purpose (§III.6). Meanwhile the fine-grain signal that WOULD have triggered a real edge is gone, and the run reports a coarse scan as if it were the audit.
- **evidence:** residual_scan.py:213-234 (_coarsen_residuals, mean pooling at :233), :304-306 (triggered when estimate_residual_scan_bytes exceeds budget); MSD-III §III.6 line 226 (HSIC targets nonlinear/threshold/seasonal structure)
- **recommendation:** Do not mean-pool. If memory-bound, either (a) subsample municipalities within blocks (preserving the empirical marginal, valid for HSIC) rather than averaging, or (b) use the Nyström/RFF approximation at the FINE grain (its whole purpose is O(n·features) memory, avoiding the n×n kernel) rather than falling back to spatial averaging, or (c) tile the pair loop over cell-blocks with streaming HSIC. Emit certification_status='descriptive' whenever coarsening occurred (currently only a warning is added, :377-378, and the edge can still be 'selected').
- **risk:** At national scale the exact-kernel path (n_eff·n_eff for p variables) exceeds RAM (envelope.py:82 notes ~48 GB), so coarsening is the COMMON national path — meaning the flagship national run silently runs a Gaussianized linear-equivalent audit and calls it nonlinear residual detection.


### FDR under dependence

**`LDO-HSIC-05` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — BH/BY/Storey assume PRDS or independence; a spatial health panel's p-values are strongly, non-PRDS dependent, so the FDR guarantee is unproven
- **detail:** correct_p_values (fdr.py) applies BH (factor=1), BY (factor=harmonic(m)), or Storey. BH controls FDR under independence or PRDS (positive regression dependence on a subset); BY controls it under ARBITRARY dependence at the cost of the harmonic(m) inflation. The regime map (nulls.py) assigns BY to annual/monthly/sparse panels and BH to cross-sectional. Two problems. (1) The p-values across the p(p-1)/2 variable pairs are NOT independent and NOT plausibly PRDS: the residuals E share the SAME precision fit and the SAME set of permutations (perms reused across all pairs, residual_scan.py:358-362), inducing strong, sign-indefinite cross-pair correlation in the test statistics — this is neither independence nor guaranteed positive dependence, so BH is unjustified and even BY's arbitrary-dependence guarantee assumes the p-values are valid marginally (which LDO-HSIC-02 shows they are not). (2) Reusing the identical permutation set across pairs makes the null statistics comonotone across pairs, maximizing dependence and further stressing the PRDS assumption. The MSD (§6.8) picks the FDR method by panel type but never analyzes the dependence structure of the pairwise HSIC p-values — a hand-wavy assignment.
- **evidence:** fdr.py:29-42; nulls.py:38-72 (fdr_method per regime); residual_scan.py:358-362 (single shared perms list reused for every pair)
- **recommendation:** For arbitrary dependence, BY is the defensible default everywhere (drop BH for cross-sectional given shared-fit dependence). Better: use a permutation-based FDR that respects the joint null — draw a fresh permutation-null MATRIX (all pairs under the same permutation) and compute the FDR from the joint exceedance distribution (Westfall-Young maxT / step-down), which is valid under arbitrary dependence AND uses the actual dependence rather than a worst-case bound. Consider knockoffs or e-values (e-BH is valid under arbitrary dependence with less conservatism than BY).
- **risk:** The q-values gating edge promotion (residual_scan.py:381 qv<=alpha) carry no valid FDR guarantee; the '≤alpha false discovery' claim on the national determinant set is unsubstantiated.


### permutation p-value resolution vs BY inflation

**`LDO-HSIC-06` · HIGH · computational_trap** — BY×harmonic(m) inflation collides with the 1/(perms+1) p-value floor, making national-scale edges structurally uncertifiable at alpha=0.1
- **detail:** The minimum achievable permutation p-value is 1/(perms+1) (residual_scan.py:369; hsic.py:322). For the annual regime perms=1000 → p_min≈9.99e-4; monthly perms=2000 → p_min≈5e-4. BY multiplies by the dependency factor harmonic(m). For p=130 variables, m=p(p-1)/2≈8385 tests, harmonic(8385)≈9.6. The smallest possible BY q-value for the single most significant edge is ≈ p_min × m × harmonic(m) / m = p_min × harmonic(m) ≈ 9.99e-4 × 9.6 ≈ 9.6e-3 for annual (borderline under alpha=0.1) but the SECOND-ranked edge is p_min×m×9.6/(m-1)... the practical issue: with only ~1000-2000 permutations the p-value grid is coarse, and after BY inflation the effective detectable q-floor rises steeply with m. At national p and few permutations, a genuine strong nonlinearity can be capped at a q-value above 0.1 purely by permutation resolution × BY factor, so it is NEVER certified — a systematic false-negative floor that scales with the number of variables. Conversely the code hardcodes alpha=0.1 (residual_scan.py:245) with no adjustment for the permutation grain.
- **evidence:** residual_scan.py:369 pval floor, :375 BY correction, :381 qv<=alpha, :245 alpha=0.1; fdr.py:31 factor=harmonic(m); nulls.py:36,45 permutations=1000/2000
- **recommendation:** Scale permutation count with m so p_min ≪ alpha/(m·harmonic(m)) for the intended detectable effect (or use a p-value estimator with tail modeling — GPD fit to the permutation tail, Knijnenburg 2009 — to get sub-1/(B+1) resolution without exploding B). Report the achievable q-floor alongside alpha so uncertifiable-by-resolution is typed, not silent.
- **risk:** The national run reports 'no significant nonlinear residual edges' when the truth is 'the test had no resolution to certify any' — a power failure masquerading as a negative scientific finding.


### complete-case selection

**`LDO-HSIC-07` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Complete-case + sparsest-variable trimming makes the residual sample a non-random subset biased toward dense, urban, high-count municipalities
- **detail:** scan_residual_nonlinear_edges keeps only cells where ALL kept variables are jointly finite (observed_full, residual_scan.py:256, :269) and, when that is too small, TRIMS the sparsest variables until n_eff≥100 (:258-264). Both steps induce selection bias. (1) Complete-case over municipal×time cells preferentially retains large municipalities where every rare cause (e.g. C25 pancreatic deaths) is observed and drops small munis where sparse counts are zero/missing — but zeros in sparse rural munis are exactly the epidemiologically informative low-incidence signal. The retained sample over-represents urban, high-SUS-coverage areas, so any detected nonlinear edge is conditional on 'municipality dense enough to observe all variables', which is itself correlated with the socioeconomic determinants under study (collider/selection bias on the very association being tested). (2) Trimming the sparsest VARIABLES silently changes the hypothesis set: the national context variables most likely to be trimmed (rare SIDRA socioeconomic indicators) are dropped from the scan, so their nonlinear determinant role can never be discovered — and which variables survive depends on the data realization, breaking the pre-registered variable set.
- **evidence:** residual_scan.py:256-277 (observed_full ALL-finite, trimming loop, ResidualScanUnderpowered), :277 (Zc = where(finite, Zc, 0.0) fills residual non-finite with 0 AFTER selection)
- **recommendation:** Model missingness rather than list-wise deleting: pairwise-complete HSIC (each pair uses its own mutually-observed support, restoring sparse-muni signal per pair) instead of a single all-variables-complete sample; or multiple imputation / inverse-probability-of-observation weighting on the residual field. Never fill non-finite residuals with 0 (:277) — 0 is a valid residual value and injecting it biases the kernel toward a spurious mode. Record the trimmed variable set as a typed limitation.
- **risk:** Determinant edges are estimated on a self-selected urban subsample and generalized to Brazil; the epidemiological-transition and undercount gradients (rural, Norte/Nordeste) that drive real C25 heterogeneity are systematically excluded.


### UF block derivation / boundary changes

**`LDO-HSIC-08` · MEDIUM · epidemiological_risk** — Spatial block = first 2 digits of the space id assumes a stable 7-digit IBGE code and ignores AMC boundary changes
- **detail:** The spatial stratum is derived as str(space_ids[s])[:2] (residual_scan.py:291-294) — the first two characters of the geographic id, taken to be the UF code of a 7-digit IBGE municipality code. Two fragilities. (1) If space_ids are not 7-digit IBGE codes (e.g. already UF-level, region codes, health-region codes, or zero-padded differently), [:2] silently mis-blocks cells, and the fallback '00' for out-of-range indices (:292) lumps unrelated cells into a single bogus block — corrupting the null strata without any error. (2) Over 2000-2025 (the C25 study window) municipal boundaries changed (new municipalities split off, code reassignments); the pancreatic-cancer memory notes AMC harmonization exists elsewhere, but here a raw [:2] slice does not consult any AMC map, so a municipality that changed code mid-panel is treated as two different cells possibly in inconsistent UF blocks, leaking cross-time identity into the spatial null.
- **evidence:** residual_scan.py:291-294 (str(field.space_ids[s])[:2], '00' fallback); memory demographic-tensor/AMC harmonization notes AMC is handled in the population layer, not here
- **recommendation:** Derive the UF (and, better, a finer spatial block from the neighbor graph / SpatialWeightGraph already in the codebase) from a validated code→UF map, asserting the id length/format; refuse or warn on unmapped codes instead of '00'. Use AMC-harmonized stable municipality identity so a code change does not split one place into two cells.
- **risk:** Mis-blocked strata make the restricted permutation neither within-UF nor spatially coherent, quietly invalidating the structured null for exactly the boundary-changing municipalities that are common in the Norte agricultural frontier.


### Nyström/RFF approximation control

**`LDO-HSIC-09` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — Feature-map HSIC uses a fixed seed-determined landmark/frequency draw for both statistic and null, so approximation error is a deterministic bias not integrated into the null
- **detail:** In approx mode _pair_stat_and_null (residual_scan.py:172-183) computes stat = ||fx^T fy/(n-1)||_F^2 and the null by permuting fy[pm] with the SAME frozen feature maps fx, fy (built once per variable in _build_var_reprs, :137-155). The RFF frequencies (_np_rff_features, hsic.py:179-187) and Nyström landmarks (:190-203) are drawn from a fixed seed. This is internally coherent (stat and null share the same map, so under H0 the permutation distribution is still valid FOR THAT MAP), but it has two costs. (1) The finite-feature HSIC is a biased, high-variance estimate of the true kernel HSIC; the bias depends on the specific random frequencies, which are fixed — so the reported statistic is one noisy draw, and rerunning with a different seed can flip an edge in/out with no diagnostic. (2) Nyström with clip(eigenvalues,1e-8) (hsic.py:202) on a near-degenerate landmark Gram (heavy-tailed count residuals cluster, making landmarks near-duplicate) inflates small eigenvalues, distorting the feature inner products and hence the statistic magnitude non-uniformly across pairs — differentially biasing which pairs look dependent. Type-I control is not proven for the feature-map null when the map is degenerate.
- **evidence:** residual_scan.py:137-155 (_build_var_reprs fixed-seed maps), :172-183; hsic.py:190-203 (_np_nystrom_features eigenvalue clip at :202), :179-187
- **recommendation:** Average the statistic over several independent feature draws (reduces variance, gives a Monte-Carlo error bar → feeds §V.5 precision controller: escalate to exact when feature-map variance dominates). Prefer leverage-score or k-means++ landmark selection over uniform random landmarks for heavy-tailed count residuals. Emit the approximation variance as an approximation_diagnostic and gate certification on it (exact-certifies-approximate, §V.6).
- **risk:** National-scale edges (always approx mode, n>5000) are seed-fragile and their type-I error is uncontrolled where landmarks degenerate — the most epidemiologically clustered variables (sparse counts) are exactly where degeneracy bites.


### median-heuristic bandwidth on count residuals

**`LDO-HSIC-10` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — The median-heuristic RBF bandwidth is estimated on a seeded 2048-pair subsample and is ill-defined for heavy-tailed / near-degenerate count residuals
- **detail:** _bandwidth (hsic.py:108-129) uses the median pairwise |Δ| as the RBF length scale, but when n(n-1)/2 > 2048 it estimates the median from only 2048 rng-sampled pairs (:113,125-127), and takes max(median,1e-9) with a fallback of 1.0 if no positive gaps. Three fragilities for epidemiological count residuals. (1) The subsampled median is a noisy, seed-dependent estimate of the bandwidth; different seeds give different bandwidths → different HSIC statistics and p-values, with no reported sensitivity. (2) For sparse counts many residuals are (near-)tied (lots of zeros/small integers), so a large fraction of pairwise gaps are 0; the positive-only median can land on a tiny gap, giving a near-zero bandwidth → the RBF kernel becomes an identity-like indicator (K≈I), collapsing HSIC toward a degenerate ties-counting statistic with pathological null behavior. (3) The median heuristic is a well-known-but-arbitrary default with no optimality for dependence DETECTION (it optimizes nothing about power); for threshold/seasonal structure a single global bandwidth is the wrong scale.
- **evidence:** hsic.py:108-129 (subsample cap 2048 at :112, positive-only median :128, fallback 1.0)
- **recommendation:** Use the full-data median (it is O(n) via a quantile on a random projection, or exact via np.median of a vectorized upper triangle — the code already vectorizes the full branch, just raise/remove the 2048 cap or make it seed-averaged). Consider a multi-bandwidth aggregated HSIC (sum HSIC over a bandwidth ladder, or the spectral MMD-agg of Schrab et al. 2023) which is adaptive and has power across scales. Detect the tied-residual degeneracy and route to descriptive.
- **risk:** Bandwidth instability makes HSIC statistics non-reproducible run-to-run and can silently degenerate on the sparse-count variables that dominate rare-cause mortality studies.


### iid fallback null

**`LDO-HSIC-11` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — When only one spatial OR temporal block exists the code falls back to a full iid permutation — the maximally anticonservative null it was built to avoid
- **detail:** If n_spatial_blocks<2 AND n_temporal_blocks<2 the code sets perm_list=None and null_strategy='iid_permutation' (residual_scan.py:343-345), then draws unrestricted np permutations (:360-362). An iid permutation destroys ALL spatial and temporal autocorrelation, so for autocorrelated residuals it produces the tightest possible null and the highest false-positive rate — precisely the failure the structured null exists to prevent (the module docstring and :281-286 say so). The guard only triggers the structured null when there are ≥2 blocks in SOME dimension; but a single-UF study, a single-year slice with one temporal bucket, or a run where trimming/coarsening collapsed the block count all fall through to iid. There is a SEPARATE descriptive gate (sufficient_blocks via descriptive_only_when_insufficient_blocks, needs ≥5 blocks in BOTH dims, :349-351) which correctly downgrades to certification_status='descriptive' — good — but the p-values themselves are still computed under the invalid iid null and reported as weights/uncertainties, and a run with, say, 3 spatial + 4 temporal blocks passes the ≥2 gate (structured null) yet FAILS the ≥5 gate, and the intra-stratum shuffle over 3 blocks is nearly iid anyway.
- **evidence:** residual_scan.py:332-345 (≥2-block gate → structured else iid), :349-351 (≥5-block descriptive gate), :360-362 (iid np permutations)
- **recommendation:** Never compute p-values under the iid null for autocorrelated residuals — if no valid restricted null exists, emit descriptive-only with no p-value (None) rather than an invalid number. Align the two thresholds: require the SAME (≥5) block count both to run the structured null AND to certify, so the 2–4 block regime is descriptive with no misleading p-value.
- **risk:** Single-state or single-period sub-studies (a natural way to run the engine) get iid p-values that look certifiable, and the 2-4 block gap produces near-iid p-values labeled as structured.


### count outcome distributional assumption

**`LDO-HSIC-12` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Gaussian-graphical conditional residuals impose a Gaussian/linear-precision model on sparse over-dispersed death counts; HSIC then audits the wrong residual
- **detail:** The residual e_j=(ΩZ)_j/Ω_jj (residual_scan.py:79-82) is the conditional residual of a GAUSSIAN graphical model — it is only the correct 'what the model could not explain' residual if Z is jointly Gaussian with precision Ω. Brazilian municipal rare-cause counts (C25 deaths per muni-year) are sparse, non-negative, over-dispersed, and zero-inflated; a Gaussian-copula margin (LDO-01) partially addresses the marginal but the CONDITIONAL residual via a linear precision still assumes homoscedastic Gaussian conditional structure. For counts the correct residual is a Pearson/deviance/randomized-quantile residual under a negative-binomial or Poisson-with-exposure model. Using the Gaussian conditional residual means: (1) mean-variance misspecification leaves heteroscedastic structure in e that HSIC will flag as 'nonlinear residual dependence' when it is merely count over-dispersion (variance∝mean) — a systematic false-positive mechanism tied to population size (large munis have larger variance), so the flagged 'nonlinear determinant' is often just a population-scaling artifact. (2) The MSD frames HSIC as detecting leftover NONLINEARITY, but the dominant leftover in a Gaussianized count model is DISTRIBUTIONAL, not a real interaction.
- **evidence:** residual_scan.py:79-82 (Gaussian conditional residual); MSD-III line 226 (residual = 'what the structured model could not explain'); MSD-I §6 count+exposure handling is in the margins, not the residual definition used here
- **recommendation:** Compute HSIC on randomized-quantile (Dunn-Smyth) residuals from the count model (NB/Poisson-with-exposure) so that under a correctly specified mean-variance model the residuals are iid uniform/normal and HSIC tests genuine leftover dependence, not over-dispersion. Explicitly deflate population-size dependence by including log-exposure as a conditioning variable or partialling it out before HSIC.
- **risk:** HSIC edges track population size / over-dispersion rather than epidemiological nonlinearity; the flagship claim of nonlinear socioeconomic determinants of pancreatic mortality is confounded by the very count-scaling the study must control.


### envelope estimate vs available RAM race

**`LDO-HSIC-13` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — Residual-scan memory guard compares a float64 estimate against 60% of INSTANTANEOUS available RAM, an unstable non-reproducible gate that can OOM or spuriously coarsen
- **detail:** _residual_scan_memory_budget (residual_scan.py:54-67) returns 60% of psutil.virtual_memory().available at call time. The coarsening decision (:304) compares estimate_residual_scan_bytes against this live number. Two issues. (1) Non-reproducibility: whether the national scan runs at fine grain or coarsens (which, per LDO-HSIC-04, changes the SCIENTIFIC result to a Gaussianized linear-equivalent) depends on how much RAM other processes happen to be using at that instant — the same run gives different edges on a busy vs idle box. (2) The estimate only counts the retained per-variable representation cache (envelope.py:74-95) and ignores the TRANSIENT per-pair n×n kernel products (_pair_stat_and_null exact mode builds kx*ky, and null builds ky[ix_(pm,pm)] — an extra n×n per permutation), so the true peak is higher than estimated; the 60% headroom is a magic number chosen to paper over this rather than modeling the transient. Under memory pressure the true allocation can exceed the 60% budget mid-scan and OOM despite passing the guard — the §V.1 'refuse, never OOM' contract is not actually guaranteed.
- **evidence:** residual_scan.py:54-67 (0.60 magic factor, live available RAM), :304-312; envelope.py:74-95 (estimate omits transient kernel/permutation allocations)
- **recommendation:** Base the guard on total system RAM (fixed, reproducible) minus a declared reservation, not instantaneous available. Include the transient n×n permutation kernel term in estimate_residual_scan_bytes. Make the fine-vs-coarse decision deterministic given (p, n_eff, budget) so the scientific output is reproducible; log the chosen grain.
- **risk:** The same national dataset yields different (and differently-valid) edge sets across machines/moments, and the 'never OOM' guarantee is violated when transient allocations spike.


### exceedance tie handling inconsistency

**`LDO-HSIC-14` · LOW · implementation_gap** — Two HSIC code paths use different tie-handling in the permutation p-value (>= vs >=-1e-15), a subtle reproducibility/validity divergence
- **detail:** The live LDO residual scan computes p = (1 + #{null >= stat}) / (1 + B) with a strict >= (residual_scan.py:369), while the reference scanner's permutation_p_value uses #{value >= statistic - 1e-15} (hsic.py:321-322), i.e. an epsilon-relaxed >= that counts near-ties as exceedances. For discrete/tied count residuals near-ties are common, so the two paths can assign different p-values to the same statistic — the reference path is slightly more conservative. Since the docstrings claim the live path reproduces numpy_kernel_hsic_permutation_test 'bit-for-bit' (residual_scan.py:88-92), this is a real divergence from the asserted equivalence. Neither is wrong per se, but the inconsistency means the acceptance-tested reference does not match the production path exactly, undermining the test's validity as a guardrail.
- **evidence:** residual_scan.py:369 (strict >=); hsic.py:321-322 (>= statistic - 1e-15)
- **recommendation:** Pick one tie convention (the +1e-15-relaxed >= is the safer, ties-conservative choice) and use it in both paths; add a test that the live path and the reference produce identical p-values on a tied-count fixture.
- **risk:** The 'bit-for-bit reproduces the reference' guarantee is false on tied data, so the acceptance test can pass while the production statistic differs — a silent guardrail gap.


### single-pooled-residual latent confounding

**`LDO-HSIC-15` · MEDIUM · identifiability_concern** — HSIC on a pair of joint-model residuals cannot distinguish a real nonlinear edge from a shared unmodeled latent driving both residuals nonlinearly
- **detail:** The scan tests HSIC(e_i, e_j) for residual pairs and promotes significant ones to edge_type='nonlinear_residual' (residual_scan.py:386-390). But a significant residual HSIC has (at least) three generating mechanisms, only one of which is a genuine i–j edge: (a) a true nonlinear i→j / i–j dependence; (b) a common LATENT factor the low-rank layer (LDO-03) did not capture, driving BOTH residuals nonlinearly (spatial confounders, epidemiological-transition stage, reporting-quality gradient — all nonlinear and unmodeled); (c) residual spatial autocorrelation shared via geography (the null is supposed to kill this but per LDO-HSIC-02 it does not fully). The MSD (§III.6, §III.8) frames HSIC as a candidate FLAG requiring downstream certification, and the certification gate mentions latent-vs-lag separability — but the residual HSIC layer itself attaches NO latent-confounding diagnostic to the edge and there is no conditional-HSIC / partial dependence test against the reconstructed latent factors. So a latent-driven co-residual is reported as a pairwise nonlinear edge with the same status as a genuine one.
- **evidence:** residual_scan.py:386-393 (edge_type='nonlinear_residual', no latent-confound ref); MSD-III §III.8 line 245 (latent-vs-lag separability is a certification requirement)
- **recommendation:** Test residual dependence CONDITIONAL on the reconstructed low-rank latent factors (conditional HSIC / KCI test of Zhang et al. 2011, or partial out the latent scores before HSIC). Populate the LinkRecord confounding_factor_refs with the latent factors whose inclusion attenuates the HSIC, and downgrade to descriptive when a latent explains the dependence. This operationalizes the §III.8 separability gate at the residual layer instead of deferring it.
- **risk:** Shared latent socioeconomic/epidemiological-transition confounders masquerade as direct nonlinear determinant edges — the classic ecological-confounding trap that discredits small-area determinant studies.


### LDO-causal / Rung-2 ITS

**`LDO-CAUSAL-ITS-GAUSS-01` · CRITICAL · implementation_gap** — ITS segmented regression runs on GAUSSIANIZED latent Z, not raw counts — destroys the level/slope semantics it claims to measure
- **detail:** In orchestrator.py:339 the ITS series is built as `series_by_var = {v: np.nanmean(gf.Z[i], axis=0)}`, i.e. the SPATIAL MEAN of the gaussianized field gf.Z, NOT the raw counts. The copula PIT map z=Phi^{-1}(F_hat(x)) is a monotone, RANK-based, marginally-flattening transform: it maps every variable to a standard-normal marginal and, crucially, is designed to DESTROY the marginal shape (level, scale, trend curvature) while preserving only rank-dependence. Segmented regression y_t = b0 + b1 t + b2 D_t + b3 (t-t0) D_t interprets b2 as an *immediate level change in the outcome's native units* and b3 as a *slope change*. On a rank-gaussianized series these coefficients have no epidemiological meaning: a doubling of a mortality rate and a 10% rise can produce identical z-jumps if their ranks shift identically, and a genuine step in counts is compressed toward the tails nonlinearly. Worse, gaussianization is applied over the POOLED (S·T) distribution, so the per-time spatial-mean of z is not even a monotone function of the per-time mean count. Every Rung-2 promotion, level_t, and slope_t is therefore computed on a quantity that is not the outcome. The MSD-III §IV text ('interrupted-time-series around dated shocks') presumes the outcome series; the wiring silently substitutes a latent surrogate.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §IV (l.255); src/pegasus/ldo/orchestrator.py:339 (series from gf.Z), quasi.py:35-57 interrupted_time_series
- **recommendation:** Run ITS on a native-scale aggregate: use the raw count/rate series (sum of raw counts / sum of exposure per t, from raw_field.X and its exposure), or at minimum inverse_gaussianize the per-t spatial aggregate back to native units before regression. Fit ITS as a per-municipality mixed/segmented Poisson-GLM and pool, not a single national OLS on z. Report effects in native units with an offset for population.
- **risk:** Every Rung-2 causal-effect magnitude and its significance is scientifically meaningless; a policy 'level change' reported to an epidemiologist is a rank-space artefact, not deaths averted.

**`LDO-CAUSAL-ITS-AGG-02` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — National spatial-mean ITS ignores ecological heterogeneity and treats a single national series as the unit of analysis
- **detail:** The ITS is fit on np.nanmean over municipalities: one national aggregate series of length T. For Brazilian municipal health data this collapses ~5570 municipalities of vastly different size, epidemiological-transition stage, and undercount into one line. (a) A policy shock that raises the outcome in the North but not the Southeast averages away; a break real in 20% of munis is invisible. (b) The nanmean is an UNWEIGHTED average of per-muni values, so a rate step in a 3000-person muni counts equally with São Paulo — Simpson-style aggregation bias. (c) Aggregating first then regressing forfeits all within-panel degrees of freedom: T is ~12-300, so the t-tests have almost no power and are dominated by autocorrelation. The design should exploit the S×T panel (fixed effects + segmented trend, clustered SEs), not a length-T scalar series.
- **evidence:** orchestrator.py:339 np.nanmean(gf.Z[i], axis=0); quasi.py:35 single-series ITS
- **recommendation:** Population-weight the aggregate (weight by exposure), or fit a panel ITS with municipality fixed effects and a common/region-varying break, with cluster-robust or spatial HAC SEs. Report effect heterogeneity, not a single national coefficient.
- **risk:** Real localized policy effects are missed (false negatives) and small-muni noise spikes drive spurious national breaks (false positives); the national coefficient is uninterpretable ecologically.

**`LDO-CAUSAL-ITS-AUTOCORR-03` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — ITS t-statistics assume iid Gaussian residuals — segmented health series are strongly autocorrelated and seasonal, inflating significance
- **detail:** interrupted_time_series computes se from sigma2 = RSS/dof and diag((X'X)^{-1}) — the textbook OLS SE that assumes homoskedastic, INDEPENDENT residuals. Interrupted time series of monthly health counts have strong AR(1)+ autocorrelation and 12-month seasonality; the segmented model has no seasonal terms and no autocorrelation correction. Positively autocorrelated residuals make OLS SEs anticonservative — |level_t| and |slope_t| are systematically inflated, sometimes by 2-4x. The break detector (detect_structural_break) then scans EVERY interior index and keeps the max |level_t| (a max over ~T-8 correlated tests) with NO multiple-testing adjustment, so under a pure autocorrelated null it will routinely return a 'significant' break at min_t=3.0. This is a classic ITS pitfall (Bernal/Cummins/Gasparrini) the code ignores.
- **evidence:** quasi.py:47-56 (OLS SE), quasi.py:60-73 detect_structural_break (argmax scan, no MHT)
- **recommendation:** Use Newey-West/HAC or Prais-Winsten/GLS SEs; add harmonic seasonal terms; correct the break scan for multiplicity (supF / Andrews–Quandt critical values, or Bai-Perron) rather than a fixed |t|>=3. Report the break as sup-Wald with its proper distribution, not the max of naive t's.
- **risk:** Spurious structural breaks and inflated Rung-2 promotions; the ladder manufactures quasi-experimental 'evidence' from autocorrelated noise.


### LDO-causal / negative-control veto

**`LDO-CAUSAL-NC-BINOM-04` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Negative-control veto is an ad-hoc fraction>=0.5 threshold, not a principled test; magic numbers uncalibrated to the null false-positive rate
- **detail:** negative_control_break_fraction counts the fraction of ALL other variables that show |level_t|>=3 at the SAME shock_index, and escalate_rung2_its vetoes when nc_frac>=0.5 with nc_n>=3 (nc_veto_fraction=0.5, nc_min_controls=3). This is not a statistically principled negative-control test (cf. Lipsitch/Tchetgen-Tchetgen negative-control calibration, which estimates and SUBTRACTS a bias function). Problems: (1) Because the ITS SEs are anticonservative (finding 03), under a common seasonal wave a LARGE fraction of controls will 'break' at many indices, so the veto will fire almost always at seasonal peaks — over-vetoing genuine effects. (2) Conversely 0.5 is arbitrary: with 3 controls the veto fires at 2/3; the decision hinges on a couple of noisy series. (3) There is no notion of a control being a *valid* negative control (unaffected by the exposure but sharing confounders) — it just uses every other variable, many of which are legitimately caused by the same shock (mediators/descendants), so the 'common shock' interpretation is wrong. A true effect with pathway-shared movers is vetoed as a common shock.
- **evidence:** quasi.py:94-113, 155-160; thresholds nc_veto_fraction=0.5, nc_min_controls=3, min_level_t=3.0
- **recommendation:** Replace the fraction heuristic with a calibrated negative-control-outcome procedure: (a) pre-declare true negative controls (outcomes on the same platform sharing confounding but no causal link to exposure), (b) estimate the empirical null distribution of ITS effects across them and use it to compute a calibrated p-value / bias-corrected effect (Schuemie-style empirical calibration), (c) veto/deflate based on that null, not a raw >0.5 count. Exclude known descendants of the exposure from the control set.
- **risk:** The single most load-bearing causal safeguard is an uncalibrated knob: it both over-vetoes real effects at seasonal times and lets confounded breaks through, with no quantified false-positive control.


### LDO-causal / Rung-1 LiNGAM

**`LDO-CAUSAL-LINGAM-POOL-05` · HIGH · identifiability_concern** — Pairwise LiNGAM runs on the flattened S×T panel as if iid — spatial/temporal autocorrelation and mixture-of-municipalities fabricate non-Gaussianity and break the ANM independence test
- **detail:** orient_data = raw_field.X[i].reshape(-1) pools all municipalities and months into one long vector treated as iid draws for is_non_gaussian (D'Agostino) and _nonlinear_dependence. Two failures: (1) The non-Gaussianity 'licence': a mixture of many municipal distributions (each perhaps near-Gaussian on its log scale) with wildly different means/variances is AUTOMATICALLY non-Gaussian (heavy skew/kurtosis) by mixing alone. So is_non_gaussian nearly always returns True for reasons that have nothing to do with the disturbance non-Gaussianity LiNGAM needs — the identifiability precondition is satisfied by aggregation artefact, not by the structural noise term. (2) The additive-noise-model orientation logic (residual independent of cause) assumes iid samples; with strong spatial autocorrelation (neighbouring munis) and temporal autocorrelation, the residual carries structured dependence that the moment-based _nonlinear_dependence score reads as 'dependence', and the asymmetry dep_xy vs dep_yx is driven by the heteroskedastic muni-mixture, not the causal arrow. Sun-style / pooled LiNGAM on panel data is known to be inconsistent under such heterogeneity.
- **evidence:** orchestrator.py:329; orient.py:37-53 is_non_gaussian, 63-106 lingam_pairwise_direction
- **recommendation:** Do not pool. Either (a) orient on de-meaned within-municipality residuals after removing muni and time fixed effects (so mixture-induced non-Gaussianity is stripped and the test targets structural noise), or (b) use a panel-aware ANM/LiNGAM (e.g. per-muni orientation with a vote + heterogeneity check, or time-series-LiNGAM/VAR-LiNGAM), and require the non-Gaussianity to survive fixed-effect removal before licensing orientation.
- **risk:** Orientation direction is decided by demographic heterogeneity across municipalities, not causation; a confident 'X->Y' arrow is an aggregation artefact.

**`LDO-CAUSAL-LINGAM-LINEAR-06` · MEDIUM · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Orientation uses a LINEAR OLS residual but scores NONLINEAR dependence — an internally inconsistent ANM that mis-orients under nonlinear or heteroskedastic mechanisms
- **detail:** _residual computes a purely LINEAR OLS residual t - beta*r (beta = correlation), then _nonlinear_dependence measures higher-order (squared/cubic/tanh) cross-moments. This mixes two model classes. LiNGAM proper assumes a LINEAR SEM with non-Gaussian noise and tests linear-residual INDEPENDENCE via kurtosis-based measures; ANM (Hoyer et al.) fits a NONLINEAR regression then tests residual independence. Here a linear fit is combined with a nonlinear independence score, so: if the true mechanism is nonlinear (common in epidemiology — saturating dose-response, thresholds), the linear residual retains the nonlinear signal in BOTH directions, and the asymmetry that decides the arrow reflects which variable is more skewed, not which is the cause. Under heteroskedastic noise (variance depends on the cause — near-universal for counts, Var∝mean) the linear-residual variance correlates with the regressor, injecting a spurious (a^2-1)*b term into _nonlinear_dependence that biases the direction toward the higher-variance variable. The method is neither clean LiNGAM nor clean ANM.
- **evidence:** orient.py:56-60 _residual (linear), 63-78 _nonlinear_dependence (nonlinear moments)
- **recommendation:** Commit to one identifiable model: for clean LiNGAM use DirectLiNGAM/ICA-LiNGAM with kurtosis-based independence on the LINEAR residual only; for nonlinear mechanisms fit a nonparametric regression (GAM/GP) and test residual independence with HSIC. Explicitly model count heteroskedasticity (work on variance-stabilized / log scale) so mean-variance coupling does not masquerade as directional asymmetry.
- **risk:** Systematic mis-orientation whenever the mechanism is nonlinear or the noise is count-heteroskedastic — i.e. most epidemiological edges.


### LDO-causal / Rung-1 collider

**`LDO-CAUSAL-FAITHFUL-07` · HIGH · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Collider/v-structure orientation presumes faithfulness, which epidemiological data with cancellation and near-deterministic denominators routinely violates
- **detail:** is_collider orients A->C<-B when marginal|corr(A,B)|<0.1 but partial|corr(A,B|C)|>0.2. This is the PC v-structure rule and it REQUIRES the faithfulness assumption: that all and only the (in)dependencies implied by the DAG hold in the distribution. Epi data violates faithfulness in identifiable ways: (1) Cancellation — two pathways of opposite sign make A and B marginally near-independent even when they ARE directly linked (A–B edge present but corr≈0), so the code sees an 'unshielded' triple that is actually shielded → a false collider. (2) Near-deterministic relations through shared denominators/populations create almost-perfect conditional structure that trips the >0.2 threshold. (3) With ~5570 munis the sampling noise on a partial correlation is tiny, so the 0.1/0.2 thresholds resolve trivially small effects as 'independent'/'dependent', and any weak confounder flips a triple to a collider. Faithfulness is untestable and the MSD asserts collider detection is 'machine-checkable' (l.254) — it is only checkable CONDITIONAL on an unverifiable assumption, which the honesty layer does not surface.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §IV l.254 ('machine-checkable'); orient.py:116-124 is_collider (thresholds 0.1/0.2)
- **recommendation:** Use significance-calibrated CI tests (Fisher-z with n_eff, not raw thresholds), require stable v-structures across resampling (majority-PC / stability selection), and add a faithfulness-violation stress diagnostic (test for path cancellation by checking sign-consistency). Downgrade collider claims to 'assumes faithfulness' in causal_assumptions and never call them machine-checkable without that caveat.
- **risk:** Confident A->C<-B arrows that are faithfulness artefacts; a collider mis-orientation also opens a spurious backdoor if downstream adjustment uses the DAG.

**`LDO-CAUSAL-COLLIDER-THRESH-08` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — Collider decision uses hard magic thresholds (0.1 marginal, 0.2 conditional) with no sample-size scaling or hypothesis test
- **detail:** is_collider(indep_threshold=0.1, dep_threshold=0.2) decides marginal independence and conditional dependence by comparing raw |correlation| to fixed constants. These are pure magic numbers with no statistical justification: (1) They do not scale with n — at national panel n the standard error of a correlation is ~1/sqrt(n)≈0.0002, so a 'marginal' correlation of 0.09 is astronomically significant yet is declared 'independent'; the test conflates effect size with independence. (2) The gap between 0.1 and 0.2 is a dead zone where triples are neither confirmed nor rejected, silently dropped. (3) Because orientation pools the flattened panel, the effective sample is inflated and every correlation is 'significant', so the thresholds, not the data, decide everything. There is also no correction for the O(deg^2) triples tested per node.
- **evidence:** orient.py:116-124 default thresholds; called from apply_collider_orientation:191
- **recommendation:** Replace magnitude thresholds with Fisher-z conditional-independence tests at a controlled level using n_eff (spatially/temporally adjusted, not raw N), with FDR/Bonferroni across the triples tested. Make the CI test the licence, not an arbitrary 0.1/0.2 band.
- **risk:** Collider orientation is governed by hand-tuned constants that break at national sample size — either everything or nothing is a collider depending on N, untethered from evidence.

**`LDO-CAUSAL-COLLIDER-CONFLICT-09` · MEDIUM · implementation_gap** — Conflicting v-structures resolved by 'last write wins' with no Meek propagation — orientation is order-dependent and can be globally inconsistent
- **detail:** apply_collider_orientation admits (docstring l.170) 'Conflicting triples resolve to the last consistent orientation — a conservative first pass, not a full PC constraint propagation.' Concretely, if edge (P,C) is oriented P->C by one collider and the same edge is claimed C->P (as a parent) by another triple, the dict `oriented[idx]` is simply overwritten by whichever triple is iterated last (iteration is sorted by collider node c, then neighbours). This makes the emitted DAG depend on variable-name sort order, not the data, and can produce an edge set that is not a consistent CPDAG (cycles, contradictory arrowheads) — the very thing PC's orientation rules + Meek propagation exist to prevent. It also never propagates forced orientations (Meek R1-R4), so it leaves orientable edges undirected and directable-only-by-acyclicity edges unresolved.
- **evidence:** orient.py:180-204 (oriented dict overwrite), docstring l.170
- **recommendation:** Run a proper constraint-based orientation: collect all v-structures first, detect conflicts explicitly (flag the edge as unorientable/ambiguous rather than silently overwriting), then apply Meek's rules to propagate and return a valid CPDAG. Make the output invariant to variable ordering.
- **risk:** Non-reproducible, potentially self-contradictory causal skeletons whose arrows flip if variables are renamed/reordered.


### LDO-causal / Rung-2 ITS identification

**`LDO-CAUSAL-ITS-SINGLEBREAK-10` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Single-break, no-anticipation, no-concurrent-intervention ITS is indefensible for national policy shocks in Brazil
- **detail:** The ITS design (quasi.py) identifies ONE break with immediate level + slope change. National policy/epidemic shocks violate every underlying assumption: (1) Anticipation — programmes are announced before implementation, so the outcome moves pre-t0 (e.g. vaccination ramp-ups), biasing b2 toward zero or wrong sign. (2) Phase-in — effects are gradual, not a step; a step model mis-specifies the transition and attributes phase-in to the linear slope. (3) Concurrent interventions — Brazil rarely changes one policy in isolation (SUS reforms, economic crises 2015-16, COVID 2020 all co-occur); a single break cannot separate them, and the negative-control veto (which flags this as 'common shock') then vetoes the very policy effect of interest. (4) COVID (2020-2022) is a structural discontinuity in ALL health series that the single-break detector will lock onto, masking every other shock. detect_structural_break returns exactly one index; there is no multi-break (Bai-Perron), no anticipation window, no phase-in kernel.
- **evidence:** quasi.py:60-73 single-break argmax; §IV l.255 'ITS around dated shocks'
- **recommendation:** Support multiple breaks (Bai-Perron / segmented with unknown breakpoints), model anticipation with a pre-period window and phase-in with a transition function, and require an EXTERNALLY DATED shock (the code already notes 'validated external shock date remains an expert refinement') rather than a data-driven argmax break. For COVID, mask/adjust the 2020-2022 discontinuity explicitly.
- **risk:** Causal effects attributed to the wrong date/policy; COVID swamps everything; anticipation and phase-in bias the reported level change arbitrarily.


### LDO-causal / rung semantics

**`LDO-CAUSAL-RUNG-SEMANTICS-11` · HIGH · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Rung labels overclaim causal content: an auto-applied Rung-2 'quasi-experimental' tag with no adjustment set and no exchangeability check is association wearing a causal badge
- **detail:** The ladder assigns causal_rung=2 with assumptions ('interrupted_time_series','negative_control_outcomes') fully automatically once |level_t|>=3 and the veto does not fire. But a defensible quasi-experimental Rung-2 claim requires: a credibly exogenous shock, a plausible no-confounding-of-the-break argument, correct counterfactual trend, and (for DiD) parallel-trends evidence. None is checked: the break is self-detected (endogenous to the outcome, finding 10), the counterfactual is a linear extrapolation (rarely valid over decades of epidemiological transition), and DiD's parallel-trends assumption is never tested (difference_in_differences just differences means). Meanwhile Rung-1 orientation is licensed by artefactual non-Gaussianity (finding 05) and unverifiable faithfulness (finding 07). So the emitted rung OVERSTATES identification: the MSD's own principle 'strength is explicit and never overstated' (l.258) is violated by auto-promotion. The rung should encode 'what assumptions were CHECKED', but it encodes 'which estimator was RUN'.
- **evidence:** quasi.py:162-169 auto rung=2; orient.py:154-158 auto rung=1; MSD-III §IV l.258
- **recommendation:** Redefine rungs by ASSUMPTIONS VERIFIED, not estimators executed. Auto-Rung-2 only with an external shock date + a passed parallel-trends/pre-trend test + calibrated negative-control null; otherwise emit Rung-1.5 'ITS-run-assumptions-unverified'. Attach the unfalsified assumptions (exogeneity, no-anticipation, counterfactual form) explicitly as UNCHECKED in causal_assumptions.
- **risk:** Downstream consumers read causal_rung=2 as 'quasi-experimentally identified effect' when it is an autocorrelated OLS break on a rank-transformed national average; the honesty contract is breached at the point it matters most.


### LDO-causal / negative-control + ITS

**`LDO-CAUSAL-NC-NEFF-12` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — Break detection and negative-control counts reuse the same min_level_t=3.0 OLS test, so their errors are perfectly correlated — the veto cannot be a genuine independent check
- **detail:** detect_structural_break, the edge ITS, and negative_control_break_fraction all call the identical interrupted_time_series with the same OLS SEs and the same min_level_t=3.0 threshold. Because the SEs are miscalibrated by autocorrelation/seasonality identically across all series, the errors are not independent: at a seasonal peak, the target 'breaks' AND most controls 'break' together for the SAME artefactual reason. The veto then fires — but not because it detected confounding; because the shared miscalibration co-triggers. Conversely, if the SE calibration were fixed only for the target and not the controls (or vice versa), the fraction would be biased. A negative-control test is only informative if its false-positive behaviour is independent of the primary test's; here they are the same test, so the veto adds little true information and mainly reflects seasonality. The reliability machinery (n_eff floors elsewhere in the LDO) is not applied to any of these T-length series.
- **evidence:** quasi.py:71,111,148,155 (shared min_level_t=3.0, same estimator)
- **recommendation:** Decouple the tests (different, properly-calibrated nulls), apply n_eff-based effective-sample deflation to the T-length series, and evaluate the negative-control null empirically (distribution of control effects) rather than counting shared-threshold exceedances. Deseasonalize before any of the three tests.
- **risk:** The veto's apparent protection is illusory — it co-fires with the target on shared artefacts, giving false confidence that common-shock confounding was ruled out.


### LDO-causal / distributional model

**`LDO-CAUSAL-COUNT-DIST-13` · HIGH · enhancement_over_msd** — Entire causal ladder ignores the count/overdispersion nature of the data; Gaussian-OLS everywhere where Poisson/NB with exposure is mandatory
- **detail:** Every estimator here is Gaussian-linear: ITS is OLS on a (gaussianized) mean, DiD differences arithmetic means, collider uses Pearson/partial correlations. But the substrate is sparse counts with structural zeros, overdispersion, and population denominators. On rare outcomes (pancreatic cancer, the stated win-condition) most municipality-months are 0 or 1; a Gaussian model's variance assumption is grossly wrong, and a 'level change' in a mean of near-zero counts is dominated by discreteness. The MSD elsewhere (§III.5) establishes the count-with-exposure denominator principle for the copula margin, but the CAUSAL layer discards it — it operates on the gaussianized or spatial-mean series with NO offset. This is an enhancement opportunity the MSD does not require but should: the causal rungs should inherit the denominator principle.
- **evidence:** quasi.py (OLS/mean estimators); §III.5 count-exposure margin exists but is not used in causal layer; orchestrator.py:339
- **recommendation:** Reformulate ITS/DiD as segmented Poisson or negative-binomial GLMs with log(population) offset and overdispersion (NB or quasi-Poisson), retaining the segmented-regression structure but on the correct likelihood. For rare outcomes use exact/small-area methods (Besag-York-Mollié spatial random effects, empirical-Bayes shrinkage). This directly extends §III.5's denominator principle into Part IV.
- **risk:** For rare-disease national studies (the project's headline goal) the causal effects are dominated by count discreteness and mis-specified variance; NB-vs-Poisson matters more than any of the orientation subtleties.


### LDO-causal / multiple testing

**`LDO-CAUSAL-MHT-14` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — No multiple-testing control across the many edges, break candidates, and control series simultaneously escalated
- **detail:** escalate_rung2_its loops over ALL Rung>=1 edges; for each it scans T-8 break candidates (argmax), and tests up to (p-2) control series. With p variables and E edges this is O(E·T·p) hypothesis tests at a fixed |t|>=3 (~p=0.0013 two-sided) with NO family-wise or FDR control anywhere in the causal layer. At national scale (dozens of variables, hundreds of edges, T up to ~300) the expected number of spurious Rung-2 promotions under the global null is large. The break argmax already inflates per-edge type-I error (finding 03); across edges it compounds. The MSD §IX honesty layer measures a false-alarm rate on known-negative controls but does not impose per-run multiplicity correction on the causal escalations themselves.
- **evidence:** quasi.py:116-170 nested loops, fixed min_level_t=3.0; no FDR/FWER
- **recommendation:** Apply Benjamini-Hochberg (or Benjamini-Yekutieli for dependence) across all edge-level ITS tests within a run, and use sup-Wald critical values for the within-edge break scan. Report the number of tests and the adjusted thresholds in the certification metadata.
- **risk:** A national run will emit a batch of Rung-2 'effects' that are pure multiple-comparisons artefacts, with no accounting for how many tests were run to find them.


### LDO-causal / DiD

**`LDO-CAUSAL-DID-ORPHAN-15` · LOW · implementation_gap** — difference_in_differences is implemented but never wired into escalation, and has no parallel-trends test — dead or dangerously naive if used
- **detail:** difference_in_differences (quasi.py:83-91) computes (treated_post-treated_pre)-(control_post-control_pre) but is not called by escalate_rung2_its or the orchestrator (grep shows only the ITS path is wired at orchestrator.py:340). If it is intended for use it is dangerously minimal: it (1) requires a pre-specified treated/control split and masks with no guidance on choosing valid controls, (2) has NO parallel-trends diagnostic (the core DiD identifying assumption), (3) provides no standard error or inference at all (returns a point effect), and (4) differences raw means with no covariate adjustment or population weighting. A DiDResult with no SE cannot support a Rung-2 claim. Either wire it with proper inference and a pre-trend test, or remove it so it is not mistaken for a validated capability.
- **evidence:** quasi.py:83-91; orchestrator.py:337-340 wires only ITS; MSD-III §IV l.255 lists DiD
- **recommendation:** If DiD is to count, implement it as a two-way fixed-effects / callaway-sant'anna estimator with clustered SEs and a pre-trends test, population-weighted; otherwise delete the stub to avoid an unvalidated causal path being invoked.
- **risk:** Latent capability that, if invoked, emits point 'treatment effects' with no inference and no assumption check — a silent overclaim; if truly unused it is dead code masquerading as MSD coverage.


### edge uncertainty propagation (§V.6(2))

**`LDO-CERT-FISHERZ-01` · CRITICAL · statistical_validity_risk** — Fisher-z SE 1/√(n_eff−3) is the wrong SE for a whitened partial correlation on pairwise-complete data
- **detail:** edges.py:173 sets stat_se = 1/√(max(n_eff−3,1)) where n_eff (_n_eff, line 152) counts localities with ANY finite cell across ANY variable — a single scalar reused for every edge. This is the textbook Fisher-z SE for the transform of a MARGINAL Pearson correlation between two variables on n iid bivariate pairs. Three assumptions are violated simultaneously. (a) The reported weight is a PARTIAL correlation conditioned on the whole lag-extended feature block (F=p·(K+1) features); the correct SE is 1/√(n−k−3) where k is the number of conditioning variables, which at national p with K lags can be dozens — n_eff−3 overstates the effective df and understates the SE. (b) Correlations are estimated pairwise-complete (precision.py min_overlap=20), so each edge's true sample size is its own overlap subset, NOT the global n_eff; a sparse-count edge estimated on 25 co-observed localities carries the SE of a dense edge estimated on 5000. (c) The field is spatially WHITENED (lags.py:110, Σ_space^{-1/2}=(κI+L_W)^{1/2}) before correlating, which is a linear mixing across the S localities — the whitened residuals are not iid draws, so 'n_eff independent pairs' is not even approximately the sample size. The propagated 'uncertainty' is therefore not a calibrated SE; the entire certification conjunction and the exact-vs-approx band are built on it.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §V.6(2); src/pegasus/ldo/edges.py:151-176; src/pegasus/ldo/precision.py:108-119; src/pegasus/ldo/lags.py:109-114
- **recommendation:** Compute a per-edge SE from that edge's actual pairwise overlap n_ij and conditioning-set size: SE_z = 1/√(n_ij − |cond| − 3). Better, replace the analytic Fisher-z with the stability-selection subsample distribution you already compute — the empirical SD of the partial correlation across the n_subsamples refits is a whitening/partial/overlap-aware bootstrap SE at no extra cost. For the whitening-induced dependence, use an effective-sample-size deflation n_eff^spatial = n/(1+2Σρ_k) from the residual spatial autocorrelation rather than the raw locality count.
- **risk:** Every 'selected' edge carries a mis-scaled confidence statement; edges are certified (or the exact-vs-approx band drawn) with SEs that can be off by an order of magnitude for sparse or highly-conditioned edges, directly invalidating the §V.6 validity claim the gate exists to enforce.

**`LDO-CERT-UNITS-02` · HIGH · computational_trap** — stat_se (Fisher-z scale) and numerical_error (relative eigenvalue ratio) are combined in quadrature despite being different units
- **detail:** edges.py:174-176 forms edge_uncertainty = hypot(stat_se, numerical_error). stat_se is 1/√(n−3), an SE on the Fisher-z (arctanh r) scale — dimensionless but interpretable as an SD of a transformed correlation. numerical_error (lowrank.py:264-267) is float(dropped.max()/vals.max()), the largest OMITTED eigenvalue of the low-rank component divided by the top eigenvalue — a relative truncation ratio of the L-factor spectrum. These are not the same quantity: one is the statistical SD of a partial correlation, the other is a relative error of the latent-factor magnitudes. Adding them in quadrature (√(a²+b²)) is only valid for independent quantities measured in the SAME units. Here it conflates 'how noisy is this correlation estimate' with 'what fraction of the low-rank spectrum did randomized-SVD drop', producing an uncertainty band whose magnitude has no defined interpretation. For a directed lagged edge (which does not even touch the low-rank factor readout) numerical_error still gets folded in, inflating its band by a quantity irrelevant to that edge.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/edges.py:172-176; src/pegasus/ldo/lowrank.py:261-267
- **recommendation:** Convert the randomized-SVD truncation bound into the SAME scale as the edge weight before combining: for a partial correlation weight, the Halko–Martinsson–Tropp bound gives an error on the L-entries, which must be pushed through the S/L split and the -S/√(dₐd_b) partial-correlation map to yield a δ(partial_correlation). Only then is quadrature meaningful. For directed/contemporaneous edges that read from S (not L), the low-rank truncation error should propagate through the S-block perturbation, not be added as a raw spectral ratio.
- **risk:** The uncertainty band is a category error; certification and the exact-vs-approx tolerance both consume it, so 'agreement within propagated bounds' is agreement within an uninterpretable number — the validity contract is decorative.

**`LDO-CERT-COPULA-08` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Uncertainty ignores the randomized-PIT copula transform's own sampling noise and its instability for rare-event counts
- **detail:** The latent Gaussian Z is produced by randomized_pit_gaussianize (margins.py:42-63): for tied values (the many zero cells of a rare count) it assigns u=(#less + U·#equal)/n with U~Uniform(0,1), then z=Φ⁻¹(u). This injects genuine Monte-Carlo randomness into every zero cell, and for a rare-event count (e.g. C25 deaths, mostly 0 with occasional 1–2) the ENTIRE latent representation of that variable is dominated by where the uniform draws happened to land within the huge zero-band. Two consequences the uncertainty ignores: (a) the partial correlation is a random function of the PIT seed — its sampling variance across PIT draws is a real, often dominant uncertainty source that is NOWHERE propagated (edge_uncertainty only has Fisher-z + SVD terms). (b) For heavily zero-inflated counts the rank PIT compresses almost all mass into a narrow z-band, inflating apparent partial correlations between two sparse variables that merely share their zero pattern (co-absence), which is an epidemiological artefact of undercount/reporting, not co-occurrence. The Gaussian-copula partial correlation is not robust here and its SE is not the Fisher-z SE of a well-behaved continuous variable.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/margins.py:1-63; src/pegasus/ldo/edges.py:170-176
- **recommendation:** Propagate PIT-transform uncertainty by repeating the fit over several PIT seeds and folding the across-seed SD of each edge into edge_uncertainty (a third quadrature term, and often the largest for sparse counts). For zero-inflated counts prefer a proper discrete copula / Gaussian-copula-with-jitter-marginalization (Dunn–Smyth randomized-quantile residuals averaged over the randomization, per Feng et al.) or a hurdle/zero-inflated model on the margin, so co-absence does not masquerade as dependence.
- **risk:** For exactly the rare-disease targets PegaSUS exists to study, the copula step is the dominant uncertainty and is completely unmodeled; sparse co-absence edges are certified as real associations, and the reported bands are far too narrow.

**`LDO-CERT-SVDERR-13` · MEDIUM · implementation_gap** — numerical_error uses the largest DROPPED eigenvalue ratio, not the Halko–Martinsson–Tropp probabilistic error bound the MSD invokes
- **detail:** MSD §V.3/§V.6(1) require 'bounded, not heuristic' error with a KNOWN distribution, naming Halko–Martinsson–Tropp for the randomized SVD. lowrank.py:264-267 instead sets numerical_error = dropped.max()/vals.max() — the largest eigenvalue that fell below the 2%-of-top keep threshold, divided by the top eigenvalue. This is a truncation-tail heuristic, not the HMT bound. The HMT bound on the randomized rank-r approximation is ‖A−QQᵀA‖ ≤ (1+ε)σ_{r+1} with a probabilistic tail controlled by the oversampling parameter and number of power iterations — i.e. the error depends on the SPECTRAL GAP and the sketch parameters, which the code never reads. dropped.max()/vals.max() can be ZERO (numerical_error=0) whenever the kept set already captures everything above 2% even though the randomized projection itself introduced error in the RETAINED subspace (rotation within a near-degenerate top block), so the reported numerical error understates the true approximation error precisely when eigenvalues are close (the hard case). It is a proxy for 'how much tail did we cut', not 'how wrong is the randomized readout'.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §V.3, §V.6(1); src/pegasus/ldo/lowrank.py:261-267
- **recommendation:** Report the actual HMT-style bound: after the randomized SVD, cheaply estimate ‖A−QQᵀA‖ via a few random probes (posterior error estimation, Halko §4.3) and propagate THAT (with its probabilistic tail as a function of oversampling p and power iterations q) as numerical_error. This gives the 'known distribution' the MSD demands and correctly captures the near-degenerate case where the subspace, not the tail magnitude, is uncertain.
- **risk:** The propagated numerical error is a heuristic that reads 0 exactly in the ill-conditioned near-degenerate regime where randomized SVD is least reliable, so the uncertainty band is narrowest where it should be widest, violating the §V.6(1) 'bounded, known distribution' requirement the certification rests on.


### exact-certifies-approximate (§V.6(3))

**`LDO-CERT-SLICE-SCOPE-04` · CRITICAL · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Exact-certifies-approximate on a same-slice re-run certifies the METHOD, not the national data scope the MSD claims
- **detail:** The MSD §V.6(3) claim is 'a state-scale exact run MUST agree (within bounds) with the national approximate run where they overlap.' The implementation (holdout.py:70-75) instead takes ONE spatial slice sl, runs fit_lagged_links(sl, randomized_factors=True) AND fit_lagged_links(sl, randomized_factors=False) on the SAME slice, and compares. This certifies only that randomized-SVD ≈ exact-eigh ON IDENTICAL DATA — i.e. that the randomized NLA is faithful to the exact NLA on p>48 features. It says NOTHING about whether the national-scope approximation (national S≈5570 with all its spatial heterogeneity, epidemiological-transition gradients, and undercount structure) recovers the same edges the exact math would recover at national scope. The dominant approximation error at national scale is not the SVD truncation but the fact that the national fit conditions on a totally different variable/locality population than any state slice. Two edges present in both scopes can legitimately differ because the confounding set differs (Simpson-type reversal across scope), and this is precisely what a real cross-scale certification should catch — and precisely what a same-slice A/B cannot.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §V.6(3); src/pegasus/validation/holdout.py:42-78; src/pegasus/ldo/orchestrator.py:221-237
- **recommendation:** Split the two claims. Keep the same-slice A/B as a 'randomized-NLA fidelity' check (rename it so). Separately implement the ACTUAL §V.6(3) contract: fit the national approximate run, fit an EXACT state-scale run on a genuine geographic state (e.g. a full UF), and compare the edges whose endpoints are present in both — accepting that scope-driven differences are a finding about identifiability, not a numerical rejection. Report cross-scope disagreements as 'scope-sensitive edges' (candidates for latent confounding by out-of-slice variables), which is scientifically the more valuable output.
- **risk:** The system advertises a cross-scale validity guarantee it does not provide; a national approximation that is numerically faithful but scientifically wrong (because national conditioning differs from state conditioning) passes certification, and the MSD's headline validity contract is unmet while appearing met.

**`LDO-CERT-LATENTBAND-03` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — exact-vs-approx band compares latent_shared LOADINGS as if they were partial correlations
- **detail:** exact_certify.py:55-61 keys edges by (endpoints, lag, edge_type) and for each overlap compares diff=|w_approx−w_exact| to band=tolerance_sigma·hypot(σ_a,σ_e). For edge_type=='latent_shared' the weight is min(|load_i|,|load_j|) (lowrank.py:282) — a factor LOADING, not a partial correlation — while both σ values are the same Fisher-z-derived edge_uncertainty (a correlation SE). So the band is a correlation-scale tolerance applied to a loading-scale difference. Randomized vs exact SVD legitimately produce loadings that differ by a sign flip or a rotation within a degenerate eigenspace (eigenvectors are only defined up to sign, and up to arbitrary rotation when eigenvalues are close). A sign flip alone makes |w_approx−w_exact|≈2|load|, guaranteeing a spurious 'disagreement' that in strict mode raises ApproximationRejectedError and aborts the national run — or in non-strict mode falsely reports the approximation rejected. The comparison is not sign/rotation invariant and the band units don't match the weight units.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/exact_certify.py:33-66; src/pegasus/ldo/lowrank.py:273-295
- **recommendation:** For latent_shared, compare subspace angles (principal angles between the exact and approximate factor subspaces) or |load| magnitudes with sign canonicalization, not raw signed loading differences; and derive the band from a loading-scale SE (jackknife of the loading across subsamples), not the correlation Fisher-z. Alternatively exclude latent_shared from the weight-difference certification and certify it via its own subspace-agreement prong.
- **risk:** In strict mode a benign SVD sign flip on a shared-factor pair aborts the entire national LDO with a false 'approximation rejected'; in non-strict mode it fills the disagreement report with phantom failures, discrediting a valid approximation.

**`LDO-CERT-SLICE-BIAS-05` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — The certification slice is the max-coverage (least-sparse) 700 localities — the opposite of the population where the approximation is stressed
- **detail:** _spatial_slice (holdout.py:26-39) picks the max_slice_S=700 localities with the MOST finite cells (argsort(-coverage)). This is a deliberately non-representative subsample: the densest, best-observed, typically largest-population municipalities. Randomized-SVD error and sparse-count copula distortion are both WORST in sparse, low-count, small-population localities — exactly the ones excluded. So the certification runs where the approximation is easiest and the copula is best-behaved, then generalizes the pass to the national run whose bulk is the sparse tail. This is a favorable-case validation, not a worst-case bound. For Brazilian municipal data the ~4000 small municipalities with rare-event counts (e.g. pancreatic cancer deaths, 0–2/yr) are the regime that breaks the Gaussian copula and the low-rank readout, and they are systematically absent from the slice.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/validation/holdout.py:26-39,66-73
- **recommendation:** Certify on a STRATIFIED slice: sample localities across the coverage/population distribution (some dense, some sparse) so the exact-vs-approx comparison spans the regime where the approximation actually fails. Report the disagreement rate as a function of locality sparsity, and set the pass criterion on the sparse stratum, not the aggregate. The 700 magic number should be derived from the exact-eigh affordability budget, not hardcoded.
- **risk:** The approximation is certified on the friendly 12% of the data and deployed on the hostile 88%; sparse-locality edge distortions (the dominant error source in municipal count data) never enter the certification, so the loud-rejection guarantee is silent exactly where it is needed.

**`LDO-CERT-BAND-ZERO-12` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — Zero-uncertainty edges make the agreement band collapse to 0, so any float difference disagrees; and missing overlap silently passes
- **detail:** certify_exact_vs_approx (exact_certify.py:57-61): sa/se default to 0.0 when uncertainty is None, so band = tolerance_sigma·hypot(0,0) = 0, and the comment concedes 'band==0 → any nonzero diff disagrees'. Since the exact run has numerical_error=0 by construction (lowrank.py:266 gates on use_randomized) and the statistical term is the SAME Fisher-z scalar, an exact edge whose stat_se is legitimately present avoids this — but any edge that reaches this comparator with uncertainty=None (e.g. an edge with n_eff falsy, edges.py:173 yields stat_se=None → edge_uncertainty=None) gets a zero band and disagrees on the tiniest floating-point difference between two independent fits, manufacturing a false rejection. Symmetrically, the certification only inspects the INTERSECTION set(a)&set(e) (exact_certify.py:53): an edge that the approximate national run promotes but that is ABSENT from the exact slice run (a false edge the approximation invented) is not in the overlap and is never flagged — the certification is blind to approximation-induced FALSE POSITIVES, catching only weight disagreements on shared edges. n_disagree can be 0 ('certified': True) while the approximation hallucinated edges the exact run never saw.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/exact_certify.py:51-84; src/pegasus/ldo/edges.py:173-176
- **recommendation:** Floor the band at a minimum resolvable difference (e.g. edge_threshold or a small epsilon) so a zero-uncertainty edge does not disagree on numerical dust. Crucially, add a SET-difference prong: report edges present in the approximate run but absent in the exact run (approximation false positives) and edges present in exact but missed by approximate (false negatives) — these are the scientifically important approximation failures and are currently invisible. Certification should require both weight-agreement on the overlap AND bounded set symmetric-difference.
- **risk:** A national approximation that invents spurious edges (or drops real ones) relative to the exact math passes certification as long as the edges it happens to SHARE agree; the 'loud rejection' never fires for the most damaging failure mode (false edges), and a zero-band edge can trigger a false rejection on floating-point noise.


### certification gate (§III.8)

**`LDO-CERT-STABTHRESH-06` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Stability threshold is an uncalibrated magic number (0.6 in policy, 0.5 in readout) with no Meinshausen–Bühlmann error control
- **detail:** The MSD (§III.7) claims stability selection 'controls the edge-set error without per-pair testing' — invoking Meinshausen–Bühlmann. But MB error control requires a specific construction: complementary-pair subsampling at fraction exactly 1/2, and the per-family-error-rate bound PFER ≤ (1/(2π_thr−1))·(q²/p) where q is the average number of selected variables per subsample and π_thr the frequency threshold. The implementation uses subsample_frac=0.7 (edges.py stability_select default, orchestrator passes its own), NOT 1/2; draws simple subsamples, NOT complementary pairs; and then applies two DIFFERENT thresholds — LDOCertificationPolicy.min_stability=0.6 (certify.py:26) and to_link_records stability_threshold=0.5 (edges.py:161) — neither derived from a PFER target. So there is no q, no p²-scaled bound, no error-rate guarantee: the 'stability selection' is a recurrence heuristic wearing MB's name. The 0.5/0.6 discrepancy also means an edge with stability 0.55 is 'selected' at readout (edges.py:187) but the gate policy's 0.6 floor would demote it — two layers disagree on the same guarantee.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §III.7; src/pegasus/ldo/certify.py:26; src/pegasus/ldo/edges.py:88,161,187
- **recommendation:** Either implement genuine MB: complementary-pair subsampling at frac=1/2, compute q (mean selected edges/subsample), invert the PFER bound for the user's target expected false edges to SET π_thr rather than guessing it, and report the implied PFER. Or drop the MB claim from the docstrings and present stability as an explicitly heuristic recurrence filter. Reconcile the 0.5/0.6 to a single threshold used by both readout and gate.
- **risk:** The system claims finite-sample edge-set error control it does not have; users read 'stability-selected, MB-controlled' and treat the skeleton as multiplicity-controlled when it is an uncalibrated threshold, and the 0.5-vs-0.6 gap silently double-classifies borderline edges.

**`LDO-CERT-MULTITEST-07` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — No multiple-testing control over the p²·(K+1) simultaneous edge tests; FDR fields are plumbed but unused
- **detail:** The LDO screens on the order of p²·(K+1) candidate edges (every ordered variable pair at every lag, plus contemporaneous). Promotion depends on |partial_correlation|≥edge_threshold (lags.py:170,189) plus a stability frequency — neither is a test with controlled type-I error across the family. The LinkRecord carries null_strategy and fdr_method fields (edges.py:163, records.py) and to_link_records accepts them, but they are passed through as labels (default None) and NOTHING in certify.py or the gate computes a p-value or applies Benjamini–Hochberg/Benjamini–Yekutieli. The MSD's position is that stability selection REPLACES per-pair testing, but as shown in LDO-CERT-STABTHRESH-06 the stability construction does not deliver an error-rate bound, so the family is effectively uncontrolled: at national p (dozens–hundreds of disease/context variables) even a 0.6 recurrence threshold will pass a non-trivial number of null edges by chance across p² comparisons.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/edges.py:161-204; src/pegasus/ldo/records.py:27,59; src/pegasus/ldo/certify.py (no FDR computation)
