- **recommendation:** Add a genuine multiplicity layer: for each candidate edge derive a p-value from the (corrected) Fisher-z or from the stability-subsample null, then apply Benjamini–Yekutieli (dependence-robust, since edges are correlated) at a stated FDR q. Populate the existing fdr_method field with the actual method and store the adjusted p-value per record. Use FDR as a SECOND conjunct alongside stability, not a substitute.
- **risk:** Across hundreds of thousands of implicit edge tests, a heuristic recurrence threshold yields an uncontrolled expected number of false discoveries; the promoted skeleton contains chance edges presented as certified hypotheses, undermining every downstream causal-escalation rung.

**`LDO-CERT-CONJ-CONFIRM-11` · MEDIUM · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Certification is a conjunction of confirmatory filters with no aggregate error budget; conjuncts are treated as independent when they are correlated
- **detail:** certify_link (certify.py:30-72) promotes an edge to 'selected' iff it clears a chain of ANDed gates: not low-n_eff, not unconverged, not low-path-agreement, not latent-lag-confound, stability≥0.6, and uncertainty present. The MSD (§III.8) frames these as complementary anti-spurious gates, but statistically a conjunction of thresholded filters has an uncontrolled and NON-additive effect on both false-positive and false-negative rates: (a) the conjuncts are strongly correlated (a sparse low-n_eff edge also tends to fail stability and path-agreement), so ANDing them does not multiply the type-I control the way independent tests would — the effective stringency is much less than the product of the individual pass rates; (b) more importantly, each additional AND raises the false-NEGATIVE (missed real edge) rate, and nowhere is the power cost accounted — a genuine lagged mechanism that happens to share a weak latent factor (LDO-CERT-... latent flag) is unconditionally demoted (certify.py:48) even when the lagged partial correlation is strong and stable. There is no aggregate error budget balancing the four conjuncts; each was added defensively.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/certify.py:30-72; src/pegasus/ldo/certgates.py:60-73; MSD-III §III.8
- **recommendation:** Replace the hard AND-chain with a calibrated combined score (e.g. a logistic or rank aggregation over the conjunct signals) tuned on planted-edge synthetic data to a target (sensitivity, FDR) operating point, so the trade-off is explicit and measured rather than emergent from four independent thresholds. At minimum, report per-conjunct pass/fail per edge (not just a single status) so a strong, stable lagged edge demoted solely by a weak latent-flag is visible and reviewable rather than silently descriptive.
- **risk:** The gate's aggregate operating characteristics (sensitivity, FDR) are unknown and untuned; defensive conjuncts stack into an over-conservative filter that silently drops real edges (notably real lagged mechanisms with incidental shared factors) while providing no quantified false-positive control.


### §III.8 conjunct: regularization-path agreement

**`LDO-CERT-PATHGRID-09` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — Regularization-path agreement over a 3-point λ grid (×0.5,×1,×2) with floor 0.66 is a coarse binary vote, not a stability path
- **detail:** regularization_path_agreement (certgates.py:35-57) refits at base_lambda1×{0.5, 2.0} plus the base and scores each edge by the fraction of the 3 grid points on which it appears; apply_certification_gates (certgates.py:81,91) demotes edges below min_path_agreement=0.66. With only 3 points the achievable scores are {1/3, 2/3, 1}, so the 0.66 floor is exactly 'must appear at ≥2 of 3' — a single point of disagreement (e.g. dropping out at ×2 where the ℓ1 penalty is strongest and legitimately kills weak edges) demotes an otherwise-robust edge. Conversely, a threshold artefact that happens to survive at ×0.5 and ×1 but not ×2 scores 2/3 and passes. The grid multipliers 0.5/2.0 and the 0.66 floor are magic numbers with no relation to the actual λ range over which the graphical lasso is identifiable, and a 3-point grid cannot distinguish a monotone edge-death (expected, benign) from a non-monotone flicker (the real artefact signature).
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/certgates.py:35-57,76-96
- **recommendation:** Use a denser log-spaced λ path (≥8 points spanning the full regularization range from near-saturated to near-empty), and score edges by the LENGTH of the contiguous λ-interval over which they persist (a genuine stability path / regularization persistence), not a 3-point vote. Demote edges whose selection is non-monotone in λ (flickering), which is the actual artefact signature, and keep edges with a long stable interval even if they die at high λ.
- **risk:** The path-agreement conjunct is too coarse to do its job: it demotes robust weak edges (monotone death at high λ) and passes genuine threshold artefacts (flicker within 2/3), so the conjunct adds noise rather than the fragility control the MSD intends.


### temporal holdout (§IX.3)

**`LDO-CERT-HOLDOUT-NULL-10` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Persistence rate has no null distribution — a high value can arise from trend/autocorrelation, and sign-only matching is a weak test
- **detail:** temporal_holdout (holdout.py:98-144) fits on the training window and the holdout window, then reports persistence_rate = fraction of training edges whose SAME-SIGN edge recurs in the holdout window. There is no null distribution: the docstring asserts 'a real link persists / predicts; a fluke evaporates', but a fluke need NOT evaporate. Two variables that both carry a strong secular trend (epidemiological transition — cancer mortality rising, infectious falling over 2000–2025) will show a persistent same-sign partial correlation in BOTH windows purely from co-trending, with no mechanism. Sign-only matching (np.sign of weight, holdout.py:94) is the weakest possible agreement: for a symmetric null the sign matches ~50% of the time by chance, so a persistence_rate near 0.5 is exactly the null expectation, yet nothing compares the observed rate to that baseline. The holdout also uses adjacent time windows (train = years 1..T−1, test = last year), so temporal autocorrelation leaks the training signal directly into the 'held-out' year. And no p-value or CI is attached to the rate.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/validation/holdout.py:90-144; MSD-III §IX.3
- **recommendation:** Attach a null: permute the holdout-window time labels (or use a block/circular permutation preserving autocorrelation) to build the persistence-rate null distribution and report an empirical p-value, so a rate is judged against its by-chance baseline (~0.5 for sign-only). Detrend/difference the series before the holdout fit so co-trending does not manufacture persistence. Prefer a quantitative agreement (weight correlation across windows, or predictive out-of-sample partial-correlation) over a sign match, and use a gap between train and test windows to reduce autocorrelation leakage.
- **risk:** The headline temporal-validity number is uninterpretable: co-trending epidemiological-transition variables produce high persistence with no mechanism, sign-only matching floors the null at ~0.5, and adjacent-window leakage inflates the rate — so 'the edge persisted out of sample' is asserted without evidence it beats chance.


### certification gate (§III.8) — enhancement over MSD

**`LDO-CERT-ENH-CONFORMAL-14` · LOW · enhancement_over_msd** — Replace the analytic-Fisher-z + heuristic-stability gate with subsampling-bootstrap conformal edge inference
- **detail:** The whole certification stack reinvents, heuristically, machinery that has distribution-free guarantees. The stability_select refits (edges.py:61-148) already produce, for every edge, an empirical distribution of its partial correlation across Place×Time subsamples — this is a ready-made bootstrap/subsampling ensemble. Rather than (a) an analytic Fisher-z SE with the wrong df (LDO-CERT-FISHERZ-01), (b) a magic 0.6 recurrence threshold with a false MB claim (LDO-CERT-STABTHRESH-06), and (c) no multiplicity control (LDO-CERT-MULTITEST-07), a single conformal/subsampling framework yields all three with guarantees: use the subsample partial-correlation ensemble to build a per-edge conformal prediction interval (distribution-free finite-sample coverage under exchangeability of subsamples), promote an edge iff its interval excludes 0 at a level Bonferroni/BY-adjusted across the p² family, and use the interval half-width AS the edge uncertainty (automatically whitening/partial/copula-aware because it is computed on the actual fitted quantity). This collapses five fragile components into one coherent, guaranteed procedure.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/edges.py:61-148; src/pegasus/ldo/certify.py:30-72; MSD-III §III.7-III.8, §V.6(2)
- **recommendation:** Build edge uncertainty and promotion from the stability-subsample ensemble via subsampling/conformal inference: per-edge interval = conformal quantiles of the subsample partial correlations; certify iff the multiplicity-adjusted interval excludes zero; export the half-width as the propagated uncertainty. This makes uncertainty, selection, and multiple-testing one internally-consistent, distribution-free object and removes every magic number in this subsystem.
- **risk:** Not a defect but a missed unification: the current stack maintains five independently-fragile heuristics (Fisher-z SE, quadrature units, 0.5/0.6 thresholds, absent FDR, tail-ratio numerical error) where one subsampling-conformal construction — computable from data already generated — would give calibrated, guaranteed, mutually-consistent results.


### copula margins feeding certification — enhancement over MSD

**`LDO-CERT-ENH-NB-15` · MEDIUM · enhancement_over_msd** — Gaussian-copula partial correlations on rank-PIT counts should be replaced by negative-binomial / small-area-shrunk margins before certification
- **detail:** The certification gate can only be as valid as the partial correlations it certifies, and those come from a Gaussian copula over randomized-PIT counts (margins.py). For sparse municipal counts this is doubly fragile: (a) the Poisson/Gaussian assumption ignores the strong overdispersion of disease counts (variance >> mean from spatial heterogeneity and reporting), so partial correlations are attenuated/inflated unpredictably; (b) raw small-area rates are dominated by sampling noise (a municipality with 3 expected deaths has enormous relative variance), so the copula-latent Z of a rare-event variable is mostly noise, and any edge to it is a noise edge that stability selection may nonetheless find 'recurrent' because the noise pattern (driven by population size, which persists across subsamples) is itself stable. Certifying such an edge is certifying a population-size artefact. The MSD stops at a Gaussian copula; the epidemiological standard for exactly this data (rare events over Brazilian municipalities with strong spatial structure) is negative-binomial or Poisson-lognormal margins with BYM2/INLA-SPDE small-area shrinkage.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/margins.py:1-63; MSD-III §III.5, §III.7; edges.py:170-204
- **recommendation:** Before the copula, shrink small-area rates with a negative-binomial or Poisson-lognormal BYM2 model (INLA/SPDE, or a lightweight empirical-Bayes shrinkage toward the spatial neighborhood mean), so the latent field reflects the smoothed signal rather than population-size noise. Then the Gaussian copula operates on stabilized rates and stability selection cannot lock onto a population-size pattern. Carry the shrinkage posterior SD into the edge uncertainty. This directly protects certification from the dominant municipal-count artefact.
- **risk:** Population-size-driven noise patterns in rare-event counts are stable across subsamples and thus pass stability selection, so the gate certifies artefactual edges to rare-disease variables — the exact failure mode most dangerous for the national C25/Zika targets PegaSUS is built to study.


### q-tensor / n_eff

**`NEFF-KISH-WEIGHT-01` · CRITICAL · statistical_validity_risk** — Live n_eff uses rate VALUES as Kish weights, not the denominator — a dimensionless, meaningless effective sample size for every rate field
- **detail:** MSD-I §3.12.3 defines n_eff = (Σw)²/Σw² with w = μ_den(s,t) (the denominator) for rate/proportion variables and w = ν_num (the count) for count variables. The live compile path (compile_attach.py _vector_diagnostics, lines 252-253) instead computes _kish_effective_n(observed) where `observed` is the field's own VALUE vector — i.e. the per-cell RATE for an RN intensive_density field. So for a mortality rate it computes (Σ rate_i)²/Σ rate_i², which is not an effective sample size at all: it is dominated by cells with the largest rates (typically the smallest-population, noisiest municipalities — the exact opposite of Kish's intent, which is to DOWN-weight concentration). q_tensor.compute_q_state (line 217) does prefer denom_values when present (`weights = denom_values if denom_values else values`), but the primary live producer is compile_attach, which never passes the denominator vector as weights — it only threads a scalar support['n_denom'] that is not used in the Kish sum. Consequence: n_eff is systematically wrong for every rate, and since n_eff drives the verified/fragile/quarantine gate (§3.12.14), the state classification of every intensive field is decided by a statistic with no valid interpretation. A rate that is spatially uniform (all cells ≈ equal) gets n_eff ≈ number of cells regardless of the underlying population, so a rate built on 50 tiny municipalities can be certified 'verified' (n_eff≥100 impossible, but n_eff≥30 fragile is easily reached) while carrying almost no information.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §3.12.3 (lines 4919-4943); src/pegasus/efg/compile_attach.py:252-253 (_kish over `observed` value vector); q_tensor.py:217 (weights fallback); compile_attach.py:318 (n_denom threaded but unused in Kish)
- **recommendation:** In _vector_diagnostics, pass the actual per-cell denominator vector (the MeasuredQuantity exposure column, already materialized as the sidecar) as the Kish weights for intensive/rate fields, and the numerator count vector for extensive fields — never the ratio value. Route the RN sidecar's `exposure` series into compute_q_state's `denominator` argument at the compile boundary. Add a contract test: a rate field whose n_eff is computed must equal the Kish n_eff of its exposure column, not its value column.
- **risk:** Every rate field's certification is decided by a meaningless number; small-population high-rate municipalities are systematically over-certified, and the entire verified/fragile honesty layer is invalid for intensive quantities — the LDO then trusts rates it should quarantine.


### denominator fragility

**`DENOMFRAG-SPEC-DIVERGE-01` · HIGH · implementation_gap** — denom_fragility measures MISSING-denominator share, not the §3.12.8 below-μ_min share — a small denominator (n=3) counts as perfectly non-fragile
- **detail:** MSD-I §3.12.8 defines denom_fragility = share of cells with μ_den(s,t) < μ_min, μ_min=50 by default (raised for highly stratified cells). Its purpose is precisely to flag rates built on tiny denominators, where Poisson/binomial variance explodes. The implementation in kernels.py _compute_rn_ratio (lines 361-362) instead computes denom_fragility = share of joined cells whose denominator is NULL or NaN (a JOIN-COVERAGE metric). A cell with population = 3 has a non-null denominator and contributes 0 to fragility, so a cause-specific race×age×sex rate over municipalities of population 3–20 is scored denom_fragility = 0.0 (fully robust) as long as the population panel covers those cells. μ_min=50 appears nowhere in the code (grep confirms no threshold). This is the single most important small-area-reliability guard in the spec and it is silently replaced by a coverage check. Given Brazilian municipal C25 data (pancreatic cancer: ~0–1 deaths/year in most of the 5570 municipalities, denominators in the hundreds once stratified by age×sex), essentially every stratified rate has near-zero true denominators yet scores denom_fragility ≈ 0.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §3.12.8 (lines 4997-5015, μ_min=50); src/pegasus/efg/executor/kernels.py:361-362 (null/nan share, not threshold share); grep: no μ_min/50 threshold anywhere in efg/
- **recommendation:** Compute denom_fragility per §3.12.8 as mean(1{exposure < μ_min}) over materialized cells, μ_min=50 configurable per family and auto-raised with stratification depth (e.g. μ_min scaled by product of strata cardinalities). Keep the null-share as a SEPARATE 'denom_coverage_gap' metric — both matter, but they are different failure modes. Better still (enhancement): replace the hard threshold with a continuous fragility = mean over cells of Var-inflation factor 1/μ (or the width of an exact Poisson/Clopper–Pearson interval on the rate), which degrades smoothly and has a probabilistic meaning.
- **risk:** Rates on unusably small denominators are certified robust; the μ_min=50 small-area safeguard is inoperative on exactly the sparse-count municipal data the system exists to analyze; spurious 'hotspots' from 1-death-over-population-8 cells pass into the LDO as verified.


### n_eff spatial correction

**`MORAN-DOUBLECORRECT-01` · HIGH · msd_theoretical_fragility** — The 1/(1+MoranI) n_eff deflation double-counts spatial dependence the GMRF precision already models, and rests on an ad-hoc formula with no sampling-theory basis
- **detail:** The MSD applies n_eff_final = n_eff_Kish · 1/(1+max(0,MoranI)) (§3.12.3), and _moran_corrected_n_eff (q_tensor.py:165-175) implements it. Two problems. (1) NO DERIVATION: the standard variance-inflation result for a mean under autocorrelation is n_eff ≈ n·(1-ρ)/(1+ρ) for an AR(1)/CAR field, or n·(1 - I) type corrections — none of which is 1/(1+I). The chosen form is a monotone-decreasing heuristic with the right qualitative sign but arbitrary magnitude: at I=1 it deflates by exactly 2, at I=0.3 by 1.3, numbers with no relation to the actual effective independent count of a CAR field with that autocorrelation. (2) DOUBLE-CORRECTION: downstream, the LDO whitens with a spatial GMRF precision (κI + L_W) (per memory 'Wire spatial GMRF whitening') that explicitly models the same spatial dependence. Deflating n_eff for autocorrelation AND then modeling autocorrelation in the precision penalizes the same structure twice — the field is first declared to carry less information because it is smooth, then the model is told it is smooth. Kish n_eff already accounts for weight concentration; Moran deflation on top of a spatial random effect is not standard practice and biases certification toward quarantine for genuinely smooth (well-measured, spatially-coherent) fields — the opposite of what small-area estimation wants (smoothness is signal, not redundancy).
- **evidence:** MSD-I §3.12.3 (line 4930, the 1/(1+MoranI) factor); q_tensor.py:165-175; memory: spatial GMRF whitening wired into LDO estimator
- **recommendation:** Either (a) drop the Moran deflation from n_eff entirely and let the GMRF precision be the single locus of spatial-dependence accounting (n_eff then = Kish effective count, an honest design-based ESS), or (b) if a design-based ESS is wanted, replace the ad-hoc factor with a derived one: for a CAR/AR field estimate the variance inflation factor VIF = 1'Σ1/n (Σ the modeled spatial covariance) and set n_eff = n/VIF. Document that n_eff is a DESCRIPTIVE gate, not fed into the likelihood, to make explicit that it does not double-count with the GMRF. Add a test pinning n_eff to a derived VIF on a synthetic CAR field of known ρ.
- **risk:** Smooth, well-measured spatial fields are wrongly deflated toward quarantine; the certification gate and the model disagree about how much information a field carries; the magic 1/(1+I) form makes the verified/fragile boundary depend on an un-derived constant.


### RN ecological guard

**`ECOFALLACY-GUARD-01` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — The ecological-fallacy guard only aggregates numerator to denominator geography — it does NOT prevent ecological inference across the demographic strata it silently broadcasts
- **detail:** The guard in _compute_rn_ratio (kernels.py:334-341) handles ONE mismatch: if the numerator has municipality_cod6 and the denominator does not, it sums the numerator up to the denominator's coarser geography. That is a support-alignment fix, not an ecological-fallacy fix. The genuine ecological hazard is in the STRATIFIER broadcast (kernels.py:352-359, and operators.py:404-411): cause/ICD/demographic strata present on the numerator but ABSENT from the denominator are carried through as output columns while the join broadcasts the SAME denominator across all strata (how='left' on keys that exclude the strata). So an icd_chapter-stratified death count is divided by the WHOLE-POPULATION denominator, producing a 'cause-specific rate' whose denominator is the total population — mathematically fine for a cause-specific mortality rate, but the code path is identical for a numerator stratified on an axis the denominator genuinely differs on (e.g. numerator by age_group where the population IS age-structured but the specific population field supplied is the crude total). There is no check that a stratifier broadcast against a crude denominator is epidemiologically the intended estimand vs. an ecological error. The guard also never fires the reverse case (denominator finer than numerator), and it hard-codes the single column name 'municipality_cod6' — any other geography grain (cod7, region, UF) silently bypasses the guard entirely.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/efg/executor/kernels.py:334-341 (geo-only guard, hard-coded municipality_cod6); kernels.py:352-359 + operators.py:404-411 (unchecked strata broadcast); align.py:141-146 (denominator_extra_projectable path)
- **recommendation:** Make the guard stratifier-aware: for every axis on the numerator absent from the denominator, require an explicit declaration in the ratio rule of whether that axis is 'broadcast-legal' (cause-specific rate uses crude denom by design) or 'requires-stratified-denominator' (age/sex/race rates MUST divide the correspondingly-stratified population, else refuse). Generalize the geography aggregation to any declared geo grain via the axis registry, not a literal string. Emit a per-field 'ecological_broadcast_axes' provenance tag so the LDO and dashboard know which axes were broadcast rather than matched.
- **risk:** An age- or sex-stratified numerator can be divided by a crude population denominator and reported as a demographic-specific rate — a textbook ecological inference error — with no warning, and any non-municipality_cod6 geography mismatch bypasses the only guard that exists.


### CV state field

**`CV-COUNT-VS-SAMPLE-01` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — Live CV is the cross-cell sample coefficient of variation (spatial heterogeneity), not the §3.12.9 sampling CV of the estimate — they measure opposite things
- **detail:** MSD-I §3.12.9 defines CV as the sampling coefficient of variation of the ESTIMATE: for Poisson counts CV(Y)=1/√(Y+ε), for rates a delta-method Var(Y/μ), for binomial √(p̂(1-p̂)/n). This is a per-cell measurement-precision quantity (how noisy is THIS estimate). Both implementations instead compute the sample CV ACROSS cells: q_tensor._cv (lines 80-89) and compile_attach._vector_diagnostics (lines 241-244) compute std(values)/mean(values) over the cell vector — i.e. spatial/temporal heterogeneity of the field, not the sampling noise of any estimate. A perfectly-measured field with real geographic variation (e.g. income, which varies a lot across municipalities but is precisely known) gets a HIGH CV and is penalized as imprecise; a uniformly-tiny-count field (all cells ≈ 1 death) gets a LOW cross-cell CV yet each cell is maximally Poisson-noisy. The two quantities are nearly anti-correlated. Because CV is reported in Q and consumed downstream as a precision signal, this inverts the intended meaning.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §3.12.9 (lines 5017-5052, per-estimate sampling CV); q_tensor.py:80-89; compile_attach.py:241-244 (cross-cell std/mean)
- **recommendation:** Compute the §3.12.9 sampling CV per cell from the count and exposure (1/√Y for counts; delta-method for rates) and aggregate (e.g. exposure-weighted mean or median) to the field level. Keep the cross-cell dispersion as a distinct 'spatial_heterogeneity' descriptor if useful, clearly named. Do not overload one 'cv' field with two incompatible meanings.
- **risk:** Precisely-measured but genuinely-variable fields are flagged imprecise; noisy sparse-count fields look precise; any downstream weighting or gating on CV is inverted relative to the spec's intent.


### legality algebra closure

**`ALGEBRA-CLOSURE-DIV-01` · MEDIUM · msd_theoretical_fragility** — The kind/aggregation algebra is not closed: divergence(log-ratio) and Ψ-functional outputs are 'non_aggregable' terminal leaves with no legal recomposition, and no operator produces a legal ratio-of-ratios / interaction
- **detail:** The operator registry (operators.py:54-103) types outputs into {extensive_measure/additive, intensive_density/weighted_mean, marked_functional/statistical_functional, bridge_divergence/non_aggregable, observer_proxy/non_aggregable, context_gradient/additive}. The composition rules (legality.py) only admit specific input aggregations: PROJECT requires additive (line 260); RN requires both parents additive (line 285). This means the algebra is a shallow tree, not a closed category: (1) An RN output (intensive, weighted_mean) can NEVER be a numerator or denominator again — so a ratio-of-ratios (e.g. rate-ratio, standardized mortality ratio SMR = observed/expected where expected is itself a rate×population) has NO legal operator; the type system silently makes SMR unconstructible rather than typing it. (2) DIVERGENCE and Ψ outputs are non_aggregable dead-ends — they cannot be projected/marginalized even when doing so is legal (a mean-of-means with weights is a well-defined weighted_mean recomposition, but statistical_functional is not admitted by PROJECT). (3) There is no interaction/product operator, yet §III.4 effect-modification requires interaction terms — the algebra cannot express the very product it later needs. The type system is SOUND (it rejects illegal ops) but INCOMPLETE (it also rejects a class of legal, needed ops), which the MSD-III claim 'retained in full … is sound' overstates.
- **evidence:** operators.py:54-103 (output kinds); legality.py:259-291 (only additive parents admitted to PROJECT/RN); no product/interaction operator in EFGOperator enum; MSD-III §II.3 (line 125) 'sound and retained in full'; §III.4 effect-modification interaction terms
- **recommendation:** Extend the algebra with (a) a typed RATIO_OF_RATIOS / STANDARDIZE operator for SMR/SIR with expected-count semantics (indirect standardization), typed intensive; (b) a weighted-recompose rule so statistical_functional and weighted_mean outputs can be re-aggregated with their exposure weights (mean-of-means is legal given the weights are carried); (c) a first-class INTERACTION operator producing a typed product term for §III.4. Prove closure by a table: for every (op, input-kind) pair, either a legal output kind or an explicit refusal reason — and test that the refusals are 'illegal' not merely 'unimplemented'.
- **risk:** Standardized ratios (SMR/SIR — the workhorse of small-area cancer epidemiology) and effect-modification interactions cannot be expressed in the EFG at all; the 'soundness' claim hides that legal, essential estimands are silently unconstructible.


### divergence operator

**`DIVERGENCE-EPSILON-01` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — Fixed additive epsilon=1e-9 in the log-ratio divergence makes structural zeros produce enormous spurious divergences unbounded by the data scale
- **detail:** The divergence operator computes log((left+ε)/(right+ε)) with ε=1e-9 hard-coded (operators.py:342, kernels.py:613-615). For sparse municipal counts, a structural zero on one side (0 deaths in a small municipality that HAD hospitalizations) yields log((0+1e-9)/(k+1e-9)) ≈ log(1e-9/k) ≈ -21 - log(k): a divergence of magnitude ~20+ that is an artifact of the epsilon, not a real morbidity/mortality signal. Conversely log((k+1e-9)/(0+1e-9)) ≈ +21. So every cell where one source is zero and the other positive is pinned to ±(≈21), and these dominate any downstream statistic (variance, HSIC, correlation) on the divergence field. The epsilon is a fixed absolute constant, but counts span 0 to 10^5 — an additive smoothing that is negligible for large cells is catastrophic for zeros. This is the classic log-ratio-of-counts trap.
- **evidence:** operators.py:342 (epsilon 1e-9); kernels.py:613-615 (log((v+ε)/(v_right+ε))); Brazilian municipal counts routinely 0 on one system
- **recommendation:** Replace the fixed additive epsilon with a principled small-count treatment: (a) a Bayesian/Haldane–Anscombe continuity correction (add 0.5 to both counts) so the log-ratio is bounded and has a defined variance; or (b) model the divergence as a difference of Poisson-offset log-means (log λ_left - log λ_right) with shrinkage, which regularizes zeros toward the local mean rather than toward -21; or (c) mask cells where either count is below a support threshold and type them missing rather than emitting a saturated ±21. Carry the chosen ε/correction as declared provenance, not a buried literal.
- **risk:** The divergence field is dominated by epsilon artifacts at every structural zero; any correlation/HSIC/edge readout the LDO computes on it is driven by the smoothing constant rather than by real morbidity–mortality discordance.


### Moran's I input

**`MORAN-VALUE-NOT-RATE-STD-01` · MEDIUM · epidemiological_risk** — Moran's I is computed on raw counts/rates without population standardization or variance-stabilization, so it measures population density autocorrelation, not disease-risk clustering
- **detail:** _moran_i_adjacency (compile_attach.py:136-162) computes Moran's I on the field's raw value vector (counts for count fields, crude rates for rate fields), municipality by municipality. For raw counts this is dominated by population size: big cities cluster with big cities, so I is high because POPULATION is spatially autocorrelated, not because RISK is. For crude rates on sparse denominators, the per-cell variance is wildly heteroscedastic (Var ∝ 1/μ), so a small-denominator municipality with a freak-high rate inflates or deflates I unpredictably — Moran's I assumes homoscedastic values. The standard fix (Empirical-Bayes-smoothed Moran's I, or an Assunção–Reis EB-standardized I) is not used. Because this I then FEEDS the n_eff deflation (finding MORAN-DOUBLECORRECT-01), the error compounds: a spurious population-driven I deflates n_eff for count fields on exactly the metric that should measure risk clustering.
- **evidence:** compile_attach.py:136-162 (Moran on raw values, no EB standardization); q_tensor._moran_contiguity:116-144 (same, plus ordering-proxy); n_eff deflation consumes this I
- **recommendation:** Compute an Empirical-Bayes-standardized Moran's I (Assunção & Reis 1999) that shrinks each cell's rate toward the global mean by its denominator before computing autocorrelation, removing the small-denominator heteroscedasticity and the population-size confound. For count fields, standardize by expected counts (O/E, indirect standardization) before Moran. Record the standardization in provenance.
- **risk:** Reported spatial autocorrelation reflects where people live, not where disease clusters; the n_eff deflation and any 'spatial signal' claim are confounded by population density and small-area rate instability.


### Moran's I fallback

**`MORAN-ORDERING-PROXY-01` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — The q_tensor Moran fallback uses 1-D tensor-ordering contiguity as a geography proxy — an essentially random adjacency that produces a meaningless autocorrelation
- **detail:** When no adjacency matrix is supplied, q_tensor._moran_contiguity (lines 116-144) treats cells adjacent in the TENSOR CELL ORDER as spatial neighbours (w_ij=1 for |i-j|=1). The cell order is whatever the group_by/sort produced (sorted by municipality_cod6 string, then possibly year) — this has NO relation to geographic adjacency. Two municipalities with consecutive IBGE codes can be in different states 2000 km apart. So the 'Moran's I' is the lag-1 autocorrelation of a nearly-arbitrary sequence: for a rate sorted by code it will be near zero (codes are not geographically ordered), systematically UNDER-estimating true spatial autocorrelation and therefore under-deflating n_eff. The code honestly appends warning 'moran_i_ordering_contiguity_proxy', but it is still WRITTEN into moran_i and CONSUMED by the n_eff deflation as if real. A warning tag does not neutralize a number that flows into the certification gate. Note compile_attach now defaults to a structural queen-contiguity adjacency (line 202), so the live path is better — but the q_tensor fallback remains reachable (compute_q_state when moran_i is None and no adjacency) and any caller using q_tensor directly inherits the garbage proxy.
- **evidence:** q_tensor.py:116-144 (chain contiguity over cell order); q_tensor.py:208-213 (computed then written to moran_i and used at line 218); compile_attach.py:202 (structural fallback exists in the other path)
- **recommendation:** Never fabricate an autocorrelation from cell ordering. If no geographic adjacency is available, set moran_i = None and n_eff = Kish (no deflation) and emit a hard 'spatial_autocorrelation_unavailable' state penalty, rather than a proxy number. Route the structural queen-contiguity adjacency (already built in compile_attach) into q_tensor so the direct-call path uses real geography too.
- **risk:** Any consumer of q_tensor.compute_q_state without an adjacency gets a spatial statistic derived from IBGE-code sort order, silently biasing n_eff; the warning tag gives false assurance that the number is safe to use.


### n_events for rate fields

**`RN-N-EVENTS-RATE-01` · MEDIUM · implementation_gap** — For an RN rate field, n_events is populated by summing the RATE vector, not the numerator count, inflating/deflating the event count nonsensically
- **detail:** compile_attach.py:304-312 sets materialized_count_total = Σ vector only when the unit is a count-like unit or the role is a source/restricted count. For an RN field (unit is the ratio output_unit, e.g. 'per_100k' or 'proportion', kind intensive_density), this branch does NOT fire, so n_events falls back to support['n_events'] — which _compute_rn_ratio DOES set correctly to the numerator total (kernels.py:387,407). So the RN path is actually saved by the support value. BUT any rate field NOT produced through _compute_rn_ratio (e.g. a rate re-materialized, or an intensive field whose unit accidentally matches a count-like token, or a proportion emitted with unit 'proportion' that is later re-tagged) risks summing the rate/proportion vector as 'events'. More concretely: §3.12.1 says n_events = Σ ν_num, i.e. the NUMERATOR count, and the only place this is guaranteed correct is the support hand-off; the vector-based fallback (line 309) is a landmine that produces Σ of proportions (a meaningless number in [0, n_cells]) for any proportion field whose unit string collides with the count-token set or whose role set includes a count role. The dual source of truth (support['n_events'] vs Σvector) with a fragile unit-string gate is the defect.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §3.12.1 (n_events = Σ ν_num); compile_attach.py:304-312 (unit-token gated Σvector fallback); kernels.py:387,407 (support n_events set from numerator total)
- **recommendation:** Make n_events ALWAYS come from the numerator-count provenance (the MeasuredQuantity numerator_count total for RN fields; the materialized count for count fields), never from summing the field's own value vector. Remove the unit-string heuristic; drive the choice off field.kind (extensive → sum value; intensive → read numerator provenance). Test with a proportion field whose unit is 'proportion' and a rate field to pin n_events to the true numerator count.
- **risk:** Any rate/proportion field that slips the unit-token gate reports a garbage n_events (sum of proportions), which then feeds n_eff fallback and the state gate; the correctness currently rests on a brittle string whitelist.


### state classification

**`STATE-GATE-THRESHOLDS-01` · MEDIUM · msd_theoretical_fragility** — The verified/fragile thresholds (n_eff≥100, frag<0.05, miss<0.10, risk<0.5) are un-calibrated magic constants applied uniformly across incommensurable estimands
- **detail:** classify_q_state (q_tensor.py:34-49) and §3.12.14 gate on n_eff≥100 ∧ frag<0.05 ∧ miss<0.10 ∧ risk<0.5 for verified, n_eff≥30 ∧ frag<0.20 for fragile. These are seven fixed constants with no derivation and no per-family calibration. n_eff≥100 is a reasonable rule of thumb for a proportion CI half-width, but it is applied identically to a Poisson count (where 100 events gives CV=10%), a rate (where the relevant quantity is the denominator, not n_eff), and a latent field (where n_eff is only partly meaningful). The AND-composition is brittle: a field with n_eff=99.9 and everything else pristine is downgraded to fragile purely on a decimal. There is no notion of the DECISION the certification supports — a rate used for a null-hypothesis screen needs different precision than one used for a point estimate. The thresholds also interact pathologically with the broken n_eff (finding 01): since rate n_eff is meaningless, the n_eff≥100 clause is effectively random for rates. A principled system would set the ESS threshold from a target CI width for the specific estimand and link family, not a global 100.
- **evidence:** q_tensor.py:34-49; MSD-I §3.12.14 (lines 5112-5149); constants 100/30/0.05/0.20/0.10/0.5 hard-coded
- **recommendation:** Derive the ESS threshold from a declared target relative CI half-width per family (e.g. verified ⇔ Poisson/binomial CI half-width < 20% at the estimand's link), so the constant follows from the science rather than a round number. Make the composition graded (a continuous certification score) rather than a hard AND on knife-edge thresholds, or at minimum add hysteresis. Calibrate risk and missingness cutoffs per provenance family. Document each constant's justification or mark it a tunable governed parameter.
- **risk:** Certification is decided by round-number folklore; knife-edge downgrades; the thresholds are miscalibrated for counts vs rates vs latent fields; combined with the broken rate n_eff, the verified label is not defensible.


### MeasuredQuantity

**`MQ-EXPOSURE-VARIANCE-01` · HIGH · enhancement_over_msd** — MeasuredQuantity carries count+exposure but discards the exposure's OWN reconstruction uncertainty (population is an estimate with a growing envelope), breaking the offset assumption
- **detail:** MSD-III §II.3 / §I.2: the offset model is log E[count] = log(exposure) + effects, which treats log(exposure) as a KNOWN constant offset. But §II.4 is emphatic that the population denominator is itself a RECONSTRUCTED estimate whose uncertainty GROWS with distance from a census anchor (projected cells typed ≤fragile, widening cell_uncertainty). MeasuredQuantity (measured_quantity.py:31-91) stores only a scalar `denom_fragility` in uncertainty and a point `exposure` column — it throws away the per-cell exposure variance/envelope that §II.4 went to great lengths to compute. So a rate on a 2024 projected denominator (wide envelope) and a rate on a 2010 census denominator (exact) enter the LDO with IDENTICAL offset treatment; the count-variance is preserved but the EXPOSURE-variance (often the dominant term for extrapolated years) is dropped. This silently contradicts the honest-edge contract: the whole point of the FAL-POP envelope is that a rate on a projected denominator should carry visibly wider bars, and the terminal object that feeds the model discards exactly that.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §I.2 (offset = known log exposure); §II.4 (lines 149-151, per-cell exposure uncertainty grows with anchor distance); measured_quantity.py:31-91 (scalar denom_fragility only, point exposure column, no per-cell exposure variance)
- **recommendation:** Extend MeasuredQuantity with a per-cell exposure_uncertainty column (the FAL-POP cell_uncertainty / envelope width) and propagate it as a measurement-error-in-offset term: use a Berkson/classical measurement-error offset (log-exposure with variance) or an errors-in-variables Poisson so the LDO widens intervals on projected-denominator cells. At minimum carry exposure_cv per cell and expose it to the LDO count-with-offset margin. Add a test that a projected-year cell yields a wider implied-rate interval than a census-year cell.
- **risk:** Rates on extrapolated/projected populations are reported with the same confidence as census-year rates, directly violating the §II.4 honest-edge contract; the LDO under-propagates the dominant uncertainty source for recent-year epidemiology.


### Kish weights

**`KISH-ABS-NEGATIVE-01` · LOW · computational_trap** — Kish n_eff takes abs() of weights, silently accepting negative-valued fields (log-ratios, deflated indices, z-scores) as if they were counts
- **detail:** _kish_effective_n (q_tensor.py:147-162) computes over np.abs(weights). Kish's (Σw)²/Σw² is defined for NON-NEGATIVE sampling weights (counts, exposures). Taking abs() means a divergence field (log-ratios, symmetric around 0), a deflated economic index, or any signed context field will be fed to Kish and produce a number, when the correct behaviour is to REFUSE (an ESS is undefined for signed values). Worse, abs() lets cancellation hide: a field with values {+10,-10,+10,-10} has Σ|w|=40, Σw²=400, n_eff=4 — but the signed sum is 0, so if this field were ever (incorrectly) routed as a count-weight the abs() masks that it is not a count at all. This is a silent-acceptance trap: the guard that SHOULD reject signed weights instead sanitizes them. Given that divergence/context fields are non_aggregable and shouldn't reach the count-weight branch, the risk is contained today, but the abs() removes the tripwire that would catch a mis-typed field.
- **evidence:** q_tensor.py:157 (np.abs on weights before Kish); Kish ESS defined for non-negative weights only; divergence fields are signed
- **recommendation:** Replace abs() with a precondition: if any weight is negative, return None (ESS undefined) and emit an 'ess_undefined_signed_weights' warning, forcing the caller to handle signed fields explicitly rather than silently. Keep abs() only where the weight is provably a count/exposure by kind.
- **risk:** A mis-typed signed field yields a plausible-looking but meaningless n_eff instead of a hard refusal; the type tripwire is disabled by sanitization.


### temporal roughness

**`TEMPROUGH-SPEC-01` · LOW · implementation_gap** — Temporal roughness uses a first-difference RMS normalized by the mean, not the §3.12.11 second-difference (curvature) normalized per the spec
- **detail:** MSD-I §3.12.11 defines R_T = (1/(|S*|(|T|-2))) Σ [v(s,t) - 2v(s,t-1) + v(s,t-2)]² — a SECOND-difference (discrete curvature / acceleration) averaged over space and time. q_tensor._temporal_roughness (lines 92-98) instead computes sqrt(mean(diff²))/|mean| where diff = np.diff(arr) is the FIRST difference — a first-difference RMS, then divides by the mean (a normalization the spec does not have). First differences penalize any trend (a smoothly rising incidence has large first differences but zero second differences / curvature); the spec's second difference is deliberately trend-invariant, penalizing only non-smoothness. So a cleanly trending rate (the epidemiological transition — exactly what this system studies) is scored ROUGH by the implementation but SMOOTH by the spec. The mean-normalization also blows up for fields with mean≈0. This is a divergence that mis-flags the most important epidemiological signal (secular trend) as instability.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §3.12.11 (lines 5067-5077, second difference); q_tensor.py:92-98 (np.diff first difference, /mean normalization)
- **recommendation:** Implement the §3.12.11 second-difference curvature exactly (v_t - 2v_{t-1} + v_{t-2}), averaged over cells and interior time points, and drop the mean-normalization or replace it with a scale that is robust at mean≈0 (e.g. normalize by the field's IQR or leave it in native units per the spec). Test on a linear trend (must give ≈0 roughness) and a sawtooth (must give high roughness).
- **risk:** Secular epidemiological trends are mis-scored as temporal instability; a rising-incidence field is penalized for the very signal the study seeks, and mean≈0 fields produce spurious infinities.


### POP-reconstruction / identifiability

**`POP-IDENT-01` · CRITICAL · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Intercensal (age,sex,race) tensor is structurally under-determined; the prior IS the posterior, so demography is fabricated
- **detail:** The closure constraint (MSD-I §2.8.10) pins only the marginal sum_{a,x,r} P_{s,t,a,x,r}=E_{s,t} — one scalar per (locality,year). The full cell has |A|*|X|*|R| unknowns (101*2*6 ~ 1212 per locality-year at full support). For any intercensal year with no census strata, the ONLY things that select a point in this ~1211-dimensional affine null-space are the soft smoothness priors (age_smooth lambda=0.05, race lambda=0.1) and the cohort-aging coupling to adjacent years. All of these are priors, not data. The census-composition warm start (layer1.interpolate_census_composition) sets P^0 = E_{s,t} * pi_census, and the objective's minimum over the null-space, absent informative flows, is exactly that warm start (orchestrator explicitly returns it when informative=False). So for every intercensal year the reported age/sex/race breakdown is a deterministic linear interpolation of the two bracketing census shares scaled to the year's total — it carries ZERO intercensal information about composition change (differential mortality by race, age-specific migration, epidemiological transition). Rates computed against these denominators inherit fabricated stratum structure while being reported with reconstruction_uncertainty=0.02. This is the central scientific fragility: the MSD frames this as 'reconstruction consistent with the tensor's purpose' (§2.8.10) but never quantifies that the intercensal composition posterior variance equals the prior variance (data is uninformative on it), so downstream stratum-specific rates have effectively infinite unmodeled uncertainty on their denominator composition.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §2.8.10 closure; orchestrator.py:452-495 (informative gate → returns layer-1 projection); layer1.py:29-78 (interpolate_census_composition); MSD-I §2.8.1
- **recommendation:** Make the identifiability explicit and propagate it: (1) emit a per-cell 'composition_identified' flag distinguishing census-anchored strata from null-space-interpolated ones; (2) replace the point-estimate denominator with a posterior that inflates intercensal stratum uncertainty to the prior width (e.g. Dirichlet-multinomial on the composition with concentration → the census-interpolated mean but variance reflecting 0 intercensal observations); (3) downstream rate CIs must integrate over this denominator-composition uncertainty (delta method or MC), not treat the denominator as fixed. At minimum, reconstruction_uncertainty for intercensal stratified cells must not be a flat 0.02.
- **risk:** Every intercensal age/sex/race-specific rate (the bulk of a 2000-2025 study) is reported with false precision; a race- or age-differential trend that is purely an artifact of holding census shares fixed will be read as a real epidemiological signal.


### POP-reconstruction / solver convergence

**`POP-ITER-02` · HIGH · computational_trap** — max_iterations=12 hardcoded in the strata build guarantees the solver never converges and returns a prior-dominated iterate
- **detail:** solve_population_tensor_from_sidra_strata defaults max_iterations=12 (orchestrator.py:138), and passes it to the blocked SPG solver whose own default is 5000. Twelve projected-gradient/SPG iterations on a ~1e6-cell-per-block quadratic with a badly scaled Hessian (anchor weight 10 vs age_smooth 0.05, a 200x conditioning spread, plus population-scale values ~1e6) cannot reach the 1e-5 projected-gradient tolerance. The code comment rationalizes this ('the census warm start IS the answer'), but that rationalization is only valid in the under-determined case — precisely the case where the extra iterations would do nothing anyway. In the INFORMATIVE case (>=2 censuses, or births/deaths/migration residual present), where the flow terms genuinely reshape the composition, 12 iterations is a hard cap that stops the solver far from the constrained optimum. The telemetry then reports converged=False and the run appends 'population_tensor_solver_nonconvergence', bumping reconstruction_uncertainty to 0.1 — but the returned tensor is still whatever the 12th iterate happened to be, an arbitrary prior-biased point, not a certified stationary point. This conflates 'we chose not to iterate' with 'we cannot identify', and ships a non-optimal denominator either way.
- **evidence:** orchestrator.py:138 (max_iterations=12), :462-466 (blocked solve + nonconvergence warning); projected_gradient.py:436-446 (telemetry converged flag); solvers.py:115 (solver default 5000)
- **recommendation:** Do not cap iterations at a magic 12. Instead: (a) in the under-determined branch skip the solve entirely (already done via `informative`); (b) in the informative branch run to real convergence with the SPG's own tolerance and a generous cap, and if it genuinely cannot converge, treat that as a modeling failure to investigate, not a silent 0.1 uncertainty bump. Add a curvature-aware preconditioner (diagonal Hessian scaling by the summed quadratic weights per cell) so the anchor/smoothness conditioning spread does not stall BB steps.
- **risk:** Informative-flow reconstructions (the scientifically interesting ones) silently return non-optimal denominators tagged only with a generic warning; the 12-cap makes the result depend on the arbitrary iterate index rather than the objective.


### POP-reconstruction / closure projection

**`POP-CLOSURE-03` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Closure is enforced by Euclidean simplex projection (subtract-and-clip), which distorts composition non-multiplicatively and can zero out small strata
- **detail:** _simplex_project_rows (projected_gradient.py:151-171) implements the Duchi/Held Euclidean projection onto {y>=0, sum=E}: it subtracts a uniform threshold theta from every cell and clips at 0. This is the L2-closest feasible point, NOT proportional rescaling. Consequences: (1) it is ADDITIVE, so it shifts every stratum by the same absolute theta regardless of its magnitude — a stratum with 3 people and one with 300000 both lose theta; small strata (rare race x old-age cells) get driven to exactly 0 when theta exceeds them, deleting demographic mass that demographically must be nonzero. (2) It is not composition-preserving: the census share vector pi that layer-1 carefully built is warped by the projection, so even at the warm start the emitted composition differs from the intended E*pi whenever any clipping occurs. The demographically correct closure operator is multiplicative (Iterative Proportional Fitting / raking): scale the whole (a,x,r) block by E / sum, which preserves the composition exactly and never zeros a cell. The MSD writes closure as an equality on the sum (§2.8.10) and is silent on HOW to project, so the implementation chose the mathematically convenient L2 projection over the demographically correct multiplicative one.
- **evidence:** projected_gradient.py:151-171 (_simplex_project_rows), :22-39 (_simplex_projection); orchestrator.py:479-484 (layer-1 optimum is this projection); MSD-I §2.8.10
- **recommendation:** Replace the Euclidean simplex projection of the closure with multiplicative (raking/IPF) scaling P_block *= E/sum(P_block) for the nonnegative warm start, which preserves composition and mass exactly. If an L2 projection must be kept for the gradient step's feasibility, at least warn/telemeter when theta clips any cell to zero, and never let the layer-1 closed-form path (the reported denominator in the data-poor case) pass through subtract-and-clip — use exact proportional scaling there.
- **risk:** Rare-stratum denominators (small race x old-age x small-municipality cells) are silently set to zero or distorted, making their rates undefined or wildly inflated; the reported composition is not the census composition it claims to be.


### POP-reconstruction / two-layer denominator

**`POP-DISC-04` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Method discontinuity at the informative/non-informative boundary and at census years produces artefactual jumps in the denominator series
- **detail:** The denominator is produced by three different procedures depending on the year: (a) census years reproduce observed strata exactly (hard anchor + closure); (b) intercensal years in an INFORMATIVE window get 12 SPG refinement steps from the warm start; (c) the same years in a NON-informative window get the raw layer-1 projection with no refinement. Because the two paths (b) and (c) are chosen by a global boolean `informative` for the whole run, and because the SPG (even 12 steps) moves cells away from the pure interpolation, the transition from a study window with only one census to one with two censuses changes the ENTIRE intercensal series discontinuously — same input year, different denominator, purely from window choice. Separately, at census years the tensor snaps to the observed 9606 composition while the immediately adjacent intercensal year is a smooth interpolation; if the census enumeration composition differs from the interpolated trend (it generally does — real composition is not linear in share-space), there is a kink at every census year. The FAL-POP-SV re-anchoring fixes the TOTAL (E) discontinuity from the 6579-vintage revision but does nothing for the COMPOSITION kink, which is the epidemiologically relevant one for stratified rates.
- **evidence:** orchestrator.py:452-495 (informative branch vs closed-form branch); closure.py:108-153 (_reanchor_closure_single_vintage fixes totals only); layer1.py:13-26 (linear share-space interpolation)
- **recommendation:** Make the reconstruction path a per-year property, not a per-run boolean, so a year's denominator does not depend on whether OTHER years happen to make the window 'informative'. Interpolate composition in ILR/log-ratio space (as §2.8.8 already does for race) rather than raw share space to reduce the census-year kink, and smooth the aging coupling across the census boundary so census years are not isolated hard snaps. Emit a diagnostic on the composition-derivative discontinuity at each census year.
- **risk:** Extending or shrinking a study window silently changes intercensal denominators; census-year kinks masquerade as demographic shocks in stratified time series.


### POP-reconstruction / migration gravity prior

**`POP-GRAV-05` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Gross migration volume is formally unidentified from net marginals; the whole flow field's scale is set by the magic base_rate=0.01
- **detail:** The MSD itself concedes (§2.8.7 'Identifiability, stated honestly') that the net marginal is invariant to balanced circulation, so sum F (gross volume) is set entirely by the gravity prior's rate rho. In code rho=base_rate=0.01 (migration.py:164) — a hardcoded 'assume every origin emits 1% of its population per year'. This is not a Brazilian internal-migration fact; internal migration rates vary enormously by region (Amazonian frontier vs consolidated Southeast), age (young-adult peak), and period. A single national constant multiplies the entire reconstructed flow field, so any downstream use of gross flow (the migration-affinity spatial kernel feeding MSD-II §II.4) is scaled by an unvalidated guess. distance_decay=2.0 (Newtonian gravity exponent) is likewise assumed, not fit; real migration decay on a contiguity-hop metric is not gamma=2. Because hops are integer graph distances (1,2,3), hops^2 gives decay weights 1, 0.25, 0.11 — an extremely steep, arbitrary cliff with no calibration to observed flows. Without a census O→D anchor, the reconstruction is a gravity SIMULATION lightly constrained to reproduce net marginals, presented as an 'estimate'.
- **evidence:** migration.py:164 (base_rate=0.01, distance_decay=2.0), :120-151 (_gravity_prior); MSD-I §2.8.7 identifiability note; instances.py:47-92 (net null-space)
- **recommendation:** Do not ship a single national base_rate. Either (a) calibrate rho and gamma per region/period against the decennial census O→D tables (which the code already supports as census_flows) via a held-out fit, or (b) refuse to emit gross-flow-dependent products (the affinity kernel's magnitude) when unanchored and expose only net-consistent, scale-free RELATIVE structure. Replace hops^gamma with a fitted decay (e.g. exponential exp(-hops/L) with L estimated), and report the sensitivity of the affinity kernel to rho.
- **risk:** The migration-affinity spatial kernel — and any spatial-autocorrelation inference downstream that uses it — is scaled and shaped by two uncalibrated constants, so 'functional adjacency' between municipalities is partly an artefact of base_rate=0.01/gamma=2.


### POP-reconstruction / migration bounds

**`POP-MIGBOUND-06` · MEDIUM · epidemiological_risk** — Symmetric per-cell migration box bound 0.25*E/n_strata is an unjustified magic fraction that can be far too tight or too loose
- **detail:** The MSD (§2.8.1) bounds eta in [-E_{s,t}, E_{s,t}] — the closure total, a loose but principled cap (net migration cannot exceed the population). The implementation instead uses a per-CELL bound of 0.25*E_{s,t}/n_strata (orchestrator.py:346-351), i.e. it divides the locality-year cap by the number of strata AND multiplies by an unexplained 0.25. Two problems: (1) 0.25 is a magic number with no derivation — why can a single (age,sex,race) cell's net migration be at most a quarter of its equal share of the population? Young-adult cells routinely have net migration that is a large fraction of the cell population (frontier municipalities), so 0.25/n_strata can be far too tight, artificially clipping the very cells migration is supposed to explain. (2) Dividing E uniformly by n_strata assumes migration mass is spread evenly across strata, contradicting the fact that migration is heavily age-concentrated. The bound is also symmetric [-b,+b] while the observed net residual (which the migration_total term anchors to) is signed and can be strongly asymmetric.
- **evidence:** orchestrator.py:342-351 (0.25*closure/strata bound, floor 1.0); MSD-I §2.8.1 (eta in [-E,E]); projected_gradient.py:188-189 (np.clip to +-bounds)
- **recommendation:** Either honor the MSD's [-E,E] per-cell bound (loose, principled) or derive a strata-aware bound from an age-migration schedule (e.g. Rogers-Castro model migration profile) rather than a flat 0.25/n. Make the bound asymmetric if the net residual sign is known. Remove the undocumented 0.25 or justify it with a citation/calibration.
- **risk:** Age-concentrated migration in high-churn municipalities is clipped by a too-tight arbitrary bound, biasing the reconstructed intercensal age structure toward the census-interpolated prior.


### POP-reconstruction / objective form

**`POP-GAUSS-07` · HIGH · msd_theoretical_fragility** — All-quadratic (Gaussian) loss on population COUNTS is the wrong noise model for sparse municipal demography; it is not count-appropriate and ignores heteroscedasticity
- **detail:** Every term of L (anchor, aging, birth, death, migration, race, age_smooth) is a squared residual on population/flow COUNTS (MSD-I §2.8.3–2.8.9; loss.py implements sum(residual**2)). A least-squares fit is the MLE only under homoscedastic Gaussian noise. Municipal demographic counts are anything but: a cell with 3 people and a cell with 300000 contribute residuals on the same absolute scale, so the objective is dominated by large (urban, common-stratum) cells and effectively ignores small (rural, rare-stratum) cells — precisely the small-area cells where reconstruction matters most and where Poisson/NB variance (~mean) should DOWN-weight, not up-weight, large cells. The aging and death balance equations are demographic FLOWS whose natural error is Poisson (count) or negative-binomial (overdispersed count), not additive Gaussian. Using L2 on counts also makes the anchor weight (10) vs smoothness weight (0.05) tradeoff scale-dependent on population magnitude, so the same lambda vector behaves differently for a 5000-person town and a 12M city.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §2.8.3-2.8.9 (all squared-count losses); loss.py:207-325 (every term is np.sum(residual**2))
- **recommendation:** Move to a count-appropriate objective: work in a variance-stabilized scale (sqrt or Anscombe transform of counts) so the Gaussian assumption is approximately valid, or replace the aging/birth/death balance residuals with Poisson/negative-binomial deviance terms (a GLM/IRLS solve). At minimum, weight each squared residual by an inverse-variance proxy (1/max(P,1)) so small cells are not drowned out. Overdispersion (NB) is essential given municipal count clustering.
- **risk:** The reconstruction is driven almost entirely by large urban cells; rare-stratum and small-municipality denominators — the ones with the least data and highest policy relevance — are effectively unconstrained and default to the prior with no error control.


### POP-reconstruction / smoothness priors

**`POP-SMOOTH-08` · MEDIUM · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Flat second-difference (RW2) smoothness on age and migration is a stationary global prior that fights the epidemiological transition and real age-structure features
- **detail:** L_smooth (§2.8.9) penalizes the second difference of P over age with a single global weight lambda_S=0.05, and L_migration (§2.8.7) penalizes the second difference of eta over time. A constant-weight RW2 prior encodes 'the age curve is globally linear' and 'migration is globally constant-acceleration'. Real Brazilian age structure has strong non-linear features the RW2 will over-smooth: the birth cohort echo, the 100+ pooling boundary, and the accelerating old-age share of the epidemiological transition (which is exactly the signal a C25 pancreatic-cancer study cares about, since incidence is old-age concentrated). A stationary smoothness prior with a hand-set lambda cannot adapt: too-strong lambda flattens the old-age acceleration; too-weak lambda lets census-interpolation noise through. The MSD offers no adaptivity — lambda is one scalar tuned once (§2.8.11) against census validation cells, which are themselves only at census years, so the tuning cannot see intercensal smoothness error at all.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §2.8.9 (age RW2), §2.8.7 (migration RW2); loss.py:288-325; MSD-I §2.8.11 (single global lambda tuned on census-year val cells only)
- **recommendation:** Adopt a spatially/age-adaptive smoothness: an INLA/SPDE or adaptive-RW2 formulation where the innovation variance varies over the age axis (larger at cohort echoes and the old-age transition), or a P-spline with a locally varying penalty. Tie the old-age smoothness to a known mortality schedule rather than a flat RW2. Tune the smoothness against a temporal hold-out (leave-one-census-out) so intercensal smoothing error is actually observable.
- **risk:** The old-age acceleration that a cancer-mortality study depends on is systematically over-smoothed toward the census-interpolated linear trend, biasing age-standardized rates.


### POP-reconstruction / SIM-informed death prior

**`POP-DEATHRATE-09` · MEDIUM · msd_theoretical_fragility** — delta (death rate) is estimated as SIM_deaths / census_anchor, so the death term only activates at census-coincident years and injects circular self-reference
- **detail:** The death loss (§2.8.6) is sum(delta*P - D_SIM)^2, and the MSD implementation note concedes delta is estimated as the empirical ratio D_SIM / C against the tensor's OWN census strata anchor C. This has three problems the MSD flags but ships anyway: (1) C exists only at census years, so the death term is only informative for deaths in a year with a coincident census — the minority of years once intercensal stitching is active — meaning the SIM-informed mode's flagship feedback term is silent for almost all years. (2) delta = D_SIM/C then delta*P in the loss means at the census year the term is D_SIM/C * P; since P is being fit toward C, the term drives P toward C*(D_SIM/D_SIM)... it is a near-tautology at the only years it activates, contributing almost no real information. (3) The empirical ratio uses SIM deaths (a DATASUS-universe numerator) divided by a SIDRA-universe denominator — a cross-universe rate the MSD elsewhere warns against (coverage divergence). In independent mode lambda_D=0 so none of this matters, but sim_informed mode presents feedback-risk warnings as if the term were doing real work when it largely is not.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §2.8.6 implementation note (delta = D_SIM/C, anchor-year coincidence); loss.py:254-262; orchestrator.py:398-403 (death_rates = sim/anchor where anchor>0)
- **recommendation:** Estimate delta from a proper reference-rate source (UF/Region/Brazil age-sex-specific mortality schedules, or the solver's own current P iterate in an EM/IRLS loop as the MSD's 'future work' suggests) so the death term is informative in every year, not just census-coincident ones. Restrict the SIM/census ratio to the same statistical universe or explicitly model the coverage-divergence factor.
- **risk:** sim_informed_denominator mode carries feedback-risk warnings and elevated uncertainty for a death term that is effectively inert in intercensal years — cost (warnings, fragility) with little benefit, and a genuine cross-universe bias where it does activate.


### POP-reconstruction / block separability

**`POP-SEP-10` · MEDIUM · implementation_gap** — Locality-block separability is exact only because the net-migration residual is imposed as a per-locality total, silently discarding the spatial coupling migration actually has
- **detail:** solve_population_tensor_blocked splits the national solve into independent locality blocks, justified by _locality_separable which returns True whenever migration_totals (the per-stratum cross-locality enclosure sum_s eta = M^national) is None — and 'the SIDRA denominator build never sets it' (solvers.py:104-108). So the ONLY migration term active in the real build is migration_locality_totals (the per-locality net residual, §2.8.7 open-national case), which couples nothing across localities. This makes the tensor solve embarrassingly parallel but means the population reconstruction treats each municipality as demographically isolated: person leaving municipality A is not required to arrive in municipality B. The spatial O→D structure is reconstructed SEPARATELY and afterward (migration.py) purely from the net marginals, and never feeds back into the tensor's age/sex/race composition. So the intercensal composition of who-moved-where is imposed only via the net scalar, and the block solve cannot use a neighbour's out-migration to inform a municipality's in-migration age structure. This is a real loss of identifying information (the flow field could constrain composition) traded for parallelism.
- **evidence:** solvers.py:104-108 (_locality_separable), :111-152 (blocked solve); MSD-I §2.8.7 (M^national=0 closed-national case is available but unused); migration.py (flow layer is downstream, not fed back)
- **recommendation:** Either (a) close the national migration system (M^national = 0 or the stratified residual) so out-migration and in-migration are mass-consistent across localities — this couples localities and forbids naive block separation, but a spatial ADMM (already registered as sparse_ADMM) handles it; or (b) explicitly document that the tensor is deliberately locality-isolated and the flow field is a post-hoc overlay that does not constrain composition, so no one mistakes the reconstructed age-specific migration for a mass-consistent national flow.
- **risk:** The reconstruction's parallelism is bought by discarding the cross-municipality mass-balance that would identify intercensal migration composition; who-moves-where age structure is fabricated per-locality from a scalar.


### POP-reconstruction / migration_total gradient

**`POP-AGGREG-11` · MEDIUM · identifiability_concern** — The per-locality net-migration term distributes the total residual across strata by an unweighted gradient, spreading migration uniformly over all (a,x,r) cells
- **detail:** The migration_total term (loss.py:301-312) computes flow_{s,t} = sum_{a,x,r} eta and penalizes (flow - observed_net)^2, with gradient d/d eta = grad_st broadcast IDENTICALLY to every (a,x,r) cell in the slab (gm_tens += grad_st[:,:,None,None,None]). Combined with the RW2 migration smoothness (also unweighted across strata) and the symmetric per-cell bounds, the net residual gets distributed across strata with NO age/sex/race profile — every stratum is pushed by the same amount toward absorbing the net. But migration is overwhelmingly young-adult; spreading it uniformly (including onto 90+ and infants) is demographically false and pollutes exactly the old-age cells a cancer study needs. The MSD (§2.8.7) explicitly says 'the demographic composition of migration remains reconstructed, since no source stratifies it' — but the reconstruction it gets is 'uniform across strata modulo smoothness', which is the least demographically plausible profile.
- **evidence:** loss.py:301-312 (migration_total gradient broadcast uniformly); MSD-I §2.8.7 ('composition of migration remains reconstructed')
- **recommendation:** Impose a standard age-migration schedule (Rogers-Castro model migration profile) as a soft prior on eta's age shape, so the net residual is distributed with a realistic young-adult peak rather than uniformly. This is a well-known parametric family (7-parameter double-exponential) and turns 'unstratified' into 'stratified by a demographic prior' honestly.
- **risk:** Reconstructed migration deposits/withdraws people uniformly across all ages including the elderly, distorting the intercensal old-age denominator against which age-specific cancer rates are computed.


### POP-reconstruction / race composition ILR

**`POP-RACEILR-12` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — ILR race loss uses epsilon=1e-12 flooring and a 1/P chain-rule division that is numerically explosive for structural-zero strata
- **detail:** The race term maps P over the race axis to ILR coordinates (loss.py:264-286). Two numerical hazards: (1) _ilr_coords floors shares at epsilon=1e-12 before log (loss.py:98). For a genuine structural-zero race in a stratum (e.g. Indigenous in an urban Southeast municipality, legitimately ~0), the floor makes log(1e-12) ~ -27.6, so the ILR coordinate is dominated by an arbitrary epsilon, and the residual against the census prior (which also floored the same cell) is a large spurious number that the L2 loss then tries to minimize by pushing P around. (2) The gradient divides by P: grad = 2*w*deriv / max(P_valid, 1e-12) (loss.py:282). For a small-but-nonzero cell (P ~ 0.5 persons after projection) this divides by ~0.5, and for a cell driven near zero it divides by 1e-12, producing a gradient ~1e12 that the SPG line search must then absorb — the code comments elsewhere acknowledge ~1e6-1e18 gradient magnitudes stalling the search. So the race term is both statistically arbitrary at structural zeros and a source of the ill-conditioning that forces the 12-iteration cap. The ILR is the right geometry for compositions, but not with a hard epsilon floor and a raw 1/P chain rule on count-scale P.
- **evidence:** loss.py:93-101 (epsilon=1e-12 floor), :264-286 (1/P gradient), :271 (valid = sum>1e-12); projected_gradient.py:388-402 (gradient-magnitude-driven line-search stalls)
- **recommendation:** Add a small Dirichlet/Bayesian-multinomial pseudocount to the composition BEFORE the ILR (so zeros become a principled small share, not an epsilon artefact), and take the ILR gradient with respect to the composition (shares) via a proper softmax/CLR Jacobian rather than dividing the count-space gradient by raw P. This removes both the structural-zero arbitrariness and the 1/P blow-up, improving conditioning.
- **risk:** Structural-zero race strata inject large arbitrary residuals and ~1e12 gradients, degrading conditioning (contributing to non-convergence) and biasing the composition of the affected strata.


### POP-reconstruction / closure vintage interpolation

**`POP-GEOM-13` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — Geometric closure interpolation and geometric extrapolation beyond the census range compound errors and can diverge for high-growth frontier municipalities
- **detail:** geometric_interpolate_closure (anchor.py:143+) holds the annual growth RATE constant between census enumerations and, beyond the range, carries the nearest inter-census rate forward/backward geometrically. Geometric interpolation is standard for smoothly growing populations, but: (1) constant-rate compounding is wrong for municipalities with a demographic BREAK between censuses (a new dam, mine, or agribusiness boom creates a step, not a constant rate) — the interpolation smears a real discontinuity into a smooth curve. (2) Geometric EXTRAPOLATION beyond the census range is dangerous: a frontier municipality growing 8%/yr between 2010 and 2022 will be projected at 8%/yr indefinitely, exploding for years past 2022, and a shrinking rural municipality will be driven toward zero. The FAL-POP-PROJ envelope grows uncertainty with horizon, but the POINT estimate is still an unbounded geometric extrapolation. (3) Requiring >=2 census anchors means post-2000 municipalities (created by boundary churn) fall back to their prior (6579) closure, reintroducing exactly the vintage discontinuity FAL-POP-SV was built to remove, for the churny municipalities most likely to need it.
- **evidence:** anchor.py:143-183 (geometric interp + extrapolation); closure.py:135 (len(anchors)<2 → kept_single_anchor keeps 6579); MSD-I §2.8.10
- **recommendation:** Cap/regularize geometric extrapolation (blend toward the national or UF growth rate as horizon grows, rather than carrying a local extreme rate indefinitely) and prefer a damped or logistic growth model beyond the census range. For <2-anchor municipalities, interpolate the closure from the parent-municipality trajectory (the genealogy crosswalk already exists) rather than falling back to the mismatched-vintage 6579 series.
- **risk:** High-growth frontier and churny post-2000 municipalities get unbounded or vintage-inconsistent closure totals, which then scale every stratum in those municipalities.


### POP-reconstruction / hyperparameter tuning

**`POP-VALTUNE-14` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Hyperparameter tuning against census validation cells cannot observe intercensal reconstruction error — the quantity that actually matters is unmeasured
- **detail:** MSD-I §2.8.11 tunes Lambda_pop = (lambda_A..lambda_S) to minimize squared error against held-out census VALIDATION cells C^val at census years t_c. But the entire scientific purpose of this subsystem is the INTERCENSAL reconstruction (census years are observed and need no reconstruction). Tuning on census-year fit tells you which lambda reproduces observed census strata — a near-trivial objective the anchor term (weight 10) already dominates — and says NOTHING about how well the smoothness/aging/migration priors reconstruct the UNOBSERVED intercensal composition, because there is no ground truth there to score against. This is a fundamental validation gap: the loss function is tuned on the one region of the domain where it is least needed, and left untuned where it is fabricating demography. The weights actually shipped (anchor=10, aging=1, migration=0.1, race=0.1, age_smooth=0.05, migration_total=0.5) appear to be hand-set defaults in orchestrator.py, not the output of any §2.8.11 optimization at all — so even the census-year tuning may not be running in the production build.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §2.8.11 (val cells at census years only); orchestrator.py:419-428 (hard-coded weight defaults); MSD-I §2.8.10 (val cells excluded from hard constraints)
- **recommendation:** Tune against a LEAVE-ONE-CENSUS-OUT temporal hold-out: hide the 2010 census, reconstruct 2010 from 2000+2022 via the intercensal machinery, and score composition error against the hidden 2010 strata. That is the only design that scores intercensal reconstruction. Confirm whether §2.8.11 optimization is actually executed or the hand-set weights are shipped as-is, and if the latter, document them as untuned priors.
- **risk:** The prior weights that govern all fabricated intercensal demography are either untuned or tuned on the wrong (census-year) objective, so there is no evidence the reconstruction is even locally optimal for its actual purpose.


### POP-reconstruction / birth entry

**`POP-BIRTH-15` · LOW · implementation_gap** — Birth loss ignores infant mortality and mid-year timing, and the UF→Region→Brazil race fallback cascade the MSD specifies is not implemented
- **detail:** Two gaps. (1) The birth loss (§2.8.5) sets age-0 population P_{s,t,0} ~ B^newborn_{s,t-1} + eta, i.e. this year's age-0 equals last year's births plus migration. But births occur throughout year t-1, and a substantial fraction of a birth cohort dies before the age-0 census reference date (infant mortality ~12/1000 in Brazil, much higher in some Northern municipalities), and half a year of exposure means only ~half a cohort-year is 'age 0' at reference. The loss equates births to age-0 population with no survival or timing factor, over-counting the age-0 denominator. (2) The MSD §2.8.5 specifies an explicit conditional-allocation cascade P(r_n|r_m,s)→P(r_n|r_m,UF)→P(r_n|r_m,Region)→P(r_n|r_m,Brazil) triggered at n<30, but priors.py implements only the leading r_n:=r_m term (_coalesce_race_columns) and the implementation note (§2.8.5) admits 'the deeper UF→Region→Brazil conditional-allocation tail is not yet a distinct estimator'. So small-support newborn-race cells get Bridge_R's uniform posterior rather than the specified geographic fallback.
- **evidence:** MSD-I §2.8.5 (fallback cascade + n<30); priors.py:64-105 (_coalesce_race_columns implements only r_n:=r_m); loss.py:238-252 (birth loss has no survival/timing factor)
- **recommendation:** Apply an infant-survival factor (1 - q0) and a mid-year timing adjustment to the birth→age-0 balance, using the SIM infant-death counts already ingested. Implement the specified UF→Region→Brazil conditional-allocation tail for n<30 newborn-race cells, or amend the MSD to declare the single-term approximation as the intended estimator.
- **risk:** The age-0 denominator is systematically over-counted (births not survived to reference), biasing infant-rate denominators; small-support newborn-race composition is less accurate than the MSD specifies.


### age_standardization
