**`ASTD-SAE-02` · CRITICAL · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Direct standardization is used for small-area municipal rates where it is statistically indefensible; MSD prescribes SAE/BYM but the measurement layer ignores it
- **detail:** The MSD itself (§III.3, lines 201-208) declares that sparse small-area estimates MUST be handled by multiresolution hierarchical shrinkage / small-area estimation / INLA-SPDE-BYM ('a rare leaf/tiny-municipality estimate is dominated by shrinkage toward its better-estimated parent'). But the measurement subsystem that actually produces rates (age_standardization.py) implements ONLY classical direct standardization with a Fay-Feuer CI, and standardize_grouped is called with `by=['municipality']`-type strata (analysis.py drives it by uf/region/race; the same function is the municipal ASR engine). Direct standardization of a municipal rate with a rare cause is the textbook failure case: for a municipality with 5 pancreatic deaths spread over 18 age groups, most age-specific rates r_g=d_g/n_g are 0 or one death over a tiny denominator, so the standardized rate is a high-variance weighted sum dominated by whichever age stratum happened to get a death. The variance is enormous and the point estimate is unstable — precisely what shrinkage/SAE exists to fix. The two halves of the MSD (§III.3 theory vs §II.9 measurement) are not reconciled: nothing shrinks the municipal ASR toward its state/regional parent.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §III.3 (lines 201-208) small-area/INLA mandate vs §II.9 measurement; src/pegasus/measurement/age_standardization.py:65-100 (pure direct standardization, no shrinkage); no import of any GMRF/BYM smoother into the rate engine
- **recommendation:** Route municipal-resolution rates through the §III.3 BYM/SPDE latent field (empirical-Bayes or full-Bayes Poisson-lognormal with spatial ICAR), producing shrunken small-area SMRs/ASRs with posterior credible intervals; reserve direct standardization for state/national aggregates where counts are large. At minimum, gate direct-standardization output by an effective-count threshold and mark sub-threshold municipal ASRs 'unreliable'.
- **risk:** Every municipal-level standardized-rate claim (the atlas/scanner) is scientifically invalid — noise masquerading as spatial signal, the exact artifact SAE was invented to prevent.

**`ASTD-AGEMISS-01` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Age-unknown deaths are silently binned into the 0-4 stratum, inflating infant rates and deflating the standardized total
- **detail:** standardize_grouped does `frame[age_col].cast(Int64).fill_null(-1)` then `ref.age_group_index(np.clip(ages,0,200))`. age_group_index does `searchsorted(self.lo, age, side='right')-1` then `np.clip(idx,0,len(lo)-1)`. I verified numerically: age=-1 maps to group index 0 (the 0-4 bin). So every death with a missing/unparseable age (SIM `IDADE`=missing, or the study's age_i null) is folded into the 0-4 age group rather than being reallocated by the observed age composition or excluded. In SIM, idade-ignorada is nonzero (often 0.3-2% and much higher in older DO records / poor municipalities); C25 pancreatic cancer is essentially absent below 30, so an age-unknown pancreatic death dumped into 0-4 creates a spurious infant-age rate AND, because the WHO weight for 0-4 is the largest (8860/100000), that misallocated death receives the HEAVIEST standardization weight, biasing the ASR. This is a direct 'missingness with silence' prime-directive violation inside the measurement layer.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §II.5 / prime directive (line 29,486); src/pegasus/measurement/age_standardization.py:32-35 (age_group_index clip-to-0), :121-122 (fill_null(-1)); studies/pancreatic_c25_national/analysis.py:53-57 (age_i)
- **recommendation:** Treat age-unknown as a distinct missing bin: either exclude with an explicit denominator note, or reallocate age-unknown deaths across observed age groups by the local age composition (as FAL-POP-RECON does for undeclared race) and propagate the added variance. Never map -1/999 into 0-4. Add an assertion that no negative/sentinel age reaches age_group_index.
- **risk:** Standardized rates are biased (magnitude scales with the largest reference weight) and infant-age cancer rates are fabricated; the bias is worst exactly in the poor small municipalities with the most age-ignorada.

**`ASTD-POISSON-03` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Fay-Feuer gamma CI assumes Poisson age-specific counts; real DATASUS counts are over-dispersed and person-time is itself an uncertain reconstructed denominator
- **detail:** The Fay-Feuer variance v=Σ (w_g/n_g)^2 d_g treats each d_g as Poisson with mean = person-time × rate, and treats n_g (person-years) as a FIXED KNOWN constant. Two violations: (1) municipal death counts are over-dispersed relative to Poisson (spatial/temporal clustering, coding waves, family clusters, boundary-driven residence miscoding), so the true variance exceeds the Poisson variance and the gamma interval under-covers. (2) n_g here is NOT observed person-time — it is the reconstructed population tensor (layer1 closed-form interpolation + solver), which carries its own FAL-POP-PROJ envelope uncertainty (projection_envelope.py, per-year sigma growing 0.015/yr). The CI formula propagates ZERO denominator uncertainty, so a projected-year municipal rate reports false precision — a direct 'measurement uncertainty with false precision' violation. The denominator's own uncertainty envelope exists but is never joined into the rate CI.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/measurement/age_standardization.py:84-94 (Fay-Feuer v uses d only, n fixed); src/pegasus/denominators/population/build/projection_envelope.py:13-14,56 (denominator sigma exists but unused); MSD-III prime directive line 29
- **recommendation:** Move to a negative-binomial / quasi-Poisson count model for over-dispersion, and propagate denominator uncertainty by treating log(person-years) as an offset with a variance term (delta-method or Monte-Carlo over the FAL-POP-PROJ envelope draws), widening the rate CI. Report an over-dispersion diagnostic per stratum.
- **risk:** Confidence intervals systematically under-cover; 'significant' spatial/temporal differences are declared that are within denominator+overdispersion noise.

**`ASTD-CI-PROP-15` · HIGH · implementation_gap** — Standardized-rate CIs are computed but never propagated into the LDO/inference layer; downstream determinant analysis treats point ASRs as exact
- **detail:** directly_standardized_rate returns (asr, ci_low, ci_high) and the study reports them, but the standardized rate that feeds the LDO determinant analysis (the copula-margin count-with-exposure layer, MSD-III line 222) enters as a point value or a raw count+offset — the Fay-Feuer / race-bridge interval is not carried as a measurement-error variance into the dependency/causal estimation. MSD §II.4 requires per-cell uncertainty typing and the prime directive forbids false precision, yet the rate's own CI dies at the reporting boundary. A determinant model regressing municipal C25 ASR on socioeconomic covariates that ignores the (often enormous) small-area rate variance will over-fit noise and produce anti-conservative associations — the same class of error the LDO's residual-scan null was hardened against, but reintroduced at the measurement input.
- **evidence:** age_standardization.py:53-63 (CI produced) vs MSD-III line 222 (LDO count-with-exposure margin); no path carrying ci_low/ci_high into ldo/assemble.py; §II.4 per-cell uncertainty
- **recommendation:** Propagate the standardized-rate variance (Fay-Feuer v, plus denominator-envelope and race-bridge variance) as a per-observation measurement-error term / weight into the LDO margin, or feed raw counts+offset with the over-dispersion and denominator uncertainty so the inference layer sees the true precision. Type each rate cell with its uncertainty per §II.4.
- **risk:** Determinant/causal claims are anti-conservative because the measurement layer's honest uncertainty is discarded before inference; small, noisy municipalities get equal weight to large stable ones.

**`ASTD-STDPOP-04` · MEDIUM · epidemiological_risk** — WHO World Standard is the default reference; for a cancer of the elderly its tiny 85+ weight discards most of the signal and destabilizes municipal estimates
- **detail:** The default reference is who_world_2000_2025 with weight 635/100000 for 85+ and 8860 for 0-4. Pancreatic cancer mortality is concentrated at 65-85+. Direct standardization against WHO World gives near-zero weight to the age bands where the disease actually occurs, so the ASR is dominated by young-age strata with essentially zero true rate but high sampling noise per death. This is a known distortion: WHO World was designed for all-cause international comparability, not for age-concentrated rare cancers, and it inflates the relative influence of noisy young strata. Using it as the silent default (studies default `ref='who_world_2000_2025'`) makes municipal comparisons of an elderly cancer both noisier and less epidemiologically meaningful than a truncated (e.g. 30+/50+) or the Brazil-2010 internal standard. There is no truncated-standard or age-restricted option.
- **evidence:** config/registries/health/reference_populations.yaml (WHO weights, 85+=635); studies/pancreatic_c25_national/analysis.py:66 default ref; age_standardization.py load default
- **recommendation:** For age-concentrated diseases, offer a truncated standard population (e.g. 30+ or 50-84 truncated ASR, standard in cancer epidemiology) and default studies of elderly cancers to the Brazil-2010 or a truncated standard; report both WHO (for international comparability) and a truncated internal rate.
- **risk:** Municipal/temporal signal-to-noise is needlessly degraded and cross-disease comparisons are distorted by a standard mismatched to the disease's age profile.

**`ASTD-OPENEND-05` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — 85+ open-ended group person-years vs single-year weights, and ages above 100 all collapse to one bin without a person-time consistency check
- **detail:** The 85+ bin has lo=85, hi=200. Any age 85..200 maps to group 17 (verified: 90,150,999 all -> 17). The reference weight for 85+ is a single lumped value, but the age-specific rate r_17 = d_17/n_17 is over an open-ended person-time that mixes 85-89 with centenarians whose rate is very different. Direct standardization against a single 85+ weight assumes the internal age distribution of the study population's 85+ matches the standard's — for a rapidly-aging Brazil with shifting oldest-old composition across 2000-2025 this within-group compositional drift biases the ASR over time (the standardization does not remove age-structure change WITHIN the open bin). Additionally, sentinel/garbled ages (e.g. 999 or SIM century-coded ages) silently land in 85+ with no validation, contaminating the oldest-old rate.
- **evidence:** age_standardization.py:32-35 age_group_index; reference_populations.yaml 85+ {lo:85,hi:200}; verified binning of 150/999 -> group 17
- **recommendation:** Extend the standard to finer oldest-old bins (85-89,90-94,95+) where the standard population supports it, or apply within-85+ sub-standardization; validate that ages feeding the 85+ bin are plausible (reject/flag >119) rather than absorbing garbage into the oldest bin.
- **risk:** Temporal ASR trends in an aging population carry residual within-85+ age-structure bias, and coding garbage inflates oldest-old rates.

**`ASTD-NUMSTAB-06` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — Fay-Feuer chi-square df = 2*asr^2/v can underflow to df<=0 for tiny ASRs, and the ASR-per-1 scale makes v numerically minuscule
- **detail:** The lower bound uses chi2.ppf(alpha/2, df=2*asr*asr/v) where asr and v are on the per-1 (not per-100000) scale — asr ~ 1e-5, v ~ 1e-12. df = 2*asr^2/v is a ratio of tiny numbers; for a stratum with one death, v is set by a single (w/n)^2 d term and df can become a fraction well below 1, where chi2.ppf is defined but the gamma approximation's coverage guarantee degrades, and floating-point cancellation in 2*asr^2/v is severe. The code guards only asr<=0 or v<=0; it does not guard the degenerate small-df regime or check that the resulting interval brackets the point estimate. For zero-death strata the whole ASR can be 0 with lo=hi=0 reported as a genuine CI (0,0), which is a false-precision zero, not 'no information'.
- **evidence:** age_standardization.py:87-94 (df=2*asr*asr/v on per-1 scale), :98 (scaling to per applied AFTER); no df floor / bracket check
- **recommendation:** Compute the interval on a numerically stable scale (rescale counts, or use the mid-P exact Poisson / Byar approximation for total-count-driven strata), floor/branch on df, and represent zero-count strata as (0, upper) with an exact Poisson upper bound rather than (0,0).
- **risk:** Degenerate or falsely-zero-width CIs on the sparse municipal strata that most need honest intervals.


### race

**`RACE-IDENTIFY-07` · CRITICAL · identifiability_concern** — The race bridge imposes a race distribution: with a uniform local pi and a fixed emission matrix, posterior self-declared counts are a deterministic linear pushforward, not an inference
- **detail:** MSD §II.5 requires W[k][j] ∝ C[k][j]·pi_local[j] with pi_local the LOCAL self-declared composition. But _local_target_pi (race.py:305-315) falls back to a UNIFORM pi_local={1/J} whenever counts.support carries no 'target_population_shares'. bridge_admin_race_group_counts (the shared entry point for SIM deaths and SINASC births feeding the tensor) NEVER supplies target_population_shares. So in the live path pi_local is uniform, W reduces to the row-normalized emission matrix C itself, and posterior_self[j]=Σ_k n_k·C[k][j] — a fixed deterministic crosswalk. The 'Bayesian ecological bridge' collapses to multiplying observed admin counts by a fabricated-prior confusion matrix; it does NOT reconcile to the actual local self-declared composition, and it IMPOSES whatever race distribution C encodes. The effective_bridge_mode metadata even admits this ('fixedC_dynamic_weight' unless a local composition was supplied), yet the numerator that goes into a race-specific rate is this pushforward while the denominator (census population tensor) is on the true self-declared axis — so the two are on DIFFERENT effective race distributions.
- **evidence:** MSD-III §II.5 line 161; src/pegasus/measurement/race.py:305-315 (uniform fallback), :220-252 (entry point never sets shares), :274-276 (effective mode admission)
- **recommendation:** Require pi_local from the reconstructed census self-declared composition for the (locality,age,sex,year) cell — the tensor already has it — and pass it as target_population_shares so W genuinely reconciles numerator to denominator. Refuse to emit a race-specific rate when only the uniform fallback is available; mark it unidentified, per the MSD's own §II.5 line-486 duty ('never a no-op').
- **risk:** Every race-stratified rate is numerator-on-C-pushforward over denominator-on-census — a systematic, direction-unknown bias that manufactures or erases racial disparities; the headline scientific claim of the study.

**`RACE-PRIOR-08` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Emission-matrix uncertainty is a fabricated Dirichlet(concentration=250) around an unvalidated prior C, so the reported race_bridge_cv and CIs understate true epistemic uncertainty
- **detail:** The posterior draws (_draw_prior_matrix, race.py:382-389) sample C rows from Dirichlet(250·C_row + 1e-3). The concentration 250 is a hard-coded magic number (RACE_BRIDGE_MATRIX_CONCENTRATION=250.0) with no empirical basis; it makes the sampled matrices tightly clustered around the point prior, so the propagated 'uncertainty' is arbitrarily narrow by construction. Worse, MSD §II.5 (line 164) REQUIRES C to be seeded from published Brazilian admin-vs-self-declared discordance patterns; the code path validates row-stochasticity but the ACTUAL C is whatever JSON is loaded, and memory notes the content was historically an identity/fixture no-op. If C is not literature-calibrated, the entire bridge — point and interval — is a fabricated observation operator dressed as Bayesian. The SensitivityWidth (§4.5 requirement 3) is supposed to be inf/sup over the emission-matrix CREDIBLE SET; instead _posterior_intervals widens by a heuristic value·(1±sensitivity_width) (race.py:459-460), the exact heuristic the MSD contract (MSD-II line 372, item 3) forbids.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/measurement/race.py:30 (250 magic), :382-389 (Dirichlet 250), :459-460 (value·(1±w) heuristic); MSD-II §II line 372 item 3; MSD-III §II.5 line 164
- **recommendation:** Calibrate C from published PNAD/PCERP admin-vs-self-declared discordance and set the Dirichlet concentration from the sample size behind those estimates (not 250); implement genuine inf/sup partial-identification bounds over the C credible set instead of the value·(1±w) widening. Gate emission on a check that C is not identity.
- **risk:** Reported race-bridge CIs are false-precision; a validation contract (MSD-II line 372) is nominally satisfied by a forbidden heuristic.

**`RACE-DECISION-09` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Missing-race reallocation uses the same (possibly uniform) pi_local, so under uniform fallback missing race is spread EVENLY across all five categories
- **detail:** fixedc_dynamic_weight_bridge (race.py:263-265) reallocates missing_count across targets by local_pi. When local_pi is the uniform fallback (the live path, per finding RACE-IDENTIFY-07), missing race is distributed 1/5 to each of branca/preta/amarela/parda/indigena. This is demographically absurd for Brazil: it assigns 20% of every unknown-race death to indigena and 20% to amarela, categories that are typically <1% of most municipalities. Missing race in SIM is non-ignorable (worse coding in specific regions/races), so even the correct pi_local reallocation is an MAR assumption; the uniform fallback is far worse — it manufactures indigenous and asian deaths wholesale. Race missingness in SIM is substantial (historically 10-30%+ of DO records), so this dominates the posterior for those small categories.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/measurement/race.py:263-265 (missing reallocated by local_pi), :309-310 (uniform fallback pi); MSD §II.5 missing-reallocation clause
- **recommendation:** Never reallocate missing race by a uniform prior; require the census self-declared local composition. Model race missingness as potentially non-ignorable (a selection/pattern-mixture sensitivity band) rather than MAR-uniform. Flag categories (amarela/indigena) whose posterior is dominated by missing-mass reallocation.
- **risk:** Indigenous and Asian-Brazilian mortality rates are fabricated from unknown-race mass, silently inflating those denominators-of-interest and distorting exactly the equity analyses race stratification exists to support.

**`RACE-BOOT-10` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — Bootstrap/posterior use fixed RNG seeds and only resample source-category multinomial, ignoring the dominant small-count sampling variance and giving irreproducible-looking but actually frozen intervals
- **detail:** _posterior_bridge_draws and _bootstrap_bridge_cv use hard-coded seeds (20260628 / 20260627) and resample the SOURCE composition via multinomial(observed_total, source_probs). For a municipality-year with, say, 4 pancreatic deaths, observed_total=4 and the multinomial over 5 source categories is extremely coarse — the 200 replicates explore a tiny discrete lattice, so the quantile-based CI is a step function with essentially no resolution at the counts where uncertainty matters most. Meanwhile the fixed seed makes the 'uncertainty' deterministic across runs, hiding Monte-Carlo error. The draw count RACE_BRIDGE_BOOTSTRAP_REPLICATES=200 is another unjustified magic number, too few for stable 2.5/97.5 quantiles.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/measurement/race.py:29 (200 replicates), :357 & :405 (fixed seeds), :431 (multinomial on observed_total)
- **recommendation:** For small counts use exact/analytic propagation (the posterior of a multinomial-Dirichlet count pushforward is available in closed or near-closed form) instead of a 200-draw bootstrap; report Monte-Carlo standard error; raise replicate count and vary seed with a run id while recording it.
- **risk:** Race-bridge intervals at small municipal counts are coarse step functions presented as smooth credible sets; MC error is invisible.


### she/population

**`INTERP-COHORT-11` · HIGH · msd_theoretical_fragility** — Intercensal composition is LINEAR share interpolation, not cohort-component; it ages nobody and produces demographically impossible intercensal age structures
- **detail:** layer1.interpolate_census_composition builds the intercensal (age,sex,race) joint by LINEARLY interpolating the census SHARE vector in place (_interpolate_shares: (1-w)·pi[lo]+w·pi[hi]) and scaling by the closure total. This holds each stratum's share on a straight line between censuses. That is demographically wrong: between 2010 and 2022 a 2010 cohort of 60-64-year-olds does not linearly morph into the 2022 60-64 share — it AGES into 72-76. Linear share interpolation smears the age structure and cannot represent the diagonal cohort flow the reconstruction loss's own aging term (loss.py:213-235, P_prior·survival) encodes. So the WARM START handed to the solver is off the cohort manifold, and — critically — the docstring (layer1.py:40-51) states that WHEN NO FLOW TERM IS INFORMATIVE this linear interpolation IS the reconstruction itself. For the vast majority of small municipalities with no informative migration residual, the delivered intercensal age structure is the linearly-smeared one, not a cohort-component one. The MSD's projection_envelope even claims the values 'already come from the solver's cohort-component dynamics' (projection_envelope.py:7-9) — false in the data-poor limit, where they come from linear share interpolation.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/denominators/population/build/layer1.py:13-26 (linear shares), :40-51 (docstring: 'is the reconstruction itself'); loss.py:213-235 (cohort aging term); projection_envelope.py:7-9 (claims cohort-component)
- **recommendation:** Replace linear SHARE interpolation with a cohort-component interpolation between censuses (age the earlier census forward with survival+births, age the later census backward, and blend), or always run the solver's aging term to at least a few iterations even in the data-poor limit rather than shipping the linear warm start as the answer. Reconcile the projection_envelope docstring with what actually runs.
- **risk:** Intercensal age-specific denominators are biased in a structured way (over-smoothed across cohorts), directly biasing every age-specific and standardized rate in non-census years — i.e. most of the 2000-2025 panel.

**`INTERP-GEOM-12` · MEDIUM · epidemiological_risk** — Geometric total-closure interpolation assumes constant growth between censuses and clamps composition beyond the last census, missing the epidemiological/demographic transition
- **detail:** geometric_interpolate_closure (anchor.py:143-194) holds a CONSTANT annual growth rate P(t)=Pa·(Pb/Pa)^frac between census anchors and extrapolates with the nearest inter-census rate beyond the range. Two problems for Brazil 2000-2025: (1) municipal growth is NOT constant-rate — it is punctuated by internal migration waves, economic shocks, and boundary changes; a single geometric rate across a 12-year census gap misdates population turning points, which then misdates rate trends. (2) For post-2022 years the age COMPOSITION is clamped to the last census (_interpolate_shares returns shares[last] for t>=last, layer1.py:19-20) while only the total grows geometrically — so the aging of Brazil's population (a first-order driver of cancer mortality trends) is frozen after 2022 and before 2000. Age-standardization removes level, not this frozen-composition artifact in the denominator's internal cohort flow feeding age-specific rates.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/denominators/population/anchor.py:143-194 (constant-rate); layer1.py:17-20 (composition clamp outside census bracket); projection_envelope only widens CI, does not fix the frozen composition
- **recommendation:** Beyond census anchors, project age composition with cohort-component dynamics (surviving + aging the last census) instead of clamping; within gaps, allow the closure to bend using the annual 6579 estimates as a shape constraint (already fetched as a cross-check) rather than pure geometric.
- **risk:** Denominator age structure is frozen at the census in exactly the projected years whose trends drive the study's headline temporal claims (rising C25 mortality).

**`INTERP-NEGMIG-13` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — Net-migration residual conflates registration undercount and boundary changes with true migration, and can be structurally negative/unbounded, corrupting the reconstruction
- **detail:** _migration_residual_totals (closure.py:67-105) computes NetMig = E(t)-E(t-1)-Births(t)+Deaths(t) from SIDRA/DATASUS vital totals. This residual absorbs EVERYTHING the balancing equation cannot explain: birth under-registration (SINASC coverage <100% in the North/Northeast early years), death under-registration (SIM coverage grew over 2000-2025), late registration, AND boundary changes (a municipality that gained territory shows a spurious huge 'in-migration'). The comment acknowledges absent birth/death keys are read as ZERO registered events — so a municipality-year missing from the civil-registry facts gets Births=Deaths=0 and the entire population change is attributed to migration. Feeding this contaminated residual into the loss's migration_total anchor (loss.py:301-312) pulls the reconstructed age/sex/race flows toward an artifact. The residual is only computed for consecutive years, silently leaving multi-year gaps unconstrained.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/denominators/population/build/closure.py:86-102 (residual, zero-fill semantics); loss.py:301-312 (migration_total anchor consumes it); MSD §2.8.7
- **recommendation:** Separate registration-coverage correction (a known, estimable time-varying factor for SIM/SINASC by region) from true net migration before forming the residual; bound the residual by a plausible migration rate and flag/exclude boundary-change years (link to FAL-POP-AMC) rather than letting them enter as migration; down-weight the migration anchor where birth/death coverage is low.
- **risk:** Reconstructed intercensal age/sex/race denominators inherit registration-undercount and boundary artifacts as fake migration, biasing small-area rates in the under-registered regions that are the equity focus.

**`RACE-ILR-14` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — The race-composition prior in the tensor loss is enforced in ILR coordinates with epsilon=1e-12, which is numerically explosive for structural-zero categories in small municipalities
- **detail:** The reconstruction loss's race term (loss.py:264-286) maps the 5-category race composition to ILR coordinates with epsilon=1e-12 for zeros. Many small municipalities have STRUCTURAL zeros (no amarela or no indigena residents at all in the census). ILR of a composition with a near-zero component is log(1e-12)≈-27.6, an enormous coordinate, and the gradient grad = 2·w·deriv/max(P_valid,1e-12) divides by that same tiny floor, producing gradients up to ~1e12. Even though nonnegativity is projected, the ILR residual for a structural-zero category dominates the race loss and the SPG step, so the solver spends its budget matching an essentially undefined log-ratio rather than the informative categories. ILR is the right geometry for interior compositions but is ill-posed at the simplex boundary that small-area race data lives on; there is no zero-category masking or a proper zero-replacement (e.g. Bayesian multiplicative replacement).
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/denominators/reconstruction/loss.py:93-101 (_ilr_coords eps 1e-12), :275-282 (grad divides by max(P,1e-12))
- **recommendation:** Use a Bayesian/multiplicative zero-replacement on the composition before ILR, or mask structural-zero race categories out of the ILR residual per cell (reduce to the observed sub-simplex), or penalize race composition with an aitchison distance robust to boundary; cap the per-cell race-gradient norm.
- **risk:** In the smallest municipalities (highest sampling noise, greatest reliance on the prior) the race term is dominated by boundary artifacts, degrading exactly the reconstructions that most need the prior.


### DiseaseGraph structural affinity (graph.py _structural_weight / disease_prior.variable_affinity)

**`LDO-DIS-TREE-01` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — ICD tree-distance (1/0.5/0.25) is a bureaucratic-adjacency prior, not an epidemiological-similarity prior — it couples clinically unrelated diseases and separates causally linked ones
- **detail:** The entire disease prior rests on _structural_weight: affinity=1.0 if same 3-char category, 0.5 if same block, 0.25 if same chapter, 0 otherwise. ICD-10 chapter/block boundaries are an administrative filing taxonomy assembled by body-system and historical convention, NOT a metric of pathophysiological or epidemiological relatedness. Concrete failures: (a) Dengue (A90, chapter I 'infectious') and its clinical sequela microcephaly-via-congenital-Zika (Q02, chapter XVII 'congenital') get affinity 0 — yet the Zika->microcephaly lag is THE acceptance target of this very system (SEMANTIC_AXIS §9 Appendix D). The prior actively down-weights the flagship link. (b) Diabetes (E10-E14, chapter IV endocrine) and its renal complication N18 (chapter XIV) get affinity 0 despite being the same causal chain. (c) Conversely, within chapter II 'neoplasms', pancreatic cancer C25 and benign uterine leiomyoma D25 sit in the same chapter (affinity 0.25) and are epidemiologically unrelated. The prior thus lowers the selection threshold for spurious same-chapter pairs and raises it for real cross-chapter causal chains — the exact opposite of what an informative prior should do. This is not a smoothness prior over a meaningful geometry; it is smoothness over a filing cabinet.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/disease/graph.py:36-58 (_weight_from_info/_structural_weight); src/pegasus/ldo/disease_prior.py:34-59 (variable_affinity); PEGASUS_DISEASE_SEMANTIC_AXIS.md §II.13.3
- **recommendation:** Do not treat ICD tree-distance as the affinity metric. Replace or blend with: (1) a clinically-grounded ontology distance (SNOMED-CT is-a / MedDRA / phecode hierarchy, or Disease Ontology) which encodes pathophysiology, not filing order; (2) the label-text embedding kNN graph the spec already earmarks as structural (§II.13.4) — semantic label similarity crosses chapter boundaries; (3) known causal-chain edges from an external curated KB (e.g. arbovirus->congenital-anomaly) as explicit prior edges. At minimum, document that the hierarchy prior is a WEAK filing-order prior and must not be the sole geometry, and never let it down-weight a hypothesized cross-chapter link relative to the flat penalty.
- **risk:** The disease prior degrades recovery of exactly the cross-chapter causal chains the system is built to discover (Zika->microcephaly, diabetes->CKD), while boosting spurious within-chapter co-selection. Reported edge sets are biased toward taxonomic neighbors and away from real etiology.


### Sum-of-scales GMRF (disease_prior.sum_of_scales_disease_operator / orchestrator disease_scale_precisions)

**`LDO-DIS-SCALES-02` · HIGH · implementation_gap** — The sum-of-scales per-scale GMRF is dead code at runtime: disease_scale_precisions is never populated, so only the flat single-γ Laplacian ever runs
- **detail:** orchestrator.run_ldo exposes disease_scale_precisions and, when truthy, builds G_D = Σ_level τ_level·L_level via sum_of_scales_disease_operator, baking γ_disease=1.0. But grepping the entire workflows/ path shows disease_scale_precisions is NEVER set — investigate.py threads disease_graph but no scale precisions. Therefore the branch at orchestrator.py:180 is always False in production, disease_laplacian falls back to the flat disease_laplacian_matrix (single scalar γ_disease=0.1), and the whole §III.3 'per-scale precision' apparatus (hierarchical_disease_laplacians, sum_of_scales_disease_operator) is unreachable. The spec's central claim (§III.3: 'a rare leaf is dominated by shrinkage toward its better-estimated parent; distinct τ_level per scale') is NOT realized: all scales share one γ. The docstrings assert the sum-of-scales GMRF 'is used' — it is not.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/orchestrator.py:117,180-186; src/pegasus/ldo/disease_prior.py:109-129; no matches for disease_scale_precisions under src/pegasus/workflows/
- **recommendation:** Either (a) wire an estimator for the per-scale precisions (empirical-Bayes / REML on the scale-decomposed random effects, or a light cross-validation over {τ_chapter,τ_block,τ_category}) and pass them from investigate.py, or (b) if sum-of-scales is not going to be driven, delete the dead branch and stop claiming §III.3 multiresolution shrinkage on the disease axis. Do not ship docstrings asserting a code path that never executes.
- **risk:** The subsystem advertises BYM-style multiresolution small-area shrinkage on the disease axis but delivers a flat single-scale penalty. Rare-disease borrowing-of-strength — the stated cure for count sparsity — does not happen.


### Sum-of-scales GMRF identifiability (hierarchical_disease_laplacians_from_affinity)

**`LDO-DIS-IDENT-03` · MEDIUM · identifiability_concern** — Even if wired, the per-scale precisions are confounded: nested scales are collinear and the band-splitting by affinity magnitude cannot separate their contributions
- **detail:** The sum-of-scales decomposition splits the SINGLE graded affinity matrix into three bands by magnitude (>=0.99 category, [0.4,0.99) block, [0.15,0.4) chapter) and assigns each its own τ. But these scales are strictly nested: every same-category pair is also same-block and same-chapter. In the classic sum-of-scales GMRF θ_leaf=μ_chapter+δ_block+δ_category+δ_leaf the components are INDEPENDENT nested random effects with a sum-to-zero/anchoring constraint per parent, which is what makes τ_chapter, τ_block, τ_category separately identifiable. Here there is no such nesting-with-anchoring: L_category, L_block, L_chapter are three Laplacians over DISJOINT edge sets derived post-hoc from one affinity, and G_D = Σ τ_level L_level is a linear combination that, with a single quadratic penalty tr(SᵀG_D S) and no per-scale data likelihood, only identifies the aggregate G_D, not the individual τ's. Two very different (τ_chapter,τ_block,τ_category) triples yielding the same G_D are observationally equivalent. The spec invokes INLA/BYM (§III.3) which achieves identifiability via a proper hierarchical prior with variance components; this implementation has no variance-component model, just a weighted-Laplacian sum. The τ's are confounded.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/disease_prior.py:82-129; PEGASUS_MSD_III.md §III.3 (BYM/INLA claim)
- **recommendation:** To make scale precisions identifiable, model the actual nested random-effect decomposition with per-parent anchoring (sum-to-zero within each block/chapter) and fit variance components by REML/INLA, not a post-hoc Laplacian-band sum. If keeping the Laplacian-sum form, acknowledge that only the aggregate operator is identified and collapse to a single γ (which is what runs anyway per finding LDO-DIS-SCALES-02).
- **risk:** Any future 'per-scale shrinkage' readout or tuning of τ_level is scientifically meaningless — the individual precisions are not estimable from the objective as written.


### Variable generator vs live V_fields path (variable_grammar.py, investigate.disease_variable_meta)

**`LDO-DIS-MULTICAUSE-04` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Coercing the disease axis into a σ_C count variable discards the multi-cause / comorbidity structure a proper multivariate-outcome model would retain
- **detail:** variable_grammar.py — the module that would unfold carrier × disease_selector × topology_role into a multi-label variable set honoring the SIM cause CHAIN (LINHAA-D underlying vs LINHAII mentions/associated) — is explicitly NOT wired (its own docstring, lines 22-32: 'not currently wired into the live pipeline'). The live LDO variable set is instead the EFG's per-field σ_C restriction COUNTS read from V_fields. Each such variable is a scalar count of events whose (usually underlying-cause) ICD falls in a code set. This throws away the multi-cause record structure that SIM/SIH actually carry: a single death has one underlying cause plus multiple contributing/associated causes (multi-cause mortality), and a hospitalization carries a principal plus secondary diagnoses (comorbidity). A death from pancreatic-cancer-with-diabetes-and-sepsis becomes a count in ONE variable, and the co-mention structure (the actual comorbidity signal) is destroyed at ingestion — it can never appear as an edge because it was never a variable. The spec itself frames the correct object as a multivariate outcome (§5.1 topology_role as variable identity; §II.6 multi-label), but the wired path is univariate counts. A proper multi-cause model (multivariate Poisson/log-Gaussian Cox with shared frailty across cause-mentions, or a co-mention incidence tensor) would keep this; the count coercion cannot.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/disease/variable_grammar.py:22-32 (wiring status), :52-64 (DiseaseVariable with topology_role) — UNWIRED; src/pegasus/workflows/investigate.py:80-113 (disease_variable_meta reads σ_C counts from V_fields, single code_set per variable); PEGASUS_DISEASE_SEMANTIC_AXIS.md §5.1
- **recommendation:** Build the co-mention/comorbidity tensor as a first-class carrier: for SIM use the full cause chain (underlying + all LINHAII mentions), for SIH the principal + secondary diagnoses, and instantiate variables at topology_role granularity so 'C25 as underlying' and 'C25 as mention' are distinct, and pairwise co-mention counts become edges. Wire variable_grammar (with the V_fields pinning test it asks for) or state clearly that comorbidity discovery is out of scope for the current release. Do not claim comorbidity structure while the wired path is univariate σ_C counts.
- **risk:** Comorbidity and multi-cause epidemiology — a headline capability of the disease axis — is silently absent. The engine cannot discover comorbidity links because the co-mention data was collapsed to per-cause counts before it reached the LDO.


### Live graph construction (graph.from_variable_code_sets)

**`LDO-DIS-MAXWEIGHT-05` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — Variable-to-variable affinity is the MAX (strongest) closeness over the code cross-product, inflating coupling between broad multi-code variables
- **detail:** The live graph (from_variable_code_sets, used by investigate._disease_graph_from_meta) sets the edge weight between two σ_C variables to max over all (code_a in set_A, code_b in set_B) of _weight_from_info. A single incidental shared category anywhere in two otherwise-broad code sets pins the affinity to 1.0. Example: a broad 'external causes' variable (V01-Y98 span) and a broad 'injury' variable (S00-T98) — if any pair of their member codes lands in the same 3-char category, the whole variable pair is treated as maximally related and gets the minimum ℓ1 penalty (λ1·(1-β)=0.3·λ1), regardless of how dissimilar the bulk of their codes are. Max is the least robust aggregator over sets; a mean-Jaccard-weighted or overlap-adjusted affinity would be far more faithful. This systematically over-couples broad variables and hands them a discovery advantage (lower selection threshold) they did not earn.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/disease/graph.py:137 (w = max(...) over cross-product); src/pegasus/ldo/disease_prior.py:132-142 (penalty = λ1·(1−β·affinity))
- **recommendation:** Replace max with a set-aware aggregate: e.g. the code-set-weighted mean structural closeness, or the Jaccard-on-membership at the coarsest common scale, so broad variables sharing one incidental category are not pinned to affinity 1. Also separate the mechanical-overlap notion (Jaccard on codes, already computed in edges.py) from the structural-affinity notion so they are not conflated.
- **risk:** Broad-code variables get spurious low penalties and preferential edge selection; the disease prior's coupling structure is dominated by outlier code pairs rather than the typical relatedness of the variables.


### Penalty mapping (disease_prior.penalty_from_affinity)

**`LDO-DIS-MAGIC-06` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — β=0.7, min_frac=0.2, γ_disease=0.1 and the affinity tiers 1/0.5/0.25 are unjustified magic numbers with no calibration or sensitivity analysis
- **detail:** penalty_from_affinity(affinity; λ1, β=0.7, min_frac=0.2) maps affinity to penalty λ1·(1−β·affinity) floored at 0.2·λ1. So a same-category pair's ℓ1 penalty is cut to 30% of baseline — a large, arbitrary thumb on the selection scale. β=0.7 and min_frac=0.2 have no derivation; nor do the affinity tiers (why 0.5 for block and 0.25 for chapter and not, say, exponential-in-tree-depth?); nor does the default γ_disease=0.1 for the quadratic. In an ℓ1-selection problem the per-edge penalty directly controls which edges survive, so these constants materially determine the reported edge set. There is no cross-validation, no stability-vs-β curve, no prior-sensitivity report. A reviewer cannot distinguish a 'discovered' edge from an artifact of β=0.7. This violates the spec's own anti-magic-number ethos and the stability-selection philosophy (§III.7), which is undermined if the penalty landscape is itself an untuned guess.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/disease_prior.py:132-142 (β=0.7, min_frac=0.2 defaults); orchestrator.py:118-119 (γ_disease=0.1, γ_temporal=0.1)
- **recommendation:** Calibrate β/min_frac/γ by the same stability-selection lattice-subsample machinery already in the LDO (choose the penalty scaling that maximizes selection stability / matches a target FDR on synthetic planted edges), and emit a prior-sensitivity panel (edge set vs β, vs γ_disease) with every run. Prefer an affinity→penalty map derived from the prior odds of an edge, not an ad-hoc linear discount.
- **risk:** Reported edges are contingent on undocumented constants; results are not reproducible-in-spirit and not defensible under peer review.


### Margin / count model for disease-stratified variables (variable_grammar.assemble_disease_field, LDO copula §III.5)

**`LDO-DIS-SPARSE-07` · HIGH · statistical_validity_risk** — Cause-specific municipal monthly counts are sparse, overdispersed, zero-inflated and rank-degenerate; a rank/PIT-to-Gaussian copula margin on such counts is invalid for rare diseases
- **detail:** Brazilian municipal cause-specific counts (e.g. C25 pancreatic-cancer deaths in a small município in a month) are overwhelmingly 0, occasionally 1. The LDO §III.5 transforms each variable to a Gaussian scale by rank/PIT. For a vector that is 99% zeros, the empirical CDF is a step function with one huge tie at 0; PIT maps the entire zero mass to a single value (or a randomized-tie smear), producing a degenerate near-constant transformed variable whose sample correlations with other variables are dominated by the tie-breaking, not signal. Partial correlations estimated from such margins are unstable and the precision estimate for rare variables is essentially noise. The spec asserts NB/beta-binomial GLM families as the per-variable transforms (§III.5) but a rank-PIT copula does not implement an NB likelihood — it is a nonparametric marginal transform that collapses under extreme zero-inflation. Small-area count epidemiology specifically requires a count likelihood with exposure offset + spatial smoothing (the BYM/INLA the spec cites), not a Gaussian-copula-on-ranks of raw counts.
- **evidence:** PEGASUS_MSD_III.md §III.5 (copula margins, NB claim); src/pegasus/disease/variable_grammar.py:169-209 (assemble_disease_field emits raw counts X, weight 1/0); LDO copula transform in the fit path
- **recommendation:** For sparse count variables use an actual count-with-exposure margin: negative-binomial (not Poisson — enforce the overdispersion the spec names) with a population/exposure offset, and estimate dependence via a latent-Gaussian count model (copula-Gaussian on the LATENT NB link, i.e. a Gaussian copula whose margins are NB CDFs, or a multivariate log-Gaussian Cox / GLLVM), plus BYM spatial smoothing so rare-cell estimates borrow strength. Gate rank-PIT off for variables below a nonzero-fraction threshold and route them to the count path. Verify the fit code actually applies NB, not just ranks.
- **risk:** Every rare-disease edge (which is most of the interesting epidemiology — cancers, congenital anomalies, arboviral sequelae) is estimated from a degenerate margin; partial correlations for these are artifacts of tie-handling, invalidating the scientific claims about rare-disease links.


### CID-10 chapter/block resolution (icd_adapter._CID10_ABSENT_BLOCKS, cid10_block)

**`LDO-DIS-CHAPTER-08` · MEDIUM · epidemiological_risk** — The declared WHO-gap table collapses the arbovirus block so dengue(A90), chikungunya(A92) and Zika(U06) are only same-CHAPTER, not same-block — under-coupling Brazil's highest-burden disease cluster in the very prior meant to link them
- **detail:** icd_adapter documents that WHO-2019 (backing simple-icd-10) dropped A90/A91 into A97 and retired U06, returning block=None for these codes. The gap table _CID10_ABSENT_BLOCKS restores A90/A91 to a synthetic 'A90-A99' block and U06 to 'U00-U49'. But the code's own comment (lines 57-59) concedes that WHO labels the arbovirus block 'A92-A99', so a dengue(A90)↔chikungunya(A92) pair resolves to same-CHAPTER (0.25) not same-block (0.5), and Zika(U06) sits in a completely different synthetic block (U00-U49) from dengue — affinity 0 between Zika and dengue despite their being co-circulating, co-transmitted (Aedes aegypti) arboviruses that are THE canonical Brazilian epidemiological cluster. So the structural prior that is supposed to help the engine link arboviruses actively fails to couple them. This compounds LDO-DIS-TREE-01 specifically at the highest-priority disease cluster.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/disease/icd_adapter.py:50-70 (_CID10_ABSENT_BLOCKS, comment on A92-A99 mismatch); the Zika/dengue affinity-0 consequence
- **recommendation:** Add an explicit curated 'brazilian_arbovirus' concept group (A90,A91,A92,A97,U06,P35.4 congenital-Zika, etc.) with a positive prior edge weight, injected into the DiseaseGraph as a structural concept-membership edge (the ccsr_membership mechanism the spec provides), rather than relying on the accidental WHO block layout. Arbovirus co-membership should give these a strong prior coupling regardless of chapter.
- **risk:** The arbovirus cluster — the domain the acceptance test targets — is the most under-served by the disease prior; Zika–dengue–chikungunya links get no prior help and may be missed at national sparsity.


### Custom concept range matching (concept_registry._in_ranges)

**`LDO-DIS-RANGE-09` · MEDIUM · implementation_gap** — Custom-concept range membership uses lexical string comparison that mis-handles cross-letter and zero-padding boundaries, silently mis-assigning ICSAP concepts
- **detail:** _in_ranges tests membership by lo[:1]==cat[:1] and lo<=cat<=hi with plain uppercase string comparison. Two problems: (1) It requires the FIRST LETTER of the range's low bound to equal the category's first letter (lo[:1]==cat[:1]). Ranges that legitimately span two letters (e.g. the ICSAP nutritional-deficiency style E40-E64, or any A00-B99-type chapter span) will silently fail for the second-letter portion — a code like B02 is excluded from an 'A00-B99' style range because 'A'!='B'. Several ICSAP groups in the registry are within one letter so happen to work, but the guard is a latent correctness trap for any multi-letter range and gives NO warning. (2) Lexical <= on 3-char strings is only correct because ICD categories are fixed-width Ldd; if a 2-char or malformed category slips through (cat[:3] of a short code), the comparison is silently wrong. Membership is a load-bearing correctness operation (it defines which deaths count toward an avoidable-hospitalization concept), and a silent mis-assignment violates the anti-silent-fiction contract.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/disease/concept_registry.py:99-108 (_in_ranges lo[:1]==cat[:1] gate + lexical <=); registry ranges include multi-letter spans conceptually (chapter I is A00-B99)
- **recommendation:** Compare on the (letter-ordinal, numeric) tuple, not raw strings, and support ranges whose endpoints differ in letter (iterate letters A..Z between lo-letter and hi-letter). Add a validation pass that asserts every range endpoint is a well-formed 3-char category and warns on any code that matches zero concepts unexpectedly. Unit-test a B-code against an A00-B99 range.
- **risk:** ICSAP/avoidable-hospitalization concept counts (a heavily-used Brazilian primary-care-quality indicator) are silently wrong for any multi-letter range, corrupting both the variable definitions and any concept-level edge.


### Mechanical-overlap guard (edges.type_mechanical_overlap, investigate variable_meta code_set)

**`LDO-DIS-OVERLAP-10` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — The mechanical-overlap guard depends on code sets that are only present for σ_C variables with an ENUMERATED starts_with_any predicate; range/regex-defined restrictions yield code_set=None and silently bypass the overlap check
- **detail:** _icd_code_set only extracts a code set when the σ_C predicate op is in _ICD_ENUM_OPS (starts_with_any etc.) and the column name contains 'icd'/'cid'; the enumerated value list IS taken as the code set. Variables restricted by a RANGE predicate, a regex, or an operator outside that whitelist get code_set=None. In edges.type_mechanical_overlap, any edge where either endpoint's code set is None is passed through UN-retyped (line 265: if cs_s is None or cs_t is None: append(r); continue). So two heavily-overlapping concept variables that happen to be defined by ranges rather than explicit code lists escape the §5.3 mechanical-overlap guard entirely and can be reported as genuine contemporaneous/latent_shared epidemiological edges — precisely the shared-denominator/shared-count trap the guard exists to prevent. The guard's coverage is silently partial and depends on an incidental predicate-encoding detail, with no abort when a code set is missing (contrast the spec §5.3 which mandates a §10 abort when overlap is unaccounted).
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/workflows/investigate.py:54-77 (_icd_code_set only for enum ops); src/pegasus/ldo/edges.py:262-267 (None code set => pass-through, no guard); PEGASUS_DISEASE_SEMANTIC_AXIS.md §5.3 (mandatory abort)
- **recommendation:** Materialize the actual code set for EVERY disease variable by expanding its σ_C predicate (range/regex/enum) against the ICD catalog into a concrete frozenset, so overlap Jaccard is always computable. If a variable's code set genuinely cannot be resolved, FIRE the §5.3 abort (or force the edge to descriptive), never silently pass it through as a discovery.
- **risk:** Mechanically-overlapping range-defined variables produce fake epidemiological edges; the flagship anti-artifact guard has silent holes tied to how a field's restriction happened to be written.


### CCSR-on-CID-10 provenance (concept_registry _GROUPERS, edges.annotate_disease_provenance)

**`LDO-DIS-PROV-11` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — CCSR/CCIR groupers are ICD-10-CM (US) applied to Brazilian CID-10, returning only the PRIMARY category, so multi-label concept variables are inconsistent, incomplete, and mis-projected — yet enter as 'approximate' rather than being rejected
- **detail:** Three compounding provenance problems: (1) icd-mappings@0.6.2 CCSR returns only the PRIMARY category, not the AHRQ 1-to-6 multi-label set (registry comment 'ccsr_primary_category_only'). So the multi_label=True concept family is in fact single-label at runtime — the spec's headline 'disease semantics are not a partition; CCSR lets a code sit in several categories' (§1) is NOT delivered; the code silently returns a partition it claims is multi-label. (2) CCSR is defined on ICD-10-CM (a US extension with ~5x the codes of WHO ICD-10) and applied to CID-10; many CID-10 codes have no CM analog or map to a different CM category, so the concept assignment is not just 'approximate' but potentially WRONG (a US-specific clinical grouping imposed on a Brazilian code). The system tags projection_status=approximate and forces state<=fragile, which is honest, but a wrong grouping degraded to 'fragile' is still a fabricated variable definition, not merely a low-confidence one. (3) Two variables built from the same CCSR family can have inconsistent membership because the primary-category-only reduction is order/version dependent. The spec's own §II.13.1 contract ('multi-label MUST NOT be silently forced to one') is violated by the primary-only reduction.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/disease/concept_registry.py:55-63 (_GROUPERS ccsr multi:True but warn ccsr_primary_category_only), :160-178 (single category emission); PEGASUS_DISEASE_SEMANTIC_AXIS.md §1 (multi-label claim), §II.13.1 (no-silent-partition contract)
- **recommendation:** Either import the FULL AHRQ CCSR crosswalk (all 1-6 categories per code, from the AHRQ source files, not icd-mappings' primary-only) to genuinely deliver multi-label, or drop CCSR as a variable generator for CID-10 and rely on WHO-exact structural concepts + curated Brazilian lists only. For any grouper applied cross-system, validate the mapping coverage on the actual CID-10 code inventory and report the fraction unmappable; do not present a single-label primary-only reduction as multi-label.
- **risk:** CCSR-based disease variables are internally inconsistent and cross-system-mis-projected; edges among them inherit a false variable identity, and the 'multi-label' selling point is not actually implemented.


### Extensive/intensive routing for disease counts (variable_grammar counts, §III.5 exposure)

**`LDO-DIS-DIAG-DENOM-12` · HIGH · epidemiological_risk** — Disease-stratified counts enter without a population/exposure denominator, so the disease prior smooths and links RAW counts dominated by population size and the epidemiological transition, not risk
- **detail:** assemble_disease_field emits raw event counts per (variable, município, month). Absent a population offset, a município's C25 count is dominated by its population and age structure, not its cancer RISK. Two consequences for the disease axis specifically: (a) The disease Laplacian smoothing tr(SᵀL_D S) then smooths raw-count dependency rows across 'related' diseases — but two diseases will co-vary across space simply because big municipalities have more of everything, a pure population confound the disease prior will happily reinforce into a spurious 'link'. (b) Brazil is mid-epidemiological-transition: infectious-disease counts fall and chronic-disease counts rise over 2000-2025 secularly and heterogeneously by region; raw-count time series therefore share a strong common trend that the low-rank layer may absorb as a shared factor OR the sparse layer may read as cross-disease edges, neither reflecting etiology. The spec names the denominator principle (I.2, §III.5 count-with-exposure) but the disease-axis assembly path emits bare counts with weight 1/0 and no exposure. Without age-standardization and population offset, the disease-axis edges conflate demography with epidemiology.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/disease/variable_grammar.py:190-209 (X=raw counts, W=1/0, no offset); PEGASUS_MSD_III.md §III.5 (count-with-exposure), I.2 (denominator principle)
- **recommendation:** Route every disease count through the count-with-exposure margin with the population tensor as offset (the system already builds a two-layer population tensor), and age-standardize (direct standardization to a reference population) before or within the margin so cross-region/cross-time comparability holds through the epidemiological transition. Detrend or explicitly model the secular transition as a named temporal factor so it is not misread as cross-disease edges. Confirm the disease-axis assembly actually attaches the exposure sidecar the LDO's §III.5 path expects.
- **risk:** Disease-axis links are dominated by population size, age structure, and the secular epidemiological transition; the engine reports demographic co-variation as epidemiological discovery — a first-order validity failure for national municipal data.


### Spatial support of disease variables across boundary changes (assemble_disease_field space_ids)

**`LDO-DIS-BOUNDARY-13` · MEDIUM · epidemiological_risk** — Disease counts are keyed to municipality_cod6 with no boundary-change harmonization, so new/split municipalities create discontinuities the disease prior and spatial whitening misread
- **detail:** assemble_disease_field builds space_ids from the raw municipality_cod6 in the counts. Brazil created/merged municipalities across 2000-2025 (5507->5570), and IBGE codes change on splits. A município that appears mid-panel has structural zeros before its creation that are indistinguishable, in the raw-count field, from true epidemiological zeros; a split reassigns counts between codes at a step. The disease-axis L_D prior and the spatial GMRF whitening both operate on this space index; a spurious level-shift at a boundary change enters the covariance and can manifest as a spatial or temporal edge. The population subsystem has AMC (área mínima comparável) harmonization (FAL-POP-AMC in memory) but there is no evidence the disease-axis event counts are mapped onto the same harmonized geography before assembly — they use raw cod6.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/disease/variable_grammar.py:184-209 (space_ids from raw geo_col, no AMC mapping); memory note demographic AMC harmonization exists for population but disease counts key on municipality_cod6
- **recommendation:** Harmonize disease event counts onto the same minimum-comparable-area geography (AMC) used for the population tensor before assembling the LDO field, so the space index is temporally consistent; distinguish 'not-yet-existing' cells (zero weight) from 'true zero' (weight 1, value 0). Verify the disease field's space_ids equal the population tensor's harmonized codes.
- **risk:** Boundary-change discontinuities enter the disease covariance as artifactual edges/level-shifts, and cell-level shrinkage borrows across geographies that did not co-exist.


### Prior/estimator interaction with stability selection (§III.7, disease penalty)

**`LDO-DIS-STAB-14` · MEDIUM · statistical_validity_risk** — The disease prior lowers ℓ1 penalties for 'related' pairs, biasing stability-selection frequencies upward for those pairs — the multiplicity control is no longer calibrated once a structured penalty is applied
- **detail:** Multiplicity is controlled by stability selection (§III.7): refit on subsamples, keep edges whose selection frequency exceeds a threshold. Stability-selection error control (Meinshausen-Buhmann) assumes an exchangeable penalty across candidate edges; its finite-sample false-discovery bound is derived under a uniform λ. The disease prior deliberately breaks exchangeability — same-category pairs get penalty 0.3·λ1, others λ1 — so structurally-related pairs are selected more often at every subsample, inflating their stability frequency independent of data support. A same-category pair can cross the stability threshold on the strength of the prior discount alone. The system thus double-counts the prior: once in the penalty, again in the multiplicity gate, and the stability threshold no longer corresponds to any calibrated error rate. There is no per-pair threshold adjustment for the heterogeneous penalty.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/disease_prior.py:132-142 (per-pair penalty discount); PEGASUS_MSD_III.md §III.7 (stability selection as the multiplicity control); edges.stability_select
- **recommendation:** Either (a) apply the disease prior ONLY as the quadratic smoothness tr(SᵀL_D S) and keep the ℓ1 selection penalty uniform, so stability selection stays calibrated and the prior shrinks magnitudes without biasing the selection gate; or (b) re-derive per-pair stability thresholds that account for the heterogeneous penalty (higher threshold for discounted pairs). Report selection frequencies under both prior-on and prior-off (a placebo prior) so a reviewer can see the prior's contribution to each edge's stability.
- **risk:** Related-disease edges pass the multiplicity gate partly because the prior discounted their penalty, not because the data support them; the FDR-control claim of stability selection is void for exactly the edges the disease prior touches.


### Smoothness operator scale (lowrank.build_smoothness_operator Kron; disease_prior dense affinity)

**`LDO-DIS-QUAD-DENSE-15` · MEDIUM · computational_trap** — The disease smoothness path densifies: variable_affinity/L_D are dense p×p numpy and the smoothness operator is a dense Kron over p·(K+1), contradicting the spec's sparse-L_D scaling claim and blocking national p
- **detail:** The spec (§7, §III.4 sparsity) promises L_D is sparse (hierarchy: ~few neighbors/node) so 'the disease prior adds negligible VRAM, exactly like the sparse spatial L_W'. But the LDO-side implementation is dense: disease_prior.variable_affinity allocates np.zeros((p,p)) and disease_laplacian_matrix returns a dense p×p; build_smoothness_operator forms np.kron(I_{K+1}, L_D) as a DENSE (p·(K+1))×(p·(K+1)) matrix and computes eigvalsh(G_sym) (an O((pK)^3) dense eigendecomposition) for the Lipschitz constant every fit. graph.py keeps weights sparse, but the moment it reaches the estimator it is densified. At national disease resolution (p in the hundreds-to-thousands × K+1 lags) this dense Kron and its eigendecomposition is exactly the O(p^3)/dense-storage blow-up the spec forbids. The sparse-L_D scaling story is not realized on the estimator side.
- **evidence:** src/pegasus/ldo/disease_prior.py:42 (np.zeros((p,p))), :73 (dense np.diag); src/pegasus/ldo/lowrank.py:337-341 (dense np.kron), :196 (np.linalg.eigvalsh(G_sym)); PEGASUS_DISEASE_SEMANTIC_AXIS.md §7 sparse claim
- **recommendation:** Keep L_D and the smoothness operator sparse (scipy.sparse Kron), apply G·S as a sparse matvec in the prox-gradient step, and bound the Lipschitz constant by a sparse power-iteration on G (or a Gershgorin bound) instead of a dense eigvalsh. This preserves the spec's negligible-cost claim at national p.
- **risk:** Turning on the disease quadratic prior at national scale triggers dense p·(K+1) storage and an O((pK)^3) eigendecomposition per fit, either OOM-ing or forcing the prior off — so the disease smoothing the spec centers on is unusable at the scale it targets.
